# Changelog

All notable changes to Construct are documented here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and the project follows [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- Skill effectiveness pipeline, phase 1 — a local per-skill quality signal (capture only, no routing changes). When a specialist outcome carries a `sessionId` (`lib/outcomes/record.mjs` gains an optional `payload.sessionId`; the agent-tracker hook passes the hook input's `session_id`, and MCP `outcomes_record` accepts `session_id`), new `lib/telemetry/skill-outcomes.mjs` joins it against the `sessionId` column of `~/.cx/skill-calls.jsonl` and appends one attributed line per distinct skill loaded in that session to `~/.cx/skill-outcomes.jsonl` (user scope, `projectId`-tagged, rotated via a new `skill-outcomes` channel in `lib/logging/rotate.mjs`, capped/overridable with `CONSTRUCT_SKILL_OUTCOMES_MAX_MB`). New `lib/telemetry/skill-outcomes-aggregate.mjs` mirrors `lib/outcomes/aggregate.mjs` at the skill level: per-skill lifetime + 30-day success rates and a trend delta, written idempotently to `~/.cx/skill-outcomes-summary.json`. New `construct skills quality` renders the rollup — the local JSONL twin of the Postgres-only `skills correlate-quality`. Attribution is best-effort and off the critical path (all IO failures swallowed, never affects the role outcome write) and honors `CONSTRUCT_SKILL_TELEMETRY=off`. Tests: `tests/functional/skill-quality-signal.functional.test.mjs` (real agent-tracker hook in an isolated `CONSTRUCT_DOCTOR_ROOT`: attribution with per-skill dedup and cross-session exclusion, CLI rendering + summary JSON, telemetry-off suppression, unknown-session no-op). Docs: `docs/guides/concepts/learning-loops.mdx` gains an "A3-skills" section.

## [1.5.4] - 2026-07-11

Promotes `1.5.4-alpha.2` to stable (`latest`), plus one release-blocking fix found while testing the alpha on a real consumer project, plus the learning-loop self-improvement gap closure landed after the alpha was cut. See `[1.5.4-alpha.2]` and `[1.5.4-alpha.1]` below for the full itemized ADR-0069 consolidation and CI-hardening history already carried by this version.

### Added

- `construct-4z0qq` (ADR-0076, outcome-aware recruitment tie-breaker): `outcomeBoost` (`lib/outcomes/aggregate.mjs`) — a per-role, capped ±0.05 nudge derived from each role's rolling 30-day success rate — had zero production callers since it was built. The recruiter (`lib/orchestration/recruiter.mjs`) now consults it: `specialistsMatchingPatterns`' sort becomes declared-skill count ascending, then outcome boost descending, then alphabetical, so the boost only ever separates candidates the specialization signal already ranked equal — it can never promote a broader generalist over a narrower specialist. `lib/intake/classify.mjs` was deliberately never wired to this signal (its header requires classification to stay a pure, deterministic function of file content); the recruiter has no such constraint, since a participant pick is scoped to one orchestration run and is expected to reflect current state. Gated by the new `orchestration.outcomeRouting` config field (`'on'`/`'off'`, default `'on'`) in `construct.config.json` — a persistent, git-tracked setting per the repo's no-env-skip-vars rule, not an environment variable. `lib/hooks/agent-tracker.mjs`'s A3 outcome-capture block now refreshes `.construct/outcomes/_summary.json` after each recorded outcome so the boost reflects the current session rather than only whatever a previous session last aggregated; the refresh is keyed on the just-recorded role (not just the file's mtime) so several distinct roles dispatching close together each still get their first rebuild, and debounces same-role repeats within a 2-second window (`aggregateOutcomes` always rebuilds every role from the full JSONL source of truth regardless of which role triggered it — measured ~85ms at the 20-role/10k-line rotation ceiling, so the debounce only needs to guard against truly-adjacent duplicate fires, not smooth over a heavy recompute; `recordOutcome` itself is never debounced). `lib/registry/org-api.mjs#previewParticipation` now threads its `rootDir` through as the recruiter's `cwd` so preview and real dispatch never disagree on which project's outcome history applies. New `tests/functional/outcome-routing.functional.test.mjs` spawns the real `agent-tracker.mjs` binary to prove demotion and recovery through the real hook, not just an in-process call; `tests/recruiter.test.mjs` gains four unit cases (tie-break, specialization-cannot-be-inverted, `outcomeRouting=off`, missing-summary fallback).
- `construct-bh8h4` (Oracle tool-discoverability gap): tool-name-miss and tool-failure telemetry (`lib/mcp/tool-recovery.mjs`) already had a reader (`summarizeToolNameMisses`/`summarizeToolFailures`, surfaced by `npm run learning:status`) but the aggregation never reached the Oracle gap pipeline. `lib/oracle/read-model.mjs`'s `collectReadModel` now attaches `toolDiscoverability`; `synthesizeVerdict` (`lib/oracle/synthesize.mjs`) raises a `tool-discoverability` gap once unrecovered misses reach 5, naming the most-missed tool names. The gap is verdict-only (added to `lib/oracle/policy.mjs`'s `VERDICT_ONLY_GAP_IDS`) — it surfaces in `construct doctor`, session prelude, and `construct oracle gaps`, but can never auto-raise a bead even if forced to high severity, so the ADR-0043 bounded-auto envelope is untouched. Confirmed live on this repo: `construct oracle review` surfaced `[low] tool-discoverability: 36 unrecovered tool-name miss(es)` in the gaps list while correctly omitting it from the queued-for-approval actions. `tests/functional/learning-loop-capture.functional.test.mjs` gains three cases covering the read-model attachment, threshold-crossing gap emission, and below-threshold silence.
- `construct-8xjr3` (ADR-0077, proposed — design only, no behavior change): drafted the opt-in auto-apply tier for `construct optimize` — `orchestration.promptOptimization.autoApply: 'off'|'canary'|'full'` (default `'off'`), scoped to `skills/roles/*.md` only, canary-monitored against the existing quality-score signal with auto-rollback via the existing `.bak` history. While drafting, found `lib/improvement/` (`controller.mjs` + `specialist-loop.mjs`, construct-6zga.1.5/.1.7) — a separate, stricter, already-shipped governance system for the same broad domain (five target types including routing rules and contracts, per-proposal human approver required, no config-toggle standing consent). The ADR deliberately does not route through it: the narrower automation this proposes never touches that system's larger blast radius, so it does not weaken its guarantees. Status is `proposed`, not `accepted` — implementation is gated on explicit review and acceptance of the policy change (specialists mutating without a per-action human click, even within these guardrails, is a real autonomy-boundary decision that the operator should make deliberately, not one an agent session self-accepts).

### Changed

- `construct-lqxda` (prompt-optimizer docs realigned to the implementation): the optimizer docs described machinery that does not exist — a Python 3.12/DSPy auto-install, a 20-trace minimum (the implemented `--min-traces` default is 3), a staging-marker/promotion workflow with `.cx/decisions/prompt-staging-*` files and `promptVersion` span tags, `specialists/org/specialists/<agent>.json` as the patch target (the implementation patches `skills/roles/<role>.md`; org manifests are protected structural files), a `~/.cx/performance-reviews/patches/` output dir, a `promptHistory[]` registry field, and an `lib/evaluator-optimizer.mjs` attribution (that module is the document-authoring rubric loop, unrelated to prompts). `skills/ai/prompt-optimizer.md`, `commands/work/optimize-prompts.md`, `docs/guides/concepts/learning-loops.mdx` (A4, plus the A3 claim that intake classification consumes `outcomeBoost` — it deliberately never does; consumption is tracked in `construct-4z0qq`), `docs/operations/runbooks/day-2-operations.md`, `docs/guides/cookbook/observability-and-cost.md`, and the `skills/routing.md` trigger row now describe the real loop: dry-run by default → human-reviewed `--apply` (1/agent/7d rate limit, `.bak` backup cap 5, history at `~/.cx/prompt-history/<agent>.jsonl`, integrity check, auto-sync) → monitor via `construct review` → `--rollback`. New `tests/docs-optimize-consistency.test.mjs` pins the docs to `scripts/optimize.mjs` ground truth (patch target, no ghost-machinery claims, dry-run default, real minimums, no bare `__dirname`).

### Fixed

- `construct-sqtg0` (consumer upgrade was a silent no-op — **release-blocking**): `npm install -g @geraldmaron/construct@<newer>` followed by `construct init`/`sync` in an existing project did not actually upgrade it. `lib/install/stage-project.mjs#ensureProjectLauncher` only wrote `.construct/launcher/version` `if (!existsSync(versionPath))` — on a re-stage the file already existed, so the pin never moved past whatever version first staged the project. The launcher's resolver (`templates/distribution/run.mjs`) deliberately prefers a pinned `npx -p @geraldmaron/construct@<version>` over the global install for reproducibility — a well-reasoned design once the pin is correct, but with a stale pin it meant every hook kept running the old version indefinitely, silently, with no error: `construct init` reported success throughout. Found live-testing `1.5.4-alpha.2` on a real consumer project, and fixed before promoting to stable — without this, every existing 1.5.x consumer's upgrade to `1.5.4` would have silently no-op'd. Fixed by always overwriting the pin with the currently-running package's version on every stage — an upgrade is exactly what a re-stage should propagate. The launcher's resolution order itself (pinned-npx before global) is intentional and unchanged; once the pin tracks reality, npx resolving it is the correct, reproducible behavior. New regression test in `tests/distribution-bootstrap.test.mjs` stages a project at one version, re-stages at a newer one, and asserts the pin moves.
- `construct-lqxda` (optimize `--apply` validation gate): `scripts/optimize.mjs` spawned `lib/hooks/persona-validator.mjs` after applying a prompt patch — but that hook was deleted in the hooks audit (`b3277fca`, "remove orphans") and the spawn path was built with a bare `__dirname`, which throws `ReferenceError` in an ESM module and was swallowed by the surrounding try/catch. Net effect: **no validation ever ran on `--apply`**, masked as a "Validation trigger failed" warning. The ghost spawn is replaced with the validation that actually exists: a deterministic integrity check of the patched skill file (non-empty, still a markdown document, warns with a `--rollback` pointer on failure) ahead of the existing auto-`construct sync`, whose prompt composition is the real contract gate and fails loudly on a skill that does not compose. `scripts/test-closed-loop.mjs` — an unwired manual script whose purpose was exercising the deleted validator — is removed.

## [1.5.4-alpha.2] - 2026-07-10

Re-cut of the `alpha.1` pre-release (which never published — the release gate correctly blocked it on the doc-io certification failure). Adds the full `construct-gx24p` doc-io cert fix (below) plus a version-regex fix so pre-release tags pass `release:check`. Off `staging`; not promoted to `main`/`latest`.

### Fixed

- `construct-gx24p` (RichDocument doc-io certification red on the full matrix / `release:check`): two independent failures that blocked every release, since `release:check` runs the full suite in one browserless job. (1) **mermaid needs a browser, not just `mmdc`.** `detectRenderer` (`lib/render-pipeline.mjs`) only checked the `mmdc` binary, so a box with the doc engines but no Chrome (a lean CI leg, `release:check`) entered `--certified` mode and hard-failed when mmdc couldn't launch Puppeteer. Mermaid now declares `needsBrowser`, so detection reports it unavailable without a resolvable browser and the graceful matrix skips it cleanly; the release-gate cert gates `--certified` on `detectRenderer('mermaid').available` so a browserless run degrades instead of failing; and the mmdc invocation now pins the resolved browser via `PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH` and launches with `--no-sandbox --disable-setuid-sandbox` (required on Ubuntu runners) so it works where a browser *is* present. (2) **A remote image URL was resolved as a local path.** `resolveDocAssets` left `http(s)` figure refs untouched, so an engine spawned in a temp dir saw `https://example.com/x.png` as a relative path and failed opaquely (`file not found (…/templates/distribution/https:/example.com/x.png)`), non-deterministically across environments. Remote images are now fetched to a local temp file up front (in-process, sync, `fetchRemote` opt-in on the engine-export path only — the fragment/markdown/copy formats keep the portable ref); an unreachable one yields an actionable diagnostic naming the URL, with a missing export engine keeping precedence (an empty-`PATH` environment still gets its "install …" message). (3) **typst couldn't read a local image outside its `--root`.** For branded PDFs pandoc spawns with cwd = the template dir (`templates/distribution`) and defaults typst's `--root` there, so an absolute image path in a temp export dir resolved *relative to root* and failed (`file not found (searched at …/templates/distribution/tmp/…/photo.png)`) — deterministically on Linux, silently fine on macOS. typst PDF exports now pass `--pdf-engine-opt --root=<filesystem root>` so absolute local asset paths resolve regardless of spawn cwd. (4) **The pptx/odp deck export hard-failed on an unrenderable diagram.** `deck-export-pptx` renders diagram fences (mermaid via mmdc, d2) and fails the whole export when `0/N` render — but a mermaid diagram on a browserless box is *unavailable*, not a render regression. `addDiagramImage` now checks `detectRenderer(lang)` first and, when the renderer is unavailable, skips the diagram with a `[… omitted — renderer unavailable]` placeholder counted separately from real render failures, so the deck still exports; the `0/N` hard-fail is reserved for a renderer that is present but errored.

## [1.5.4-alpha.1] - 2026-07-10

Pre-release for tester validation off `staging`. Lands the ADR-0069 single-`.construct/` layout consolidation and the full-matrix CI hardening (launcher self-repo tests, OpenCode memory-bridge URL determinism, docs-site native-binary lockfile pins). Known-red on the full matrix: the RichDocument doc-io certification job (mermaid needs Chrome in CI + a remote-image diagnostic — `construct-gx24p`); the shipped package itself is unaffected. Not promoted to `main`/`latest`.

### Changed

- `construct-1in3v` (fate of `lib/orchestration/delegation-flow.mjs`): resolved to **keep the module engine-internal**. The ADR-0067 stepwise delegation flow lost its last production caller when `orchestration-delegation-next.tool.mjs` was removed under the tool-surface budget (`db90bbf2`); rather than re-expose a dispatch surface (contradicting the deliberate trim) or delete working, parity-guarded machinery, the module stays as a reserved flow surface. A rationale block now records this in the file header, the `02-deadcode:module-test-only` baseline entry is retained by design (not removed), and `tests/orchestration-delegation-flow.test.mjs` + `tests/orchestration-route-path.test.mjs` keep pinning specialist-ordering and routePath parity against `routeRequest()` so a future surface can adopt the flow without regressing. Corrected the now-stale `docs/guides/concepts/architecture.mdx` claim that delegation is driven "via the self-registered MCP tool `orchestration_delegation_next`" (the tool no longer exists). The dangling references to that removed tool in `personas/construct.md` (an active instruction to call it), `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` (`ORCHESTRATOR_DISPATCH_TOOLS` allowlist), and `STRATEGY.md` — plus the orchestrator's now-unsupported one-step-at-a-time dispatch model — are filed as a follow-up bead rather than bundled here.
- `construct-m78t` (ADR-0074 reconciliation, step 1 of the live-repo migration): merged `refactor/consolidate-project-config-dir` into staging via `integration/adr-0069-consolidate-config-dir`, resolving 53 conflicts (45 content, 6 add/add, 2 modify/delete) per the pre-flight analysis on the bead. Reconciliation decisions of record: the branch's ADR-0069/0070 were renumbered to **ADR-0074** (single project-directory consolidation) and **ADR-0075** (explicit MCP install states) — staging had already assigned 0069 (CI review gate) and 0070 (participation pipeline) — each carrying a reconciliation note, mirroring the 0071→0073 RichDocument precedent; the branch's duplicate `0071-richdocument` file was dropped in favor of staging's `0073`. Staging's versions won for the pteo2.15 substrate set (`lib/registry/org-api.mjs`, `lib/org-studio/*`, their tests), the trimmed MCP tool-surface descriptions (participation param + routePath preserved), and the deleted intake daemon (`lib/intake/daemon.mjs` + its tests stay deleted; the branch's daemon-only edits were config-dir sweeps with nothing to port). The branch's versions won for the js-yaml named-import migration (`{ load, dump }` across 10 files, matching js-yaml 5) and the resolver flip itself. Unions where both sides added features: `lib/init-unified.mjs` (staging's watch-inbox prompt + branch's commented-config scaffold, scaffold kept as the last write), `lib/document-ingest.mjs` (staging's origin frontmatter + branch's ADR-0073 asset-manifest sidecar), import blocks in 6 lib modules, and the CHANGELOG itself. Post-merge sweep fixed three staging-new `.cx` literals the branch's phase-1b migration predates: `lib/artifact-manifest-overlay.mjs` (its deliberately-temporary local `configPath` shim now imports the real `lib/config-dir.mjs` resolver, as its own comment anticipated), `lib/knowledge/search.mjs#buildObservationChunks`, and `tests/functional/participation-cross-surface.functional.test.mjs`'s observation-dir fixture; `tests/onboarding-docs-consistency.test.mjs` re-pinned from the pre-qn6e source regex to the exported `resolveShouldStartServices` behavior. `orchestration-delegation-next.tool.mjs` stays deleted (branch removal; staging's only reference was the file itself — this also clears the oracle's dead-module flag for it) while `orchestration-task-result.tool.mjs` stays (live references in the runtime, `bin/construct`, and functional tests). Regenerated rather than hand-merged: `tests/capabilities/corpus-inventory.json` + `tests/AUDIT.md` (via `scripts/generate-test-corpus-inventory.mjs`) and `package-lock.json` (from the resolved `package.json`). The full-suite pass then surfaced the second-wave class the conflict list never showed — staging-side tests whose edits beat the branch's flipped versions in auto-merge: eleven functional files' `.cx` fixtures/assertions flipped to `.construct` (embed-daemon-inbox-loop + its tick-runner fixture, embed-reasoning-executor, graph-target-build, knowledge-multiroot-corpus + its unit twin, monitor-cli, monitor-role-composed, prd-request-full-chain-audit-trail, templates-register-adhoc, unattended-budget-governance, participation-cross-surface), two init tests re-parsed with `parseJsonc` (the config is JSONC since d1r7.4 and the branch's commented-config scaffold makes comments guaranteed), `init-config-layer-tree`'s pinned entry set extended with the `launcher/` subtree that init now stages (its legacy `<home>/.cx` never-reappears assertion deliberately kept on `.cx`), the acceptance `global-install` suite flipped, and `templates/demos/**` shipped tapes/scripts/recordings/playwright-config `.cx/demos` paths flipped — the `resource-guard-rails` tape's embedded `Output .cx/demos/...` line was steering real vhs renders away from where the flipped test (and flipped code) look. ADR-number ambiguity swept across live code and docs: consolidation citations flipped 0069→0074 (`lib/config-dir.mjs`, the legacy-layout reconciler and its tests, `project-scopes.md`) while CI-review-gate 0069 citations stay; RichDocument citations flipped 0071→0073 (`lib/rich-document*.mjs`, `lib/document-assets.mjs`, `document-io.md`) while install-footprint 0071 citations stay. Audit-ratchet drift resolved: ADR-0075 linked from `docs/decisions/index.md` (repair over baseline), and two `02-deadcode:module-test-only` baseline entries added — `lib/init-unified.mjs` (spawned via `runNodeScript` path string, invisible to the import graph; newly test-imported) and `lib/orchestration/delegation-flow.mjs` (its last production importer was the deleted delegation-next tool; fate tracked in a follow-up bead). **construct-m78t completed (fresh session, step 2):** `construct sync --reconcile=legacy-layout-migration` folded this repo's `.cx/` into `.construct/` and moved the top-level launcher files into `.construct/launcher/`; a following `construct sync` repointed all 39 hook commands in the (gitignored, regenerated) `.claude/settings.json` to `.construct/launcher/run.mjs`. The four legacy force-added top-level launcher files (`run.mjs`, `bootstrap.sh`, `bootstrap.ps1`, `version`) are dropped from git tracking — `.construct/**` is regenerated machine state, not source (ADR-0027/0069), and `lib/init-unified.mjs` stages the launcher on install — and `.cx/` was never tracked, so no `git rm --cached` was needed. `construct doctor` now reports 0 failed with the legacy-layout reconciliation drift cleared (repo root is a single `.construct/`).

### Fixed

- `construct-e7amm` (OpenCode memory-bridge URL non-deterministic across shards): full-matrix CI and the local Docker CI replica (`scripts/ci-repro/`) both failed `tests/mcp-manager.test.mjs` "sync drops the legacy OpenCode cass entry" / "sync rewrites a stale OpenCode HTTP memory entry" on Linux, writing `CONSTRUCT_MEMORY_BRIDGE_URL: '{env:CONSTRUCT_MEMORY_BRIDGE_URL}'` where the test expected the literal `http://127.0.0.1:9901/` (macOS passed only as a single-shard test-ordering artifact — not platform-specific). Root cause in `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`: the OpenCode memory-bridge entry was built from `env: { CONSTRUCT_MEMORY_BRIDGE_URL: "__CONSTRUCT_MEMORY_BRIDGE_URL__" }` — the whole-value `__NAME__` template form, which `buildLocalEnvironment` (`lib/mcp-platform-config.mjs`) flips to OpenCode's `{env:NAME}` **secret reference**, since a whole-value template is treated as a credential carrier. But the bridge URL is a concrete loopback address, not a secret, so the flip left an unresolvable env ref that only materialized to the right literal when `process.env.CONSTRUCT_MEMORY_BRIDGE_URL` happened to be set (single-process shard leakage). It also preferred the stale `cass`/`memory` remote URL (port 8765) over the allocated `MEMORY_PORT` (9901). Now built from the catalog's port-templated form `http://127.0.0.1:__MEMORY_PORT__/` with `MEMORY_PORT` resolved from the env or `memoryPort()`, so it materializes to a deterministic literal regardless of shard, platform, or ambient env. Validated on Linux via the Docker CI replica.
- `construct-zy7xs` (docs site build red in full-matrix CI): the lint suite's `docs site build` step failed on the Ubuntu runner with `Failed to get registry from "yarn"` / `packageManager: yarn@npm@11.5.1`. Root cause: the root `package-lock.json` recorded only `@next/swc-darwin-arm64` (the platform the lockfile was last generated on), because npm prunes non-host platform variants of a transitive optionalDependency. So `npm ci` installed no Linux swc binary, `next build` fell back to runtime-downloading `@next/swc-linux-x64-gnu`, and that download path shells out to the runner's global Yarn 1.22.22 which chokes on the `packageManager` spec. Pinned the CI-matrix swc binaries (`darwin-arm64`, `darwin-x64`, `linux-arm64-gnu`, `linux-x64-gnu` at `^16.2.10`, tracking `next`) as **explicit** `optionalDependencies` in `apps/docs/package.json` — explicit optional deps are recorded in the lockfile regardless of host platform, unlike transitive ones — so `npm ci` installs the correct swc per runner and `next build` never runtime-downloads. The swc fix then exposed the identical pattern (npm/cli#4828) two layers down — Tailwind v4 ships two native engines, `lightningcss` (`Cannot find module '../lightningcss.linux-x64-gnu.node'`) and `@tailwindcss/oxide` (`Cannot find module '@tailwindcss/oxide-linux-x64-gnu'`), each recorded in the lockfile for the host platform only. All three families' CI-matrix binaries are now pinned (`lightningcss-*` `^1.32.0`, `@tailwindcss/oxide-*` `^4.3.2`, tracking `@tailwindcss/postcss`). Validated end-to-end by running the exact CI docs-build (`npm ci → docs:site → next build`) in a **`--platform linux/amd64`** `node:22-bookworm` container (the local Apple-Silicon Docker default is arm64, which masked the x64-only failures — an arm64 run installs the arm64 binaries and never reproduces the gap); the x64 run now completes green (412 static pages, 381 redirect stubs).
- Launcher `PROJECT_ROOT` up-walk broke under the ADR-0069 consolidated layout (found by the pre-merge gate the moment `construct-m78t` physically moved this repo's launcher). `templates/distribution/run.mjs` computed `PROJECT_ROOT = resolve(HERE, '..')` — correct when the launcher sat at `.construct/run.mjs`, but the consolidation moved it to `.construct/launcher/run.mjs`, so the one-level walk resolved to `.construct/` instead of the project root. That silently broke the self-repo resolver (`.construct/package.json` doesn't exist, so the Construct repo's own hooks fell through to a missing global `construct` → exit 127) and the workspace resolver (`.construct/node_modules/@geraldmaron/construct` → consumers degraded from fast node_modules resolution to the npx fallback). Now `resolve(HERE, '..', '..')`. The staged `.construct/launcher/run.mjs` was re-staged from the fixed template (drift guard holds byte-equality). `tests/distribution-bootstrap.test.mjs`'s two self-repo assertions — the last spots still hardcoding the pre-consolidation `.construct/run.mjs` — were repointed at `.construct/launcher/run.mjs`; full unit (4844) + functional (1085) suites green. **Follow-up (full-matrix CI):** the repoint alone was insufficient — under ADR-0069 the construct repo's own `.construct/launcher/run.mjs` is gitignored machine state and init skips staging it for the self-repo, so a fresh CI checkout has no launcher file and both self-repo assertions failed (`repo .construct/launcher/run.mjs must exist` / `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`). The old `.construct/run.mjs` passed only because it was a committed file. The two describe blocks now stage the launcher from the committed template in a `before()` hook (`ensureSelfRepoLauncher`, the same copy `stage-project` performs), so they run against a launcher whether or not the working tree already has one staged; verified passing with the staged file deleted.
- `construct-ovi8x` (dangling `orchestration_delegation_next` references): the ADR-0074 merge removed the `orchestration_delegation_next` MCP tool and `construct-1in3v` kept `lib/orchestration/delegation-flow.mjs` engine-internal, leaving three source references that still instructed calling a tool that no longer exists — a live defect, since every non-trivial orchestrated session following `personas/construct.md` would call a nonexistent tool. The persona reverts to the honest dispatch model: once `orchestration_policy` returns a non-immediate track, dispatch the returned specialist sequence (its `nextAction` names `orchestration_run`), with no per-step tool and no minted `run_id` — the "Dispatch one step at a time" section is rewritten as "Dispatch the specialist sequence". `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` drops `orchestration_delegation_next` from `ORCHESTRATOR_DISPATCH_TOOLS` (every host's regenerated orchestrator no longer grants it — verified across the `.claude`, `.codex`, `.opencode`, `.github` agent files), and `STRATEGY.md` reframes the delegation port as engine-internal with no MCP surface. The only remaining references are historical "was removed" descriptions in `docs/guides/concepts/architecture.mdx` and the `delegation-flow.mjs` header. `construct sync` re-run; `construct doctor` 0 failed.
- `construct-sqwt2`: `bd init`'s 30s ceiling flaked 7-11 init-driven tests per full parallel suite run (Dolt bootstrap under many concurrent `construct init` children was observed at 30-36s; different subset every run, all green in isolation). The `lib/init-unified.mjs` spawn ceiling is now 120s — the loud fail-on-hang contract stays, at ~3x the observed loaded worst case — and `tests/acceptance/global-install.test.mjs` raises its own project-init spawn budgets (30s → 150s, outer test 180s → 480s) so the harness clears the lib ceiling instead of killing the child first. First post-fix full-suite run: the 11 bd-init failures dropped to the 2 acceptance-harness kills this closes, and the sterile-drift report showed `construct:projects` clean for the first time (construct-9y93c).
- `construct-9y93c`: the last full-suite host-state leaks are closed. A temporary tracer in `resolveStateRoot` named the offenders: `author-artifact-context-targets` and `templates-register-adhoc` functional tests call the artifact loop in-process, and `recordArtifactProvenance → addObservation → vectorClientFor` resolves the machine-scoped lancedb dir through the real HOME, registering each tmp fixture as a real `~/.construct/projects` key (LanceDB creates the dir itself, so the `ensureDir:false` resolution never showed up as a state-root write). Both tests now redirect `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` process-wide, verified sterile; the nine leaked 40K lancedb shells from the hunt's suite runs were removed.
- `construct-nl9f` (continued): swept seven more raw `rmSync` teardown sites onto `tests/helpers/cleanup.mjs#rmTmpDir` (auto-docs, distribution-bootstrap, sync-contract, participation-cross-surface, qa sandbox, local-model-ab e2e, and the sterile-env/sterile-host-env helpers' own cleanup closures), and taught rmTmpDir's outside-tmpdir guard to also accept `<repo>/.tmp`, where the distribution/sync-contract sandboxes live and hit the same spawned-child ENOTEMPTY race.
- MCP exposed-surface budget: adding `participation_rules` to the `call` gateway's long-tail enum pushed the serialized surface to 6059 estimated tokens against the <6000 small-window budget (the margin left by the previous trim). Tightened the `orchestration_run` and `call` descriptions — same semantics, documented in full in `docs/guides/reference/mcp-tools.md` — back to 5977. Golden surface snapshot regenerated for the two new cataloged CLI commands (`studio`, `participation`).
- Test-suite hygiene for the pteo2.15/.16/.18 landings: the corpus inventory (`tests/capabilities/corpus-inventory.json`, `tests/AUDIT.md`) now indexes the four new test files, and `participation-cross-surface.functional.test.mjs` sets `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` for its in-process MCP invocations (and spawned CLI) so the machine-scoped state root never registers tmp fixtures as real `~/.construct/projects` keys — the six leaked 40K lancedb shells from the first full-suite run were removed.

### Added

- `construct-pteo2.18` (cdsp.71, cross-surface parity + routePath reconcile): one request now provably yields the same recruited set and the same reasons on all four surfaces. routePath (d1r7.15) was folded onto staging by the pteo2.15 substrate cherry-pick (`69ea7853`) and stays pinned by `tests/orchestration-route-path.test.mjs` (CLI/MCP/traces/handoffs, 6 tests). This bead closes the two remaining parity gaps: the MCP `workflow_invoke` tool now forwards the `recruitment` override (schema + handler — it was CLI-only, an agnosticism violation), and the durable decision observation written under `allow-durable-write` now records `recruited=` roles **with** `recruitmentRationale=` reasons (before, a trace audit saw who was added but not why). New `tests/functional/participation-cross-surface.functional.test.mjs` (4 tests): the cost-heavy PRD request through the real binary (CLI) and the real `workflowInvoke` MCP module returns deepEqual recruited sets + rationale; both surfaces' `.cx/observations` decision records carry every recruited role and every reason line; Org Studio's participation preview over HTTP surfaces the identical specialist→{role, gate, reason} map for the same request; and `recruitment: 'off'` reconciles identically on CLI and MCP.
- `construct-pteo2.16` (cdsp.61, CLI/MCP parity for participation rules): the participation surface is now identical on every surface. New `construct participation {list,show,add,validate,remove,preview,meta}` CLI (thin envelope over `lib/registry/org-api.mjs`, rule JSON via `--rule` or stdin, `--scope=project|user`, `--json`) and new self-registered `participation_rules` MCP tool (`lib/mcp/tools/participation.tool.mjs`, one `action` enum over the same org-api functions — long-tail, reachable via the `call` gateway so the flat schema surface does not grow). Agnosticism holds: no UI-only capability remains — list/show/add/validate/remove/preview/meta all exist on CLI, MCP, and Org Studio. New `tests/functional/participation-parity.functional.test.mjs` (5 tests, real binary + real MCP module + real HTTP server in three isolated fixtures): the same rule written via CLI/MCP/UI produces byte-identical project drop-ins, an invalid rule is refused with deepEqual-identical FieldError arrays on all three surfaces, list/show/preview/remove reconcile. Also catalogs `construct studio` and `construct participation` in `lib/cli-commands.mjs` + `docs/guides/reference/cli/core.md` (the studio cherry-pick had left the handler orphaned from the catalog, tripping the cli-catalog-accuracy guard) and documents `participation_rules` in `docs/guides/reference/mcp-tools.md` (doc-parity guard).
- `construct-pteo2.15` (cdsp.60, Org Studio participation-rules canvas): participation rules (ADR-0070) are now visually authorable. `lib/registry/org-api.mjs` — the single org-config writer — gains the participation surface every other surface must route through: `listParticipationRules` (tier-aware, flattened with owner/scope/path), `validateParticipationRule` (mirrors the coverage-gate structural contract: id shape + registry-wide uniqueness, evaluable `when` against declared watchers or the closed signalExpr grammar via the recruiter's new shape-only `parseSignalExpr`, known recruit targets, role/gate/dimension enums, enforced-needs-scope with forbidden-decision refusal and not-opted-in *warning*, legal-compliance→cx-security binding), `upsertParticipationRule`/`removeParticipationRule` (write a minimal partial drop-in in the target tier, always starting from the assembled effective rules so a project write never silently drops inherited builtin rules), `previewParticipation` (real `requestSignals` + recruiter over the assembled registry — the preview cannot disagree with dispatch), and `participationEditorMeta` (palette vocabulary). Org Studio serves the canvas: a Participation tab with a condition→rule→recruits pipeline view, a click/drag-and-drop rule editor (specialists/teams palette into recruit dropzones), enforcement-scope pickers fed by team decisionRights, inline validation with warning banners, and a sample-request preview bar; the server adds thin `/api/participation*` envelopes with the existing loopback-only + cross-origin-refusal perimeter. Substrate for this bead: org-api (`a29cde85`), Org Studio (`d984277d`), routePath (`69ea7853`), and ADRs 0072/0073 were reconciled from `refactor/consolidate-project-config-dir` onto staging per the cdsp.01 audit order, adapted to the staging layout (dead `config-dir.mjs` import dropped, layout-agnostic `customOrgDir` paths in tests, ADR renumbering 0071→0073 with reconciliation notes). Also fixes a latent Studio SPA bug where `data-field` was set as a JS expando instead of a DOM attribute, so field-level inline validation errors never attached to their form fields. Tests: 8 new org-api unit cases (validation matrix, drop-in preservation, recruiter round-trip, shadow-removal, preview, meta) and 7 new org-studio functional cases (canvas served via the real binary path, meta parity, create→list→preview→delete round-trip through HTTP, one-schema write refusal, cross-origin refusal).
- `construct-ifwhw.4` (composed build-request paper-trail test): new `tests/functional/build-request-enforced-paper-trail.functional.test.mjs` (2 tests, deterministic executor, default CI) drives "implement rate limiting for the API gateway and review for security" through the real 5-specialist chain with the construct-pteo2.14 in-run enforcement live: a seeded `engineer-to-reviewer` packet missing exactly `verificationChecklist` blocks in-run (`contractStatus: 'blocked-contract'`, run `degraded`/`blocked-contract`, runId-tagged `BLOCKED_CONTRACT` in `.cx/contract-violations.jsonl`), and the unified audit record (construct-ifwhw.1) joins the full task chain, worker trace events, and that gate verdict, with `materializeAuditRecord`/`loadAuditRecord` round-tripping deepEqual; the conforming both-ends control completes clean. This closes the paper-trail unification epic.
- `construct-d1r7.14`: **Org Studio** — a visual, no-code surface for authoring specialists, teams, relationships (contracts), and fences. `construct studio` launches a zero-dependency (ADR-0001) local web app: a `node:http` server (`lib/org-studio/server.mjs`) serving a self-contained SPA (`lib/org-studio/app.html`, all CSS/JS inline, theme-aware) over a JSON API that is a thin envelope on `lib/registry/org-api.mjs` — the single org-config writer (ADR-0072). The UI shows a live topology graph (specialists and teams as nodes, contracts as producer→consumer handoff edges, team membership dashed, the selected node's edges highlighted), inspector forms whose inline validation is byte-for-byte the same schema the CLI enforces, a scope switch (project/user), route-overlap preview for specialist drafts, and JSON export/import that round-trips the whole project topology. The server binds to loopback only and refuses cross-origin writes so a stray browser tab cannot drive local org edits; no second org-config writer is introduced. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/org-studio.functional.test.mjs`.

- `construct-pteo2.11` (cdsp.40, evolving-signal join/leave): participants now come onto and off the field mid-run as signals evolve. After each provider task completes, `executeRun` re-evaluates the task's real OUTPUT as a signal source (`extractContentSignals` → the construct-pteo2.5 recruiter, swarm assembly reused): newly recruited specialists JOIN the same run as appended tasks — executed by the remaining loop iterations — with the reason, source task, and (for team recruits, which join as a unit) the team recorded on a new durable `run.participation` ledger, plus `participant.joined` run/trace events (two new registered trace event types). A joined participant the run cancels before reaching LEAVES the same way: task marked `withdrawn` (never silently dropped), `participant.left` ledger entry + events with the reason. Bounded at 4 evolved joins per run so hostile output text cannot flood the roster, and a re-evaluation failure never breaks the run; `shapeRun` exposes `participation`. New `tests/functional/participation-join-leave.functional.test.mjs` (3 tests, deterministic fetch): a $2M cost table in the first specialist's output joins `cx-data-analyst` who then executes; a cancelled run withdraws the never-executed join with reason in record and traces; a no-signal run keeps an empty ledger. 124-test runtime sweep green.

- `construct-pteo2.7` (cdsp.22, cost/financial reference vertical): the motivating participation case, implemented as the template other verticals copy. `cx-product-manager` gains participation rule `cost-value-tradeoff-review` (`signalExpr: 'cost'` → PM as reviewer, advisory) so chains without the PM still get the value-tradeoff perspective; on `prd-draft` the PM is primary owner and `resolveRoleFramework` binds `cx-pm-value-tradeoff` (already shipped) directly. New `docs/guides/concepts/participation-verticals.md` documents the pattern — dimension keywords → registry participationRules → recruiter → surfaced rationale — with a verified copy-points table for the legal→cx-security, accessibility→cx-designer, reliability→cx-operations verticals. New `tests/functional/cost-vertical-recruitment.functional.test.mjs` (3 tests, real binary): cost PRD on `prd-draft` recruits data-analyst with the PM framework bound and traced; cost PRD on `architecture-review` adds both `data-analyst` and `product-manager` via the rule with rationale; a no-cost control recruits neither.

- `construct-pteo2.17` (cdsp.70, participation orphan-sweep matrix): new `tests/participation-matrix.test.mjs` (8 tests) sweeps the cross-entity edges the per-entry coverage gate doesn't: specialist/squad orphans (no chain endpoint, watcher, rule recruiting them, or `manualOnly`), contract producer/consumer liveness mirroring the registry validator's sanctioned-party set exactly plus an explicit retired-role pin (legal-compliance, release-manager, rd-lead, etc.), dangling rule recruit targets, watcher references in both directions (undeclared refs and declared-but-unreferenced watchers), and declared-skills-on-disk via the same `auditSkills()` the doctor uses. Emits a machine-readable matrix report (counts + orphan buckets) and asserts its shape. Current registry sweeps clean — the pinned allowlist ships empty, so the gate is red on ANY new orphan; the red path was verified by injection.

- `construct-pteo2.14` (cdsp.50, in-run executable postconditions): the run path now enforces the full `validateHandoff` pass on task output handoffs — before this bead only the standalone `workflow_contract_validate` tool ran it. New `enforceOutputHandoff` (`lib/orchestration/worker.mjs`) runs contract mustContain (both ends of the packet), binary producer postconditions, and disk-artifact postconditions when the packet names an `artifactPath`; a failed handoff marks the task `contractStatus: 'blocked-contract'` (real paid-for output preserved, never thrown away), the run degrades with `degradationReason: 'blocked-contract'` instead of reading bare `completed`, and a runId-tagged `BLOCKED_CONTRACT` record lands in `.cx/contract-violations.jsonl` — `validateHandoff`'s logged verdict now mirrors the enforcement outcome (block → `BLOCKED_CONTRACT`, warn → `CONTRACT_VIOLATION`) instead of only escalating on binary-rule failures. `shapeRun` exposes per-task `contractStatus`/`contractViolations`. Author passes join the paper trail: a failed release gate logs a `BLOCKED_CONTRACT` record from `runConstructArtifactLoop` (best-effort, never blocks the returned result). The standalone `validateOutputPacket` (shape-only, `CONTRACT_VIOLATION`) is unchanged for its existing callers. `binary-postcondition-enforcement` functional suite extended to the run path (incomplete PRD handoff blocks in-run with a durable runId-tagged verdict; conforming both-ends packet passes); worker-boundary pins updated to the strengthened semantics; 109-test contract/orchestration sweep green.

- `construct-rf26.22` (refit verification suite): four new functional suites close the refit's multi-component coverage gaps, all real-binary/real-module in mkdtemp sandboxes with zero network. `flow-join-resume`: fan-out `joinProgress` survives a crash and the and-join fires exactly once on `construct flow resume`; mismatched flow id fails loudly through the CLI; budget-exhausted is terminal (no re-entry). `init-config-layer-tree`: exact `.cx/` entry set pinned; the machine layer (`~/.construct`) is strictly lazy — absent entirely after init; config files parse with no sandbox-absolute paths. `custom-specialist-round-trip`: scaffolded specialists hot-load through a warm loader cache (`orgDirMtime` invalidation, no `clearCache`); re-scaffold refuses without `--force`; a project-tier drop-in overrides one field of a built-in and inherits the rest. `binary-flow-state-smoke`: compiles a real Bun binary (CI-gated skip without Bun; node variant always runs) and pins durable machine-scoped flow-state writes plus runtime dynamic import — the two surfaces `/$bunfs` path-collapse breaks first. 9 tests green; neighbor suites unchanged.

- `construct-pteo2.10` (cdsp.32, skills honor the recruited set): the authoring skills no longer present their fixed specialist chains as the final roster. `skills/docs/prd-workflow.md` (steps + distribution passages), `skills/docs/adr-workflow.md`, and `skills/docs/runbook-workflow.md` now state that the numbered chain is the manifest baseline, that `construct workflow invoke` results carry `recruitment: {recruited, addedRoles, rationale}` and `author_artifact` returns `recruited: [{specialist, reason, role, gate, source}]`, and instruct the host to run the recruited participants at their stated role/gate rather than substituting a memorized roster — with the override affordance (`recruitment: 'off'` or an explicit cx- id list) documented. Cross-surface parity via `construct sync` regeneration. `skills/routing.json` and certification scenarios audited: no roster-hardcoding found, no changes needed. host-mcp-emulation and skill-consolidation suites green.

- `construct-pteo2.13` (cdsp.42, enforced sign-off gate): a missing reviewer sign-off can now gate an artifact — but only by opt-in. A manifest or project-overlay entry may declare `releaseGate.reviewerGate { mode, enforcementScope: { team, decisionRight } }`; the DEFAULT stays advisory (missing sign-offs warn, exactly as before). `mode: 'enforced'` blocks the release gate ONLY when the named team's own registry entry lists the decisionRight in `decisionRights` — and never when its `forbiddenDecisions` names that decision — so a rule author cannot conscript a team into blocking (ADR-0070 opt-in-per-team). New `resolveReviewerGate` in `lib/artifact-reviewers.mjs`; `validateArtifactRelease` accepts `recruitedReviewers` (condition-recruited reviewers join the required set) and results carry `reviewerGate: { mode, blocked, missing, reason }`; `construct artifact validate` gains `--recruited=<csv>` and exits 2 on an enforced reviewer block (1 for other failures). Functional suite extended with 4 scenarios: advisory default, enforced+authorized block/clear, unauthorized and forbidden teams cannot block, recruited-reviewer merge + CLI exit 2.

- `construct-1smc4.4` + `construct-1smc4.3` (cross-repo code retrieval pins + federation scope honesty): new `tests/functional/cross-repo-code-retrieval.functional.test.mjs` (3 tests, zero network, fetch-spy guarded) pins the previously untested surfaces — per-origin attributed code-chunk retrieval through the RAG path (`origin.{targetId, projectKey, relPath, kind:'code'}` for `.py`/`.go` fixtures), `--projects` narrowing end-to-end through the real binary with `sources add`-registered targets, and multi-target code-map queries (`graph build-targets` over two repos, keyed `graph query --projects` results with import edges). 1smc4.3 re-scoped from its stale premise (markdown-only) to document the landed reality: `docs/guides/concepts/knowledge-layout.md` and `docs/guides/cookbook/multi-project-context.md` now state that federation covers docs AND code (UTF8 text extensions, vendored/build dirs excluded, `kind:'code'` origins) with the honest limit that import edges cover JS-family sources only; the `knowledge_search` MCP description matches. Every doc claim traces to named code.

- `construct-jvjow.4` (composed sterile monitoring test): new `tests/functional/monitor-role-composed.functional.test.mjs` proves the whole monitoring-as-a-role loop in one mkdtemp sandbox with zero network: the real `construct monitor --as operations --targets github:...,jira:... --no-start` binary writes the three durable setup artifacts, the real embed `Scheduler` + `registerEmbedCapabilityJobs` discovers the enabled manifest and fires the tick, and the real `createReasoningExecutor` runs with an injected fake provider — asserting the durable finding chain on disk (`.cx/runtime/embed-capabilities/operations.json` tick record `ran`/`contractStatus: ok`, `.cx/approvals/queue.jsonl` writeIntent awaiting approval, `.cx/consumption-budgets.json` ledgered spend) plus the honest opt-out counterpart (`skipped-with-reason(reasoning-executor-not-available)`, no queue, no ledger) and a repo-tree drift guard.

- `construct-pteo2.6` (cdsp.21, coverage: every specialist and team is recruitable): the hardcoded `WATCHERS` predicate map in `lib/orchestration/routing-tables.mjs` is gone — watch conditions are now registry-declared in `specialists/org/watchers.json` as structured predicates over the requestSignals shape (operators `equals`/`notEquals`/`gt`/`truthy`, combinators `all`/`any`, dot-path fields, unknown operators fail closed), closing ADR-0070 gap #1: adding a watcher is a data change. The six shipped predicates are ported with identical semantics (pinned test-by-test); new `architecture-risk` watcher binds to `cx-architect`. The 7 previously unreachable specialists now declare participation: `cx-architect` (watcher), `cx-data-analyst` (cost/data rules), `cx-debugger` (reliability advisory), `cx-qa` (wide-blast-radius review), `cx-researcher` (high-ambiguity advisory), and `cx-engineer`/`cx-orchestrator` as explicit `manualOnly: true` (chain-core, never condition-recruited — silence is no longer ambiguous). All 8 squads declare too: `governance-team` carries the ADR-0070 `legal-compliance` participation rule (recruits `cx-security` + the team); the other 7 are explicit `manualOnly`. The recruiter now reads `participationRules` from team entries as well as specialists, and a team recruit is hierarchy-aware: its participant entry lists the squad's member specialists. New audit gate `tests/participation-coverage.test.mjs` (8 tests) fails when any of the 12 specialists or 8 squads lacks a declared condition or explicit `manualOnly`, when a rule is structurally unsound (id shape/uniqueness, evaluable `when`, role/gate enums, enforced-needs-scope, legal-compliance-recruits-security), or when the hardcoded map reappears. `registry:validate --unified` passes; 113-test recruitment/routing/oracle sweep green.

- `construct-pteo2.12` (cdsp.41, execution honesty for recruited participants): a recruited reviewer either actually executes or the run says, unmistakably, that the review did not happen. `buildTasks` (`lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs`) marks tasks added by condition-driven recruitment (`dispatchReasons` entry) with `recruited: true`; new exported `assessRecruitmentHonesty(tasks)` names every recruited task that never reached `executed`/`degraded-executed`, and `finalizeRun` attaches it as `run.recruitmentHonesty` — in a mixed run where the LMCP-F4 aggregate reads `executed` and the terminal status would be a bare `completed`, the run now degrades with `degradationReason: 'recruited-reviewer-not-executed'` instead (the exact silent no-op the bead forbids); all-prepared runs keep the more specific `completed-prepare-only` while still listing the recruits. `shapeRun` (`lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`) shapes the per-task `recruited` flag through and adds a loud `RECRUITED-NOT-EXECUTED: <roles>` message whenever the honesty block is present outside prepare-only. Tests: 5 new cases in `tests/orchestration-runtime.test.mjs` (unit honesty matrix, watcher-recruited task tagging, inline prepare-only listing recruits, provider run executing recruits cleanly) plus `tests/audit/f09-orchestration/recruited-honesty-surface.test.mjs` pinning the shaped surface (3 tests); full orchestration functional set green (84 tests).

- `construct-pteo2.9` (cdsp.31, roleChain is a floor): `invokeWorkflow` (`lib/embedded-contract/workflow-invoke.mjs`) now runs the ADR-0070 recruit stage itself — request signals over the invocation input recruit reviewers onto the selected chain via the construct-pteo2.5 recruiter, appended after the manifest `roleChain` (never shrunk, order preserved: `prd-draft`'s `["product-manager","architect"]` is a minimum, not the roster). The result carries `recruitment: { recruited, addedRoles, rationale }` so `construct workflow invoke --json` shows why each reviewer joined; new `--recruitment off` CLI flag / `recruitment` request param is the caller override, and a recruitment failure leaves the chain unchanged (advisory). `runConstructArtifactLoop` passes `recruitment: 'off'` to `invokeWorkflow` since the loop owns its own request+content passes (construct-pteo2.8/.4) — otherwise the loop-level override would leak. Functional suite extended (`tests/functional/embedded-contract-workflow-invoke.functional.test.mjs`): cost/privacy-flagged `prd-draft` invocation recruits `data-analyst` + `security` with rationale through the real binary; `--recruitment off` keeps the bare manifest chain.

- `construct-pteo2.8` (cdsp.30, author_artifact recruits): the artifact-loop path now consults request signals — before this bead it never called `routeRequest` at all, so `invokeWorkflow` always received the bare `def.chain`. `runConstructArtifactLoop` (`lib/artifact-loop-core.mjs`) evaluates `requestSignals` over the authoring request, recruits via the construct-pteo2.5 recruiter, and folds recruits into the workflow invocation as an explicit role chain with `def.chain` as the floor (the declarative in-`invokeWorkflow` form remains construct-pteo2.9's scope) — when no signals fire, the invocation is byte-identical to before. Request- and content-signal recruits merge into one deduped `recruited:[{specialist,reason,role,gate,source}]` on the result; the summary names them plus the override affordance. New `recruitment` parameter (also on the MCP `author_artifact` tool schema, `lib/mcp/tool-definitions-workflow.mjs`): `'auto'` default, `'off'` disables the pass, an array of cx- ids replaces the signal-derived set verbatim; `author_artifact` results now carry `recruited` and `content_signals` (`lib/mcp/tools/artifact-author.mjs`). Advisory per ADR-0070 — recruitment failure never blocks authoring. New functional suite `tests/functional/author-artifact-recruits.functional.test.mjs` (4 tests: cost request folds cx-data-analyst into the plan, `'off'`, verbatim override, neutral-request no-op) plus a `recruited`-field assertion in the context-targets suite.

- `construct-pteo2.4` (cdsp.11, artifact-content signals): new `lib/orchestration/content-signals.mjs` reads the PRODUCED draft as a second signal source, so emergent conditions the request never named trigger late recruitment before publish — a request "write a PRD about search" with no cost language whose draft contains a $2M infra cost table now fires `cost:true` and recruits `cx-data-analyst` as reviewer. `extractContentSignals(body)` matches the same registry-declared dimension vocabulary as `requestSignals` (`lib/orchestration/signal-dimensions.mjs`, same lowercased-substring semantics) over the draft prose, table header cells, and currency-bearing table rows, parsing tables via the release-gate parser (`tableHeaderRows`/`hasTable` in `lib/contracts/validate.mjs`, now exported) — no second markdown parser. Untrusted-content posture per cdsp.81: pure lexical matching over a closed key set (hostile draft text cannot mint signal keys), fenced code blocks excluded. `runConstructArtifactLoop` (`lib/artifact-loop-core.mjs`) re-evaluates recruitment after `buildDraftBody` via the construct-pteo2.5 recruiter, excluding specialists already on the workflow plan; results carry `contentSignals` + `recruited`, the overlay folds recruits into `specialists`, and the summary names them — advisory per ADR-0070, never a release-gate change, and an extraction failure never blocks authoring. New functional suite `tests/functional/artifact-content-signals.functional.test.mjs` (5 tests, tmpdir fixtures) covers the cost-table AC end to end, header-cell matching, fence/key-minting hardening, and the no-signal null case.

- `construct-pteo2.5` (cdsp.20, generalized recruiter): new `lib/orchestration/recruiter.mjs` extracts the "signals → registry query → participant set" recruit stage (ADR-0070) out of the Oracle swarm dispatcher into a reusable, host/model-agnostic module. `recruit({signals, kind})` maps truthy signal dimensions (`lib/orchestration/signal-dimensions.mjs`) to specialists by querying the assembled registry for declared-skill matches — most-specialized-wins (fewest declared skills) on a multi-match, never a hardcoded dimension→specialist constant — and evaluates registry-declared `participationRules` (`schemas/participation-rules.schema.json`): `when.watchCondition` delegates to the shipped WATCHERS predicates via a new `watcherFires` export on `lib/orchestration/routing-tables.mjs`; `when.signalExpr` is a closed no-code-execution grammar (bare key, `!key`, `&&` conjunction) that fails closed on anything else, per the cdsp.81 untrusted-content posture. Canonical dimension affinities follow the signal-dimensions overlay convention (`.cx/orchestration/recruitment-affinities.json`, last-writer-wins). `assembleParticipants()` is the hierarchy-aware static/swarm assembly extracted verbatim from `lib/oracle/remediation-dispatch.mjs`, which now seeds and delegates to it — Oracle behavior unchanged, confirmed by the untouched 47-test Oracle suite. New `tests/recruiter.test.mjs` (11 tests) covers the bead's example mapping (cost→cx-data-analyst, accessibility→cx-designer), dedupe-with-merged-reasons, kind→role mapping, exclusions, both rule forms, expression fail-closed cases, and the preserved Oracle dispatch contract.

### Fixed

- `package.json`: the `files` whitelist shipped only `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`, but `bin/construct` dispatches to five more scripts — so for every npm consumer, `construct review legacy`, `construct optimize`, `seed-traces`, `lint:templates`, and the sync-time workflow-defs drift check crashed with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` (the drift check's non-zero exit was then misreported as "workflow-defs.mjs drift detected" on every `construct sync`). All CLI-dispatched scripts now ship; each imports only node builtins and `lib/env-config.mjs`, which was already packaged. New gate `tests/published-scripts.test.mjs` extracts script references from the CLI source and fails if any referenced script is missing from the whitelist.

### Added

- `construct-ifwhw.3` (paper-trail unification epic, final bead — composed sterile PRD test): new `tests/functional/prd-request-full-chain-audit-trail.functional.test.mjs` drives a "write me a PRD" request through the real specialist chain — `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs`'s `routeRequest` decomposes it and resolves the real `researcher-to-product-manager` contract into `run.plan.contractChain`, then `lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs`'s `runOrchestration` executes it with the `provider` worker backend and a deterministic injected `fetchImpl` (the same no-network executor-injection pattern proven in `tests/orchestration-runtime.test.mjs`, reused live by `lib/certification/real-llm-scenarios.mjs`'s opt-in S3 scenario for the same request shape) — to a durable PRD file written via the real MCP `author_artifact` entrypoint. The test then composes both paper-trail primitives from the epic onto that one real run: the `construct-ifwhw.2` provenance observation (`.cx/observations/*.json`, tagged `artifact-loop`, linked to the same authored path) and, via `build-audit-record.mjs`'s documented insertion point (setting the researcher task's `outputPacket.artifactPath` to the just-authored PRD), a real `construct-ifwhw.1` `materializeAuditRecord`/`loadAuditRecord` round trip whose `gateVerdicts` (a real runId-tagged `CONTRACT_VIOLATION` from a deliberately incomplete output packet) and `artifactVerdicts` (a real postcondition check against the on-disk PRD) both resolve. Runs in default CI — no live LLM call, no API key. Checked for overlap with `construct-rf26.22` (refit verification suite): that bead's required shape (flow checkpoint/resume, config-layer init tree, custom-specialist authoring round trip, binary smoke, full-gate run) is disjoint from this PRD/paper-trail scenario — no duplicate harness work.

- `construct-jvjow.2` (opt-in, budget-capped reasoning executor for embed capabilities): new `lib/embed/reasoning-executor.mjs` replaces the `runCapabilityTick` `skipped-with-reason(reasoning-executor-not-available)` stub with a real, off-by-default reasoning engine — `CONSTRUCT_EMBED_REASONING_EXECUTOR=1` (or `construct.config.json` `daemon.embedReasoning.enabled:true`) opts in; without it, `createReasoningExecutor` returns `null` and `lib/embed/daemon.mjs` passes `reasoningExecutor: undefined` through unchanged, so every tick keeps the exact pre-existing honest skip status. When enabled, the executor calls Anthropic's Messages API directly (the same daemon-side request/parse shape `lib/telemetry/llm-judge.mjs`'s `callLLMJudge` already uses, with the top-level `system` field placed correctly) and gates every call through `lib/policy/unattended-budget.mjs`'s `checkUnattendedSpend`/`recordUnattendedSpend` (construct-95phc.3's fail-closed `ConsumptionBudgetStore` extension) under actor key `embed-reasoning-<capabilityId>` — no parallel budget mechanism. A denied budget check returns `{ skippedReason }`, which `lib/embed/capability-jobs.mjs` now turns into the same honest `skipped-with-reason` tick status rather than a fabricated or contract-checked completion; the deterministic snapshot/plan pipeline that already ran ahead of the reasoning call is never rolled back or blocked. New `tests/functional/embed-reasoning-executor.functional.test.mjs` (fake provider + injected executor, isolated tmpdir) asserts all three states: disabled-by-default, enabled-with-budget produces a real `ran` tick with spend recorded in the durable ledger, and budget-exhausted halts reasoning without ever calling the provider or blocking the pipeline.

- `construct-jvjow.1` (unified monitor entry): new `construct monitor --as <capability-id> --targets <provider:value>[,...]` assembles, in one command, the three durable artifacts that setting up continuous monitoring-as-a-role previously required hand-assembling across three separate commands: `construct.config.json` `sources.targets[]` (via the same `lib/config/source-targets.mjs`/`lib/config/project-config.mjs` functions `construct sources add` calls), embed.yaml's `roles{}` block and a regenerated `sources:` block (new `lib/embed/config.mjs` `serializeEmbedYaml`/`writeEmbedYaml`, the write-side counterpart of the existing `parseEmbedYaml`), and the enabled `.cx/embed/<id>.manifest.json` capability manifest (via `lib/embed/capability-lifecycle.mjs` `enableCapability`, the same function `construct embed enable <id>` calls) — then starts the daemon (via `lib/embed/cli.mjs` `runEmbedCli(['start', ...])`, the same function `construct embed start` calls; `--no-start` skips this, `--supervise` also installs OS-level supervision). `--as` must name a capability already discoverable via `construct embed list`; its `embed.specialist` becomes `embed.yaml` `roles.primary`. Re-running with the same `--targets` upserts by deterministic id rather than erroring on duplicates. Prints a summary naming every file and field it wrote. New `lib/monitor-cli.mjs`; functional test `tests/functional/monitor-cli.functional.test.mjs` (real-binary, isolated HOME/cwd): usage/unknown-capability fail closed and write nothing, a full run writes all three artifacts and the summary names them, idempotent re-run, and a second capability's specialist correctly drives `roles.primary`.

- `construct-pteo2.21` (cdsp.82, prior-art and substrate validation research brief): new
  `docs/notes/research/2026-07-09-cdsp-prior-art-and-substrate-validation.md` validates ADR-0070's
  `participationRules` pipeline against ADR-0046 (registry model — consistent by construction, no
  new merge boundary), ADR-0062 (persona reasoning frameworks — compatible but the recruited-role
  vs. framework-`emits`-contract interaction is unstated in either ADR), and the bead's named
  "ADR-0072 (org-api)": that ADR is real (`docs/decisions/adr/0072-no-code-org-authoring-api.md`,
  status `proposed`, dated 2026-07-07) but does not exist on `staging` — it is stranded on
  `refactor/consolidate-project-config-dir` at commit `ff1803b0`, and its branch's own `0070`/`0071`
  numbers collide with `staging`'s already-merged ADR-0070/ADR-0071, a reconciliation-blocking fact
  the cdsp.01 substrate audit's "stranded, needs cherry-pick" framing did not surface. Five external
  sources (AutoGen, CrewAI, LangChain multi-agent docs, and two arXiv papers — DyLAN 2310.02170 and
  the Embodied LLM Agents organizational-structure paper 2403.12482) were fetched and compared:
  ADR-0070's centralized, declarative, non-learned recruitment is closest to AutoGen's rule-based
  selector and CrewAI's manager-delegated allocation, furthest from LangChain's decentralized
  Handoffs pattern, and has no analogue yet to the Embodied LLM Agents paper's runtime
  role-reassignment loop — directly relevant prior art for the still-open `construct-pteo2.11`
  (cdsp.40, join/leave). Passes `construct artifact validate --type=research-brief`.

- `construct-ztksc.5` (implements ADR-0071's install-flag rename): `construct install` now accepts `--footprint=project|user|both` (`lib/setup.mjs`'s `parseFootprintFlag`) as the canonical flag disambiguating install-write-target from the unrelated `construct scope` org-profile command. `--scope=<value>` keeps working as a deprecated alias — same values, same semantics, identical dry-run/plan output — and prints a one-line `Deprecation notice: --scope is deprecated for \`construct install\`; use --footprint instead` to stderr; when both flags are passed, `--footprint` wins silently (no alias notice). `--help`, the bare-invocation hard-error, and the dry-run plan header now read `--footprint`/`Footprint:` as primary. `README.md`, `docs/guides/start/install.mdx`, `lib/cli-commands.mjs` (and its generated `docs/guides/reference/cli/core.md` via `construct docs:site`), `bin/construct-postinstall.mjs`, `lib/config/xdg.mjs`, `lib/doctor/watchers/credential-parity.mjs`, and the Stop-hook repair message in `bin/construct` now show `--footprint` in primary examples while still documenting `--scope` as a working alias. `docs/decisions/adr/0029-install-scopes-and-hook-budgets.md` gains an "Extended by" cross-reference to ADR-0071 — its `project|user|both` semantics are unchanged, only the flag spelling changed. `tests/functional/install-scope.functional.test.mjs` extended with `--footprint` coverage plus alias/precedence/deprecation-notice assertions; `tests/functional/install-legacy-global-cleanup.functional.test.mjs` and `tests/functional/postinstall-global-scope.functional.test.mjs` updated for the renamed primary flag and guidance text. `node bin/construct doctor` and `npm run docs:verify -- --strict` both pass.

- `construct-b2t01.1`: `construct init` now asks once, at init time, whether to watch `inbox/` continuously with the embed daemon. On yes, wires `autoEmbed:true` into `construct.config.json` (`lib/embed/daemon.mjs`'s Job #9 `inbox-watcher` reads this flag) and, interactively, offers to run `construct embed supervise` to install background supervision (launchd/systemd) immediately; on no, prints the commands to enable it later (`construct config set autoEmbed true` and `construct embed supervise`). `--yes`/non-interactive runs never block on stdin — the default is off, taken instantly; pass `--watch-inbox` to opt in non-interactively (mirrors the existing `--with-adrs`/`--with-readme` flag pattern). Supervision install itself stays interactive-only so a non-interactive run never mutates system service state without a human present. New `tests/functional/init-watch-inbox-prompt.functional.test.mjs` spawns the real `construct init` binary in an isolated tmpdir and asserts both paths against the durable `construct.config.json` artifact.

- `construct-pteo2.19` (cdsp.80, FMEA challenge of the condition-driven participation model): `docs/notes/research/2026-07-09-cdsp-fmea-participation-model.md` runs the `skills/roles/devil-advocate.md` FMEA pass against ADR-0070's recruit→collaborate→execute→enforce pipeline, ranking 9 failure modes by RPN (severity × occurrence × detection). Highest-RPN finding (441): the binding advisory-default decision lets a high-risk artifact ship unsigned indistinguishably from a low-risk one, since `lib/artifact-release-gate.mjs`'s `ok` field is computed from `errors` only and `missingRequiredReviewers` (`lib/artifact-reviewers.mjs`) only ever contributes `warnings` — the doc defines the opt-in `gate: enforced` escalation criteria this bead's acceptance bar required. Also verified and recorded as concrete gates for `construct-pteo2.5`/`.6`/`.8` (cdsp.20/.21/.30): `lib/policy/unattended-budget.mjs`'s fail-closed budget governance (landed `f4d65975`) is wired into only the embed daemon and llm-judge, not into any recruitment-triggered execution path (model-spend blowup, RPN 240); `.cx/specialists/` overlay resolution (`lib/orchestration/routing-tables.mjs`'s `loadOverlays`) iterates `readdirSync` output unsorted, so overlay-vs-overlay rule conflicts resolve non-deterministically (RPN 192); a squad's `decisionRights` (e.g. `design-team.json`) is never cross-checked against its owning group's, so `participationRules`' `enforcementScope` schema has no structural guard against a squad self-declaring enforcement authority ADR-0070's own hierarchy semantics say it shouldn't hold (RPN 160). No runtime code changed; documentation-only artifact per this repo's no-fabrication rule, every claim traced to a `file:line` or `git show` output.

- `construct-ztksc.1` (learning-curve epic, "scope" vocabulary collision): `construct install --scope=project|user|both` (`lib/setup.mjs`) and `construct scope create|set|show` (org-profile lifecycle, `lib/scopes/lifecycle.mjs`) name two unrelated concepts with the same word, and both surface within the first 20 lines of README.md. New `docs/decisions/adr/0071-install-footprint-vs-org-scope-naming.md` records the decision: rename the install flag to `--footprint` (matching vocabulary ADR-0027/the existing `#footprint-contract` section already use for this exact axis) and keep `construct scope`/org-profile vocabulary unchanged (it's the more deeply embedded usage — a schema, a directory name, and CLAUDE.md's own text). `lib/setup.mjs`'s `--help` output, `README.md`, and `docs/guides/start/install.mdx` now each carry a pointer to ADR-0071 disambiguating the two meanings at the two first-five-minutes surfaces; the actual `--footprint` flag/alias implementation is deferred to follow-up bead `construct-ztksc.5`.

- `construct-pteo2.20` (cdsp.81, threat/abuse review): documentation-only threat model for
  condition-driven specialist recruitment over untrusted content — the `construct-pteo2` epic's
  design where a recruited specialist reads a produced artifact's own content (a drafted PRD body,
  feedback text) to decide whether to recruit further specialists. New
  `docs/notes/research/2026-07-09-cdsp-participation-threat-review.md` covers three threats named
  by the gating bead: (1) prompt injection via drafted content trying to trigger unwanted
  recruitment/actions — mitigated by extending this repo's existing `EXTERNAL_UNAUTHENTICATED`
  trust-stamp discipline (`lib/security/trust.mjs`, `lib/security/ingest-boundary.mjs`,
  `lib/security/recall-wrapper.mjs`) to the artifact-content signal boundary `construct-pteo2.4`
  (cdsp.11) is about to build, keeping recruitment resolved only through typed signals and
  registry-declared `participationRules`, never free-text directives; (2) UI write-safety in the
  Org Studio participation-rules canvas (`construct-pteo2.15`, cdsp.60) — verified the existing
  loopback-bind + cross-origin-refusal guardrail in `lib/org-studio/server.mjs` (on
  `refactor/consolidate-project-config-dir`) and required the new route to reuse the same
  `org-api.mjs` validator and dispatcher rather than a bespoke handler; (3) content-signal
  spoofing — an attacker crafting artifact text to force or evade which signals fire — mitigated by
  an asymmetric fail-safe default (ambiguous matches against a *required*-reviewer signal default
  to recruiting, not silence, mirroring `resolveTrustLevel`'s existing unrecognised-source-kind
  fail-safe) plus preferring structural matches (tables, acceptance-criteria lists) over bare
  keywords for high-value signals. No runtime code changed; this is a research artifact per
  `construct-pteo2`'s epic acceptance criterion 5 ("challenge beads have mitigations recorded
  before build beads close"), gating `construct-pteo2.4`/cdsp.11 and `construct-pteo2.15`/cdsp.60.

- `construct-pteo2.3` (cdsp.10, expand + externalize the signal set): `requestSignals()` (`lib/orchestration/flow-selection.mjs`) computed only a fixed set of fields, and `hasNamedConstraints` (the budget/deadline/SLA keyword flag) was dead — computed every call, consumed nowhere. New `lib/orchestration/signal-dimensions.mjs` declares six registry-declared signal dimensions (`cost`, `compliance`, `accessibility`, `data`, `reliability`, `privacy`) as plain `{key, keywords}` data, following the same canonical-entries-plus-project-overlay convention as `routing-tables.mjs`'s `WATCHERS` table (overlay path: `.cx/orchestration/signal-dimensions.json`) — adding a dimension needs no edit to `requestSignals()` or any other core function. `requestSignals()` now loops the table generically and spreads each dimension's boolean onto its output. `hasNamedConstraints` gets its first consumer: a new `named-cost-constraint` watcher in `routing-tables.mjs` (`hasNamedConstraints && cost`) is bound to `cx-product-manager`'s `watchConditions` in `specialists/org/specialists/cx-product-manager.json`, so a request naming a budget/deadline alongside cost language now proactively recruits the specialist who owns that tradeoff. New `tests/orchestration-signal-dimensions.test.mjs`: table-driven per-dimension keyword assertions, the epic's own worked example (`"PRD for enterprise tier pricing, must stay under budget, handles PII"` → `{cost:true, hasNamedConstraints:true, privacy:true}`), a neutral-request negative case, and both the positive and negative path of the new consuming rule. `node --test tests/orchestration-signal-dimensions.test.mjs tests/orchestration-policy.test.mjs tests/orchestration-events.test.mjs tests/orchestration-policy-team-routing.test.mjs` (60/60 pass); `construct doctor` shows no new failures.

- `construct-95phc.3` (cost governance for autonomous reasoning — prerequisite for the opt-in "reasoning executor" epic, `construct-jvjow.2`): the daemon paths (`lib/embed/daemon.mjs`, `lib/telemetry/llm-judge.mjs`) had two call sites that already make real, unattended LLM calls with nothing bounding their spend — the `telemetry-generation` job's periodic Anthropic/OpenRouter probe and the `llm-judge-evaluations` job's per-trace judge scoring. New `lib/policy/unattended-budget.mjs` extends the existing durable per-actor/run `ConsumptionBudgetStore` (`lib/policy/consumption-budget.mjs`, LMCP-N5) with a fail-closed default specifically for daemon-originated ticks: an unconfigured capability resolves to `{tokens: 0}` and is refused, even in solo deployment mode where interactive consumption budgets are deliberately unbounded — a human isn't present to notice runaway daemon spend the way an interactive operator would. A capability spends only once given an explicit `CONSTRUCT_UNATTENDED_BUDGET_<ID>` env var or `construct.config.json` → `daemon.unattendedBudgets.<id>.tokensPerDay`, and durably persists real token usage to `.cx/consumption-budgets.json` so a per-day cap is checked before every tick and hard-stops a batch once exhausted (`lib/telemetry/llm-judge.mjs`'s `runLLMJudgeEvaluations` now breaks out of its per-trace loop rather than skipping trace-by-trace, since a mid-batch exhaustion cannot recover). `construct doctor` now surfaces cumulative spend from the real ledgers — `lib/cost-ledger.mjs`'s USD/day total (previously wired only into the separate `construct costs` subcommand, not doctor) and per-capability unattended token spend from the new store. Tests: `tests/policy/unattended-budget.test.mjs` (fail-closed default, env/config cap resolution, check/record/durability, doctor summary shape) and `tests/functional/unattended-budget-governance.functional.test.mjs` (real `runLLMJudgeEvaluations` path: an unconfigured capability never reaches the LLM endpoint; a configured cap admits exactly the calls it can afford then hard-stops the rest of the tick, verified against the persisted `.cx/consumption-budgets.json` row; the real `construct doctor` binary prints both the USD cumulative-spend line and the unattended capability's token spend).

- `construct-ifwhw.1` (unified build-audit record): a run's task chain (`lib/orchestration/run-store.mjs`), lifecycle traces (`lib/worker/trace.mjs`), and contract-violation log entries (`lib/contracts/violation-log.mjs`) were three parallel, unlinked durable records — nothing tied a runId to the traces and violations its own tasks produced. `lib/orchestration/worker.mjs` now threads `run.runId` into `validateInputPacket`/`validateOutputPacket`'s `logViolation` calls (an optional, backward-compatible field); `lib/worker/trace.mjs` gains `readTraceEventsForRun(rootDir, runId)`; `lib/contracts/violation-log.mjs`'s `recentViolations` gains an optional `runId` filter. New `lib/orchestration/build-audit-record.mjs` joins all three into one object (`buildAuditRecord`), plus a per-task artifact postcondition verdict when a task's `outputPacket.artifactPath` names an authored doc (honestly empty today — no orchestration task populates that field yet, so this is the insertion point for a future producer, not a fabricated link) — `materializeAuditRecord`/`loadAuditRecord` persist and read it back cross-process at `<stateRoot>/runtime/orchestration/audit-records/<runId>.json`, mirroring run-store's atomic temp-then-rename write. New `tests/functional/build-audit-record.functional.test.mjs` constructs a real run, trace event, and contract violation and asserts the join.

- `construct-95phc.1` (encode the legal-review decision): recorded the 2026-07-09 user decision — legal/compliance is a participation dimension bound to `cx-security`, not a 13th roster role — as a durable, source-grounded amendment to ADR-0070's "Legal/compliance binding" section rather than a new companion ADR, since 0070 already carried the schema-level binding and only needed the concrete trigger to be named.
