The amendment cites `detectRiskFlags` (`lib/orchestration/classification.mjs:205,233,280-283`) for the exact riskFlag names (`compliance`, `privacy`, `licensing`) this dimension binds to, and the existing `specialists/org/contracts/architect-to-legal-compliance.json` → `legal-compliance-to-release-manager.json` compliance-memo handoff those flags already trigger — both contracts were correct as written and needed no shape change, only the recorded acknowledgment that nothing yet evaluates their `trigger.riskFlags` (the pass stays advisory metadata until a `construct-pteo2` follow-on bead wires a `participationRules` evaluator into `routeRequest`/`invokeWorkflow`/`runConstructArtifactLoop`). `architect-to-legal-compliance.json`'s previously-empty `description` field now states this binding and its current advisory-only status inline, so a reader of the contract alone (not just the ADR) sees the same fact.

- `construct-ifwhw.2` (author_artifact durable provenance): `runConstructArtifactLoop` (`lib/artifact-loop-core.mjs`) always invokes its workflow plan with `approvalMode: 'proposal-only'`, so the embedded-contract layer (`lib/embedded-contract/workflow-invoke.mjs`) never durably records itself for an `author_artifact` call — the authored artifact file, when one got written at all, was the only trace the call ever happened. `runConstructArtifactLoop` now writes one observation via the existing `addObservation` (`lib/observation-store.mjs`) unconditionally, before the draft-presence branch — a `.cx/observations/<id>.json` record (`traceId`, `workflowId`, `workflowType`, `artifactType`, the resolved `selectedRoles` recruited-specialist set, and the artifact's `relPath`) now exists whether or not a draft was supplied to materialize, deliberately independent of whether the artifact FILE itself gets written: the pre-existing no-draft branch is unchanged and still returns `path: null`/writes nothing. `lib/mcp/tools/artifact-author.mjs`'s `toResult` now surfaces the write outcome as `provenance: {ok, id, store}` (or `{ok:false, error}` on a non-fatal provenance-store failure, which never blocks authoring). New `tests/functional/artifact-loop-provenance.functional.test.mjs` drives the real `author_artifact` MCP entrypoint in both branches and reads `.cx/observations/*.json` back off disk to assert the record's presence and shape, and that the no-draft case still writes zero bytes to the artifact path.

- `construct-pteo2.2` (cdsp.02, ADR-0070): documentation-only ADR + JSON schema for condition-driven specialist participation, the first design artifact of the `construct-pteo2` epic ("best players on the field, in and out, configurable"). `docs/decisions/adr/0070-participation-pipeline-and-rules-schema.md` defines a recruit → collaborate → execute → enforce pipeline and names its three eventual insertion points with verified `file:line` references (`routeRequest` — `lib/orchestration/flow-selection.mjs:280`; `runConstructArtifactLoop` — `lib/artifact-loop-core.mjs:311`; `invokeWorkflow` — `lib/embedded-contract/workflow-invoke.mjs:204`); it supersedes only the recruitment scope of construct-ca4's `WATCHERS`/`evaluateWatchConditions` (`lib/orchestration/routing-tables.mjs:29-39,145-159`), leaving that file's event/doc ownership lookups untouched. New `schemas/participation-rules.schema.json` types the registry-declared `participationRules: when(signals) -> {specialists|teams, role, gate}` shape; it encodes both binding user decisions structurally, not just in prose: `gate` defaults to `advisory` and `gate: enforced` requires an `enforcementScope` naming a `decisionRight` the recruiting team's own registry entry already declares (reusing `schemas/scope.schema.json`'s existing `team.decisionRights`, never a new global switch); and `dimension: "legal-compliance"` requires `recruit.specialists` to contain `cx-security` via a closed 12-value roster enum, so a rule cannot invent a 13th role even if an author tries. No runtime code changed; `construct artifact validate --type=adr` and `construct registry:validate --unified` both pass against the new files.

- `construct-vzg2i.1` (first-run readiness line): `construct init` and `construct doctor` now print a plain, unconditional last line — `specialists will EXECUTE (provider <family> + key found)` or `specialists will only PLAN (fix: set orchestration.workerBackend=provider + a key)` — so the execute-vs-plan distinction surfaces at first run, not only via `construct orchestrate preflight`. New `formatFirstRunExecutionReadiness` in `lib/orchestration/readiness.mjs` reuses `buildOrchestrationReadiness` (the same resolution `orchestration_run` itself calls): EXECUTE requires the config-resolved `workerBackend` to be `provider` AND a materialized (non-`op://`) key present for the provider family `resolveExecution` selected; every other state (default `inline`, `host`, or `provider` with no key) reads as PLAN. `lib/init-unified.mjs` prints the line at the end of its scaffolding flow; `bin/construct`'s `cmdDoctor` prints it as the last line of output, after the `Results:` summary, reusing the readiness object it already computes for the existing worker-backend/web-capability checks. New `tests/functional/first-run-readiness-line.functional.test.mjs` spawns the real binary in isolated tmpdirs and asserts both the PLAN line (no config) and the EXECUTE line (`orchestration.workerBackend: "provider"` + `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) for both `construct init` and `construct doctor`.

### Fixed

- `construct-vzg2i.2` (execution honesty epic): a PRD/build request that resolves to `completed-prepare-only` (inline backend, no specialist LLM reasoning ran) only recorded that honestly in run metadata (`prepareOnly`/`degraded` fields) — a caller reading the actual surface it gets back, not the metadata, could miss it. `shapeRun` (`lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`) now adds a `message` field on a prepare-only run: a loud `PREPARE-ONLY: no specialist executed...` statement plus the exact remediation (`orchestration.workerBackend=provider` or an MCP host with `worker_backend=host`), reusing the same wording `construct doctor`'s Worker-backend advisory line already gives so callers see one consistent instruction. Since the MCP wire format serializes the shaped object verbatim (`JSON.stringify(toolResult, null, 2)` in `lib/mcp/dispatch-envelope.mjs`), the tool result text now states it directly; `bin/construct`'s `orchestrate run` (both the in-process and `--remote` paths) prints the same `message` line to stdout. New `tests/functional/orchestration-prepare-only-user-facing.functional.test.mjs` asserts the exact text in both the MCP tool result JSON and the real spawned CLI's stdout, and that a genuinely provider-executed run carries no such message (no false positive).

- `lib/setup.mjs` (construct-vzg2i.3): a bare `construct install` (no `--scope`) silently defaulted to the project-scope no-op and exited 0 having written nothing — a trap for every new user, since the machine-scope setup that actually installs Construct only ran when `--scope=user`/`--scope=both` was named explicitly. `parseScopeFlag` now reports whether `--scope` was passed at all; a bare invocation hard-errors (exit 1) naming the three valid scopes, while an explicit `--scope=project` keeps its documented guidance-only behavior (ADR-0029) unchanged. `--dry-run` without `--scope` is unaffected — it already discloses "no changes written" up front. README, the CLI reference, and the architecture footprint-contract doc updated to match; functional test in `tests/functional/install-scope.functional.test.mjs` asserts the new exit code and message.

- `commands/work/parallel-review.md` (construct-95phc.4): the 5-role adversarial review gate named `cx-devil-advocate`, `cx-accessibility`, and `cx-trace-reviewer` — three roles retired by the 29→12 specialist roster consolidation — so the merge gate could never resolve a reviewer set and dispatch silently failed. The gate now dispatches only names that resolve against the live roster (`specialists/org/specialists/`): `cx-architect` replaces `cx-devil-advocate` for assumption/design stress-testing (its fence already covers ADR/RFC-level assumption scrutiny, distinct from `cx-reviewer`'s code-level correctness pass); `cx-designer` (UI changes) or `cx-debugger` (non-UI) replace `cx-accessibility`/`cx-trace-reviewer` (designer owns the accessibility skills and `a11y.violation` event, debugger owns performance tracing and `regression.detected`/`hang.detected`). Added a forward-looking note documenting that a separate epic (`construct-pteo2`, not yet built) will add condition-driven invocation of additional roster roles ("cdsp recruiter") beyond this fixed 4-role baseline.

- `tests/acceptance/{packed-install,global-install,05-optional-dep-degradation}.test.mjs` (construct-rgqym): all three packing acceptance suites ran `npm pack` into the shared repo root and two deleted the tarball in cleanup, so under node --test's concurrent file execution one suite could install while another rewrote or removed the same `.tgz` — npm's `tarball data … seems to be corrupted` failure that broke the macOS legs (slower runners lose the race; Ubuntu usually wins it) of push-to-main CI on three consecutive merges. Each suite now packs into its own `--pack-destination` tmpdir and cleans up only its private copy.

- `commands/work/drive.md` (construct-95phc.2): Step 1 named `cx-explorer` as the specialist to invoke for the Explore step, but `cx-explorer` was never a live specialist id — the `rf26.11` consolidation folded it into `cx-researcher` as the `explorer` prompt-flavor overlay (`lib/roles/flavor-bindings.mjs`, `skills/roles/explorer.md`). Retitled the step to `cx-researcher`. The 4 contract files named in the same sweep (`legal-compliance-to-release-manager.json`, `architect-to-{ai,data,platform}-engineer.json`) were re-audited against `docs/decisions/adr/appendix-0065-roster-mapping.md`: their `producer`/`consumer` fields already name live specialists (`cx-architect`/`cx-engineer`, `cx-security`/`cx-operations`), their filenames follow the same documented `<flavor>-to-<flavor>` convention used by dozens of still-active contract files (e.g. `any-to-explorer.json`, `architect-to-evaluator.json`), and the one embedded `cx-evaluator` string (`architect-to-ai-engineer.json`'s postcondition id) was a previously-recorded, deliberate decision — ADR-0065 §"Additional findings" left contract `id`/postcondition `id` fields unchanged as "stable labels, not routing targets" (both `lib/decisions/enforced-baseline.json` and `tests/functional/w2-contract-enforcement.functional.test.mjs` key off `architect-to-{data,platform}-engineer`'s literal id, so renaming would be a breaking, out-of-scope change). No content or filename changes made to the 4 contracts. Verified: `construct registry:validate --unified` (35 contracts, 0 errors), `construct doctor` (`Specialist/skill audit cross-checks` ✓), `tests/contracts-coverage.test.mjs` + `tests/contracts-worker-boundary.test.mjs` + `tests/functional/w2-contract-enforcement.functional.test.mjs` + `tests/schema-validation.test.mjs` + `tests/registry-phase-1-4.test.mjs` + `tests/prompt-surface.test.mjs` (73/73 pass).

### Changed

- Dependency batch (construct-u835m — the 2026-07-06 dependabot PRs were batch-closed unmerged, applied deliberately here instead): `js-yaml` 4.2.0 → 5.2.1 (major: v5 drops the default export; all 11 import sites converted to namespace imports, `load`/`dump` API unchanged), in-range lockfile refresh via `npm update` (no majors crossed — `@ai-sdk/*` 4.x, `ai` 7.x, `apache-arrow` 21.x, `typescript` 7.x deferred to their own pass), `bun-binary-smoke.yml` checkout pin aligned to v7.0.0 (the other five action pins were already at the dependabot-target versions). `dependabot.yml` now targets `staging` on both ecosystems so update PRs follow the environment-promotion flow instead of landing unmergeable against `main`.

### Removed

- `lib/intake/daemon.mjs` (construct-b2t01.2): `buildIntakeDaemon`'s TTL/dead-letter/retry-budget inbox loop was reachable only from tests (`w5-self-loop`, `intake-pending-ttl`) — no CLI subcommand or daemon job ever called `buildIntakeDaemon`, `processInboxFile`, or `sweepPendingPackets`. The live continuous-inbox path is already covered by embed daemon Job #9 (`inbox-watcher` in `lib/embed/daemon.mjs`) and `construct intake process`, both of which scan `inbox/` via `lib/embed/inbox.mjs`'s `InboxWatcher` and persist packets through `lib/intake/queue.mjs` — a separate, actually-wired implementation. Deleted the module along with `tests/functional/intake-pending-ttl.functional.test.mjs` (entirely intake-daemon-scoped) and the intake-daemon-specific tests in `tests/functional/w5-self-loop.functional.test.mjs` (its daemon-safeguard-contract and rule-verifier tests, which exercise unrelated live modules, are unchanged). Corrected the stale `scripts/audit/02-deadcode.mjs` allowlist entry that claimed a `construct intake daemon start` entrypoint — no such CLI subcommand exists in `bin/construct`'s `cmdIntake` dispatch.

### Docs

- `construct-ztksc.2`: the EXECUTE-vs-PLAN distinction (construct-vzg2i.1's first-run readiness line) was previously discoverable only via a buried readiness note in `lib/init-unified.mjs` and one sentence in `docs/guides/start/connect-your-editor.mdx` — a new user on a non-Claude-Code host got silent PLAN-only specialist output with no clear signal why. `docs/guides/start/install.mdx`, `docs/guides/start/first-task.mdx`, and `docs/guides/start/connect-your-editor.mdx` now each explain the distinction consistently with `formatFirstRunExecutionReadiness`'s actual wording (`lib/orchestration/readiness.mjs`): EXECUTE requires config-resolved `orchestration.workerBackend: "provider"` plus a materialized (non-`op://`) key for the selected provider family (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`); Claude Code and OpenCode EXECUTE via their attached host session with no key needed, every other host (Codex, Copilot, VS Code, Cursor) needs both the config key and a provider key to avoid silent PLAN-only degradation.

### Fixed

- `docs/guides/start/first-task.mdx` + `docs/guides/start/install.mdx` (construct-ztksc.3): the docs quickstart described `construct init`, `construct sync`, and `construct dev` as three separate required onboarding steps, contradicting README's 3-command quickstart, which claims `construct init` starts local services by default. Verified against `lib/init-unified.mjs`: `construct init` calls `stageProjectAdapters` (the same mechanism `construct sync` uses) and auto-starts services unless `--no-start` or `--interactive` is passed — so README was correct and the docs path was stale. `first-task.mdx`'s init walkthrough now leads with the single `construct init --yes` command and documents `construct sync`/`construct dev` as optional follow-up commands, not required steps; `docs/guides/start/index.mdx`'s pipeline label updated to match. `install.mdx`'s top-of-page `FlowPipeline` also mislabeled its machine-setup step as `construct init` (with `construct install`'s actual behavior — config/LanceDB/completions — in the subtitle) and listed a `construct sync` step nothing in the page's body describes; relabeled to `construct install` (machine) → `construct init` (project: adapters + services), matching both the page's own prose and README. New `tests/onboarding-docs-consistency.test.mjs` pins the `construct init` auto-start code path and blocks `construct sync`/`construct dev` from reappearing as separate `<Step>`s in `first-task.mdx`'s init section.

- `construct-b2t01.3` (epic closer, "intake: one-step drop-and-forget"): a full-daemon functional test for the embed daemon's continuous inbox loop (`lib/embed/daemon.mjs` Job #9 "inbox-watcher", 2-minute cadence over `lib/embed/inbox.mjs`'s `InboxWatcher`). Previously the loop was covered only indirectly — unit tests of `InboxWatcher.poll()` and the `Scheduler` in isolation, never a real daemon boot. New `tests/functional/embed-daemon-inbox-loop.functional.test.mjs` spawns a child process (`tests/functional/fixtures/embed-daemon-inbox-tick-runner.mjs`, isolated via `sterileSpawnEnv`) that constructs a real `EmbedDaemon` with a zero-source config and calls the real `.start()`. Job #9 is registered with `{ runImmediately: true }`, so `Scheduler.start()` fires its first tick synchronously rather than waiting the full 2-minute interval; the runner polls the filesystem in short intervals for that tick's durable output instead of sleeping for the interval or mocking the scheduler. Asserts a real intake packet lands under `.cx/intake/pending/` or `.cx/intake/quarantine/` (`lib/intake/queue.mjs` contract) and an observation carrying `['inbox', 'ingested-doc']` tags lands under `.cx/observations/`, both traceable back to the dropped file. No network: the daemon config carries zero sources so `ProviderRegistry.fromEnv()` only resolves the credential-free `directory` adapter, and `CONSTRUCT_EMBEDDING_MODEL=hashing` keeps the observation-store embedding write local.

- `construct-1smc4.1` (epic construct-1smc4: multi-repo CODE knowledge — federation beyond markdown): registered directory/github source targets' actual source code, not just their markdown, now joins the federated knowledge corpus. `lib/knowledge/search.mjs` (backing `knowledge_search` and `construct knowledge search --projects=<id>`) and `lib/knowledge/rag.mjs`'s `buildCorpus` (backing `construct knowledge ask`/`index`) each gained a code-file walker — `walkCodeFiles` / `loadCodeChunks` — that folds in every file matching `UTF8_TEXT_EXTS` (`lib/document-extract.mjs`; `.ts`/`.py`/`.go`/`.rs`/... ) from a registered target's resolved content root, skipping `.md` (already covered by the existing markdown pass) and dependency/VCS directories (`node_modules`, `.git`, `dist`, `build`, `vendor`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`). Code chunks reuse the existing chunking/scoring/LanceDB-embedding pipeline verbatim — no parallel index — and carry the same structured `origin` shape as markdown target chunks (`targetId`/`provider`/`projectKey`/`relPath`/`ref`) plus `kind: 'code'`, so a code hit is attributable to its source repo and filterable via the existing `--projects`/`projects` mechanism with zero new filter code. Tests: `tests/functional/knowledge-search-code-federation.functional.test.mjs` (two local tmpdir fixture "repos", zero network — attributed `.py`/`.go` hits, `--projects` isolation, `node_modules`/non-`UTF8_TEXT_EXTS` exclusion, and a real-binary `construct knowledge search --projects=<id>` round-trip under a fetch-spy) and a `buildCorpus` case added to `tests/knowledge-multiroot.test.mjs`.

- `construct-1smc4.2` (epic construct-1smc4, per-repo code map): `lib/graph/build-import-graph.mjs` and `lib/graph/build-from-corpus.mjs` only ever built the import/symbol graph for the host project's own `rootDir`. New `lib/graph/build-target-graph.mjs`'s `buildTargetGraphs` extends this to every registered content-capable source target, resolved via the exact same mechanism construct-1smc4.1 wired into `knowledge_search` (`lib/sources/content-roots.mjs`'s `resolveContentRoots`) — no new target-resolution path. `buildImportGraph` gained an optional `sourceRoots` param (`['']` walks a target's whole content root instead of this repo's fixed `lib`/`bin`/`scripts`/`tests` layout) and `SKIP_DIRS` now matches `lib/knowledge/search.mjs`'s vendored/build exclusions. Each node is tagged `attrs.origin` (`targetId`/`provider`/`projectKey`/`kind:'code'`), the same origin shape knowledge-search chunks carry. `lib/graph/store.mjs`'s `writeGraph`/`loadGraph`/`graphDir` gained an optional `targetId` that redirects the store to `.cx/graph/targets/<targetId>/` — same JSONL + `meta.json` shape, same atomic temp-then-rename write (`writeAtomic`) the host graph already used — fully backward-compatible (all existing 2-arg call sites unchanged). New `construct graph build-targets` builds and persists one graph per target; `construct graph query <id>`/`query --type <t>` gained `--projects=<id,...>|all|self`, reusing `lib/sources/content-roots.mjs`'s `expandProjectsFilter` (same semantics as `knowledge_search`'s `--projects`, including the hard error on an unknown id) to scope a query to one or more target graphs instead of the host graph. New `tests/functional/graph-target-build.functional.test.mjs`: a fixture repo's import edge is derived and persisted to `.cx/graph/targets/<id>/nodes.jsonl`/`edges.jsonl`, a fresh `loadTargetGraph` call reads it straight off disk, and — the durability proof — `construct graph build-targets` runs in one spawned process while `construct graph query <id> --projects=<id>` runs in a **separate** spawned process and still finds the graph, plus an unknown `--projects` id fails closed.

- `construct-jvjow.3`: `pm-repos` embed capability preset — the only shipped PM preset (`pm-feedback`) bound the product-manager specialist to feedback + Confluence; the most obvious missing pairing, a PM watching engineering repos and issues directly, had no preset. New `lib/embedded-contract/workflows/pm-repos.manifest.json` binds `cx-product-manager` to `github` + `atlassian-jira` (both already-shipped `sources.targets[]` providers, `lib/config/source-target-registry.mjs`) with a `PT24H` cadence and the new `pm-engineering-signals` output contract (`specialists/org/contracts/pm-engineering-signals.json`). New `lib/embed/presets/pm-repos.mjs` is a pure deterministic `reasoningExecutor`, mirroring `pm-feedback`'s shape exactly: it inventories bound pull requests and Jira issues, flags open PRs with no activity past 7 days (stalled-pr) and Jira issues marked In Progress with no linked PR (unlinked-in-progress), and emits an artifact-only digest — no writeIntent, no provider write. New `tests/acceptance/pm-repos-preset.acceptance.test.mjs` drives the real `runCapabilityTick` over a seeded snapshot built from `FakeGitHub`/`FakeJira` (no real network I/O, fetch-guarded), asserting the stalled-PR and unlinked-issue findings cite real source ids (never fabricated), zero adapter writes, and contract validation.

### Documentation

- `construct-ztksc.4`: `docs/guides/start/install.mdx` and `first-task.mdx` no longer surface internals vocabulary (intake, signal, triage packet, `graph from-intake`, beads, broker) on the first-run path — the onboarding audit's top complaint was jumping from a simple `@construct summarize this` example straight into that terminology. `install.mdx`'s scaffold/hook-wiring bullets and deployment-mode summary now describe behavior in plain terms and link forward to `docs/guides/concepts/beads-and-state.mdx` and `docs/guides/concepts/deployment-model.mdx`. `first-task.mdx`'s "Drop a signal into the intake queue" section (inbox example, `construct intake`/`construct graph from-intake` commands, evidence flags) is replaced with a one-paragraph forward pointer to the existing deep-reference doc `docs/guides/concepts/intake-and-triage.mdx`, which already documents the full pipeline and CLI — no content was deleted, only relocated off the first-five-minutes path.

- `construct-jvjow.5` (ADR-0061 amendment): recorded the poll-vs-push monitoring stance as an explicit architectural decision instead of an implicit assumption. Verified by reading the code: every embed job (`lib/embed/daemon.mjs`, `lib/embed/scheduler.mjs`) is scheduled/interval-driven, no HTTP listener anywhere in `lib/` or `bin/` routes inbound requests to a provider, and the `webhook` capability declared in `lib/providers/contract/interface.mjs`, `lib/providers/contract.mjs`, and `lib/providers/registry.mjs` — implemented in isolation by the `github` and `jira` adapters, generated as a stub by `lib/providers/scaffold.mjs` — is never called outside its own unit tests. Outbound Slack (`lib/embed/notifications.mjs`'s `notifySlack`) posts to a Slack incoming-webhook URL after a poll cycle; it is notification, not monitoring, and receives nothing. The amendment also states what the `webhook` capability slot is for today (a declared inbound-event contract for a future receiver, not a working one) and the criteria that would justify building a real inbound receiver (sub-poll-interval latency needs, provider rate-limit pressure, provider-only events with no poll equivalent). `docs/guides/cookbook/configure-slack.md`'s "Enable slash commands" step was corrected — it previously documented a live `/webhooks/slack` endpoint that does not exist in code anywhere (`SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET` and `/webhooks/slack` appear nowhere outside that doc) — and `docs/guides/cookbook/start-embed-mode.md` now links the amendment.

## [1.5.3] - 2026-07-09

### Fixed

- `lib/decisions/golden.mjs`: the golden surface snapshot's command list came from a regex scan of `lib/cli-commands.mjs` source that paired `subcommands:` item names with the NEXT command's `core:` field — minting phantom commands and swallowing real ones, so the pinned set never matched the true CLI surface (surfaced when the `review` entry gained subcommands and the guard failed on phantom drift). `buildSurfaceSnapshot` now imports the `CLI_COMMANDS` registry and pins the actual command set; `tests/fixtures/golden/surface.json` regenerated to the true surface (136 commands, artifact entries gone).

- `pr-review.yml`: the `review` job failed on every fork PR — fork-triggered workflows get a read-only `GITHUB_TOKEN`, so the summary-comment step's `gh pr comment` was rejected (`Resource not accessible by integration`). Fork PRs now publish the cx-reviewer summary to the job summary instead; in-repo PRs keep the inline comment. First observed on #356.
- `lib/auto-docs.mjs`: `buildStructureSection`'s `git ls-tree` call let git's stderr reach the user's terminal, so every `construct doctor` and `construct sync` run from the published package (where the package root is not a git repository) printed a stray `fatal: not a git repository` line before the health check output. The call now discards stderr, matching the `stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore']` pattern used elsewhere in the codebase; the existing catch already handled the fallback. Regression test spawns `regenerateDocs` in a non-git tmpdir and asserts clean stderr.
- `pr-review.yml` + new `construct review pr` (gh-359, construct-h7501, ADR-0069): the CI `review` gate — a required-to-merge check — invoked `construct review --pr --persona=cx-reviewer`, an entry point that never existed (the workflow landed in `20d39395` without touching `bin/construct`), so `|| true` masked a Usage error and the gate posted "produced no output" on every PR while staying green. The gate now runs `construct review pr --base=<ref> --output=<file>` (`lib/review-pr.mjs`): a deterministic diff review — merge-base (`base...HEAD`) diff shape via `summarizeDiff` plus per-changed-file secret/quality heuristics via `scanFile` — that needs no model and no credentials, so it runs identically on fork and in-repo PRs. Findings are advisory (blocking enforcement stays with the secret-scanning and lint gates); the job now fails visibly when the review cannot run (`|| true` and the fallback JSON are gone), and the posted summary is honestly titled `construct review pr (deterministic, read-only)` instead of a persona attribution. Functional test spawns the real binary over fixture repos: planted-secret finding, clean diff, base-drift exclusion, loud failure on missing/unknown ref.
- `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` + `lib/adapters-sync.mjs` (construct-lqp4c): a plain `npm install` in the tool repo deleted the tracked `.github/agents/construct.agent.md` and blanked its manifest. Two-sided cause: `syncCopilot` ran with an empty write set whenever the copilot host was deselected — pruning every manifest-listed file in `.github/prompts` and `.github/agents` and degrading the copilot-instructions managed block, contradicting the prune policy (explicit-consent-only, `.github` out of adapter-prune's scope) — and `resolveAdapterHosts`' forceAll list, used by the tool-repo postinstall, omitted `copilot` entirely, so every tool-repo sync hit that destructive path. `syncCopilot` now returns before touching anything when the host is deselected, and the tool repo dogfoods copilot alongside the other hosts, regenerating the tracked agent file instead of flip-flopping it. Regression test drives the real script twice (with, then without, copilot) and asserts every Copilot output survives byte-identical; the F08 comment documenting the "regenerated/pruned by concurrent repo-root syncs" race describes the now-removed behavior.
- `package.json`: removed the ineffective `overrides` block (gh-357, construct-893i6). npm only honors `overrides` at the root project of an install, so neither pin ever applied to end-user installs. The `undici@6.27.0` pin (added to pass a Docker Trivy CVE scan) was dead — undici is no longer anywhere in the dependency tree, and a stale force-pin would be a hazard if a future dependency pulled undici v7. The `express-rate-limit@8.5.1` pin (initial-commit artifact, no recorded rationale) only froze dev installs at 8.5.1 while every end-user install already resolved 8.5.2; the lockfile now resolves 8.5.2 everywhere, removing the dev/user skew. `npm audit` is identical before and after (5 pre-existing moderate advisories, all in unrelated packages).

### Documentation

- README: document the known `boolean` and `node-domexception` npm install deprecation warnings — both are transitive dependencies of upstream packages (`onnxruntime-node`, `@lancedb/lancedb`), harmless, and not silenceable from this package because npm ignores a package's own `overrides` on end-user installs.

## [1.5.2] - 2026-07-09

### Added

- `construct-760c.8` (B8: multi-project context targets — epic closer): source-target operability + discoverability. New `lib/doctor/source-target-health.mjs` (filesystem + env only, zero outbound fetch) surfaces a directory target whose path was deleted (actionable: fix the path / `construct sources remove <id>`), a corpus cache missing or older than its TTL (`construct sources sync <id>`), and a soft credential-presence notice for network-backed targets — wired into the `construct doctor` command and registered as an hourly daemon watcher (`lib/doctor/watchers/source-targets.mjs` in the `WATCHERS` list). The health read uses the RAW declared targets (not the validated config) so a deleted directory path — which fails config validation — is still flagged rather than silently sanitized away. A project with no registered targets stays silent (no noise); the default doctor keeps its zero-network invariant. New end-to-end guide `docs/guides/cookbook/multi-project-context.md` walks the whole capability — register targets → search/synthesize across projects → bind context to runs → contribute to Jira → set a model policy. Tests: `tests/functional/doctor-source-targets.functional.test.mjs` (real-binary: healthy report, deleted-path flag, aged-cache flag, zero-target silence, all under a fetch-spy proving zero network) and `tests/doctor-source-target-health.test.mjs` (zero-target silence, healthy directory, credential soft-notice). This closes epic `construct-760c` (multi-project context targets): B1 source targets, B2 multi-root corpus + provenance, B3 cross-project synthesis, B4 orchestration context bindings, B5 template-optional generation, B6 Jira contribution, B7 model policy, B8 operability + docs.
- `construct-760c.6` (B6: multi-project context targets): Jira contribution workflow — analyze registered project corpora against a tracker and contribute governed, evidence-cited issue proposals, never writing without approval. New `lib/tracker/contribute.mjs` runs a 5-stage pipeline: FETCH existing issues (injectable; default reads via the jira embed provider's JQL), ANALYZE the B2 multi-root corpora for work the tracker doesn't cover (deterministic, retrieval-only — no model — so the proposal set is stable and CI-testable), PROPOSE a persisted proposal artifact (JSON + markdown under `.cx/tracker/proposals/<id>.{json,md}`) where every proposed issue carries a summary, an origin-cited description (`projectKey:relPath` from B2; facts beyond the source marked `[unverified]`), a suggested type, and a stable idempotency key, DEDUPE proposed vs existing issue summaries by token similarity (near-duplicates suppressed and reported with the matched issue key), and APPLY the batch through the existing governed `provider_write` path — dry-run by default (writes nothing), an approval token required to execute, idempotent so a re-apply of the same proposal creates no duplicates (an audit sidecar links issue key ↔ proposal id). Beads are never touched (R5): bd stays the internal tracker. New `construct tracker contribute --target <id> [--against <ids|all>]` emits the proposal; `--apply <proposal-id> [--approve <token>]` applies it (dry-run without `--approve`). Provider-generic: eligibility is the tracker target's write capability, so github/linear issue contribution comes nearly free later. Tests: `tests/functional/tracker-contribute.functional.test.mjs` (AC1–AC5 with the Jira read/write seams stubbed for determinism: evidence citation on every proposal, near-duplicate suppression with matched key, dry-run-without-approval writes nothing, approved write payload shape + idempotency key + audit linkage, and re-apply no-op). Manual non-CI step recorded on the bead: run against the user's real Jira sandbox with 1Password-resolved credentials.
- `construct-760c.3` (B3: multi-project context targets): cross-project synthesis — a map-reduce over the B2 multi-root corpus so one question can be answered across several registered projects with origin-cited results. New `lib/knowledge/synthesis.mjs`: the MAP pass is retrieval-only (per project, top-K from that project's content root, extracted as an attributed section — deterministic and model-free); the REDUCE pass is a single synthesis call over the per-project sections whose every cross-project claim must cite its source as `project:path`. New `construct synthesize --ask "<q>" [--projects=all|self|id,...] [--template <name>] [--dry-run] [--json]`: `--dry-run` emits the fully assembled per-project context + citation table + the reduce prompt with zero model output (the deterministic, CI-testable preview); an unknown project id is a hard error before any model call. `author_artifact` gains a `context_targets` param (`lib/mcp/tools/artifact-author.mjs`): ids are validated like orchestration bindings (unknown → hard error before authoring) and the assembled multi-project context is woven into the authoring input so an artifact can draw on and cite each bound project — a caller-supplied draft still passes the normal release gate unchanged. Ships `templates/docs/strategy-comparison.md` (convergence/divergence/recommendation shape; template shapes the reduce headings only) with a `strategy-comparison` ownership exemption in `lib/template-registry.mjs`. Works with a registered template, a `.cx/templates/docs/` override, or none (R1). Tests: `tests/functional/cross-project-synthesis.functional.test.mjs` (real-binary dry-run: attributed sections, citation table, determinism across runs, template vs no-template, unknown-id rejection, `--projects` scoping) and `tests/functional/author-artifact-context-targets.functional.test.mjs` (valid context_targets author pass clears the gate; bogus id is a hard error). Live-LLM reduce reuses the `claude --print` path (rag `ask` pattern); only the deterministic dry-run is gated in CI.
- `construct-760c.4` (B4: multi-project context targets): structured context-target bindings for orchestration runs. `planRun` (`lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs`) accepts an optional `contextTargets: [{id, role?}]`, exposed as `context_targets` on MCP `orchestration_run` (`lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`) and `construct orchestrate run --context=id[:role],...` (both `--context=` and `--context ` forms). New `lib/orchestration/context-bindings.mjs` validates each id against the project's effective `sources.targets[]` at PLAN time — an unknown id throws `ContextTargetError` (`CONTEXT_TARGET_UNKNOWN`) before any task is built or any run record is saved, so a run never plans against context it can't reach (never a silent skip) — and resolves each to a binding record `{id, provider, role, resolution, contentRoot, ref}`. Content-capable targets (directory / synced corpus, via B2's `lib/sources/content-roots.mjs`) resolve a reachable `contentRoot`; targets with no local content (a tracker like jira) resolve with `contentRoot: null`. `role` is an optional free-form hint threaded verbatim — no role enum in core. Bindings are persisted on the run record (only when present, so a run without them is byte-identical to a pre-B4 record) and surfaced on `hostAdapterMetadata` and `shapeRun` output (`contextBindings`, a typed `[]` when absent) for run inspection. Omission leaves today's implicit source resolution untouched. Tests: `tests/functional/orchestration-context-bindings.functional.test.mjs` (real-binary `--context` bind + persistence via `orchestrate status`, bogus-id plan-time rejection, omission baseline — LLM-free via `--no-execute`) and `tests/orchestration-context-bindings.test.mjs` (resolver unit: unknown-id error, role threading, directory contentRoot, jira null root, dedup, omission default).
- `construct-760c.2` (B2: multi-project context targets): multi-root knowledge corpus builders with chunk-level provenance, so retrieval can search and cite across every registered project. Every corpus chunk now carries a structured `origin` — `{targetId, provider, projectKey, relPath, ref}` — instead of nothing, and the host project is the reserved origin (`targetId: null`, `projectKey: "self"`). New `lib/sources/content-roots.mjs` resolves the content-capable subset of `sources.targets[]` (B1) to on-disk roots — `directory` targets to their path, corpus `github` targets to their synced repo-cache checkout — keyed off the manifest `content`/`existsAs` descriptors, never a hardcoded provider name; a target whose content isn't present (a vanished dir, a never-synced corpus) is silently omitted. `lib/knowledge/rag.mjs`'s `buildCorpus(rootDir)` gains a back-compatible `{roots}` option (single-root callers unchanged) that folds each registered project's markdown into one corpus, every chunk attributed and carrying a per-file `relPath`; `construct ask` resolves and passes those roots and shows `«project»` attribution on cited sources. `lib/knowledge/search.mjs` (backing both `construct knowledge search` and MCP `knowledge_search`) widens each source with the same structured origin, resolves registered target roots from project config, and gains a `projects` filter: `--projects=<id,...>` narrows retrieval to named source projects, `all` to every content target, `self` to the host — an unknown id is a hard error naming the known projects (never a silent empty result), and the widened `origin` (with a `kind` subfield preserving the prior source-type label) rides on every hit and MCP response. `construct ingest <dir> --as=<targetId>` stamps re-verifiable provenance (`origin_target_id`/`origin_provider`) into the written knowledge file's frontmatter via a new generic `extraFields` pass on `lib/doc-stamp.mjs`; `ingest` without `--as` is byte-identical to today, and an unknown `--as` id is a hard error. Distill stays single-root (R4: out of scope here — no cheap multi-root path). Tests: `tests/functional/knowledge-multiroot-corpus.functional.test.mjs` (real-binary two-directory-target search with cross-project attribution, `--projects` scoping, unknown-id hard error, `ingest --as` provenance — all under a fetch-spy proving zero network) and `tests/knowledge-multiroot.test.mjs` (content-root resolution, `--projects` expansion, `buildCorpus` origin threading + back-compat). The `research.project-search` capability test was updated for the widened origin shape (`origin.kind === 'project'`).
- `construct-760c.1` (B1: multi-project context targets): `directory` and repo-corpus source targets with a local content cache. `lib/extensions/manifests/directory.manifest.json` gains a `sourceTarget` block (path selector with tilde expansion + existence-as-directory validation at `sources validate`/`add` time), making `directory` a valid `sources.targets[].provider`; `lib/embed/providers/directory.mjs` (registered via the manifest-driven `ADAPTERS` table in `lib/embed/providers/registry.mjs`, credential-free so always available) reads markdown/doc records from the registered path with zero network access. The github manifest's `sourceTarget` gains an optional `content` descriptor — a target that sets `content: {mode:"corpus", ref?}` (byte-identical to today when omitted) opts into a shallow git clone/fetch cache at `~/.construct/projects/<key>/context-repos/<targetId>/` (ADR-0066 state root, never the project tree; `construct init` never scaffolds it) with a `<targetId>.meta.json` freshness sibling, driven by the new `lib/sources/repo-cache.mjs`. Corpus eligibility is keyed off the manifest `content` descriptor, not a hardcoded `provider === 'github'` check, so any future git-hosted provider inherits caching from its manifest. New `construct sources sync [<id>]` clones on first run and fetches incrementally (re-run-safe) thereafter; `sources list` shows corpus content mode + cache freshness. `lib/embed/demand-fetch.mjs`'s unscoped universal fallback now drives only env/poll-fetchable providers (those whose descriptor declares `embed`/`legacyEnv`), so the always-registered target-only `directory` provider no longer flips the "no providers configured" outcome — dispatch stays descriptor-driven, no provider name hardcoded. Tests: `tests/functional/source-target-directory.functional.test.mjs` (real-binary add/validate/list roundtrip over a fixture doc tree, bad-path rejection, github corpus sync against a local `file://` bare repo with clone→fetch incremental assertion, DirectoryProvider read, all under a fetch-spy proving zero outbound network). Existing github/jira/slack/linear source-target behavior unchanged.
- `construct-760c.7`: model policy presets + explain, layered on the existing resolution chain. New `construct models policy show` renders the effective policy — resolved model and winning source per tier (env-pin override attributed over registry default) plus the work-category→tier map — and `construct models policy set <budget|free|frontier|local>` computes per-tier assignments from configured providers and persists them to `specialists/org/models.json`, the single edit surface (no env writes, no config-schema writes; env pins still override). New `construct models explain --role <specialist>` prints a per-specialist resolution trace matching `models resolve --json --tier <tier>`. The `budget` preset ranks candidates by live OpenRouter pricing (`lib/model-pricing.mjs`), hard-excludes flagship ids, and ranks any candidate with an unfetchable price LAST (never $0) — so with only an OpenRouter credential no frontier model resolves for any tier or work category, even when pricing is unreachable (it then falls back to a static ordering and says so). `free` writes only `:free` slugs and reports (never substitutes) a tier with no free model; each preset refuses with a clear message when no provider credential can back it, so a clean install stays all-tiers-null (release-gate d1r7.5 unbroken). New `lib/model-policy.mjs`; `bin/construct` `cmdModels` gains `policy`/`explain` subcommands (+ `--no-sync` on `policy set` for scripted runs). Tests: `tests/functional/model-policy.functional.test.mjs` (budget-invariant sweep through the real binary with a fetch-spy pricing fixture and PATH stripped, models.json-only mutation, env-pin attribution, free-preset reporting, and the missing-price safety guard). Docs: `docs/guides/cookbook/plug-in-your-own-llm.mdx` gains a "Model policy presets" section.
- `construct-760c.5` (adhoc golden fixture): added `tests/fixtures/artifacts/adhoc/golden.md` so the sanctioned `adhoc` type has the golden fixture every manifest type requires. Without it, six certification/release-gate checks failed with `missing golden fixture: adhoc` (golden-fixtures existence, `all golden artifact fixtures pass release gates`, artifact-provenance structure + accessibility, hermetic P2, `runReleaseCandidateGate`, and `certify gate passes on HEAD`). The fixture is the deterministic output of `buildGoldenFixtureMarkdown('adhoc')` — free-form body with the citation + one-paragraph prose floor the adhoc release gate enforces.
- `construct-760c.5` (template-optional generation): a document class no longer has to be pre-registered in the builtin `specialists/artifact-manifest.json` to be generated. Two paths, both keeping the builtin manifest byte-identical (a new gate, `tests/functional/templates-register-adhoc.functional.test.mjs`, asserts this). (1) `construct templates register <type> [--description ...] [--from <file>] [--force]` writes a project template under `.cx/templates/docs/<type>.md` (the override dir that already wins `get_template` resolution) and a PROJECT-TIER overlay entry in `.cx/artifact-manifest.overlay.json`. `lib/artifact-manifest.mjs`'s `loadArtifactManifest` now deep-merges three tiers — builtin < user (`~/.config/construct/`) < project — via new `lib/artifact-manifest-overlay.mjs` (mirroring `lib/extensions/loader.mjs`), keyed by root+cwd+overlay-mtime so a register earlier in a long-running MCP process invalidates the cache. A registered class resolves author/reviewer chain and output path from memo-like defaults, overridable per overlay entry (e.g. `outputDir`). (2) A sanctioned `adhoc` type (code-injected below the builtin tier, so a builtin of the same name always wins) authors a one-off from `{type:"adhoc", title, instructions}` with zero prior registration, drafting from the instructions-driven synthetic scaffold in `lib/artifact-loop-core.mjs` and still clearing the full release gate — free-form structure, not free-form quality. `adhoc` requires explicit title + instructions and is not a bypass for a registered class (naming a known type through adhoc is redirected). `author_artifact` gained a `dry_run` scaffold-preview mode and now returns the classification/registration result for an unknown non-adhoc class instead of silently drafting a PRD (gate intact). Ships `templates/docs/adhoc.md` as the free-form starter shape (exempted in `lib/template-registry.mjs`).

### Changed

- `construct-v1wk` (findings 2 + 4 from the `construct-2xp5` sweep): the model-tier vocabulary `['reasoning', 'standard', 'fast']` had been re-typed as a literal in ~30 places (the router's family tables, the registry validator's accept-list, setup, cost selection, policy presets, the CLI, and two MCP tool-schema enums), so adding or renaming a tier could silently diverge the accept-lists from what the router actually resolves. New zero-dependency `lib/model-tiers.mjs` exports the canonical `MODEL_TIERS` / `MODEL_TIER_SET` / `isModelTier`, and every non-vendored consumer now imports it (`lib/model-router.mjs`, `lib/validator.mjs`, `lib/setup.mjs`, `lib/model-cheapest-provider.mjs`, `lib/models/catalog.mjs`, `lib/orchestration/readiness.mjs`, `lib/mcp/tool-definitions-workflow.mjs` via `[...MODEL_TIERS]`, `bin/construct`, and `lib/model-policy.mjs` where `POLICY_TIERS` is now an alias). The intentionally self-contained `lib/embedded-contract/` bundle keeps its own copy on purpose. Separately, `lib/orchestration/classification.mjs` gains an exported `docAuthoringDocTypes()` and a new drift-guard test (`tests/orchestration-doc-authoring-patterns.test.mjs`) asserting every `DOC_AUTHORING_PATTERNS` docType is a registry-known doc type, so a docType renamed/removed in the registry can no longer leave a pattern resolving to a null owner. (Finding 3 is closed separately as `construct-r70rz` below.)
- `construct-r70rz` (finding 3 from the `construct-2xp5` sweep): `lib/embed/demand-fetch.mjs` `resolveKnownSources()` no longer re-implements descriptor-driven target building by hand. The manual fallback (when no config or legacy-env target resolves) had hardcoded the github `repoWords` alias fan-out, the jira/linear provider names, their env-var names, and their display prefixes — duplicating `resolveKnownSourcesFromTargets(legacyEnvSourceTargets(env))` on the primary path. The key observation: the fallback only runs when `resolveEffectiveSourceTargetsFromConfig` (which already merges `legacyEnvSourceTargets`) is empty, which means `GITHUB_REPOS`/`JIRA_PROJECTS`/`LINEAR_TEAMS`/`SLACK_CHANNELS` were all empty — so the manual github-repos, jira-projects, and linear-teams loops were iterating empty arrays (dead code). The only live behavior was a credential-only catch-all: a generic `jira` entry when `JIRA_BASE_URL` is set and a generic `linear` entry when `LINEAR_API_KEY` is set, each with no explicit list. That gating is now declared on the manifests (`aliases.catchAllCredentialEnv` — `["JIRA_BASE_URL"]` on `atlassian-jira`, `["LINEAR_API_KEY"]` on `linear`) and the fallback iterates `listSourceTargetDescriptors()` and emits the generic source only for a `catchAll` provider whose declared credential env is present — no provider name, env var, or display prefix hardcoded in demand-fetch. Slack (no `catchAllCredentialEnv` gate) stays intentionally un-advertised without an explicit channel, matching prior behavior. Behavior-preserving: the primary path is untouched. Tests: `tests/embed-demand-fetch.test.mjs` gains a `credential-only fallback` suite (jira-via-`JIRA_BASE_URL`, linear-via-`LINEAR_API_KEY`, neither-without-credential, slack-not-advertised, both-credentials) run from an isolated tmpdir cwd so the repo's own config can't shadow the fallback; the existing github/jira/linear expansion tests (which exercise the descriptor primary path) are unchanged.

### Fixed

- `construct-7d9t`: cleared the last four residual `npm test` failures on a clean-config dev machine (the fifth, the `golden-surface` snapshot, regenerated earlier in `4d1af08b`). (1) MCP flat-surface budget overrun — the OpenCode-reachable tool surface was 6361 tokens vs the `<6000` lean-surface budget; demoted `workflow_invoke` (529 tok) out of `CORE_TOOL_NAMES` (`lib/mcp/server.mjs`) so it now dispatches through the `call` gateway like the rest of the long tail, dropping the surface to 5837 tokens / 17 tools. It is the PLAN-only counterpart to `orchestration_run` (the EXECUTE tool, which stays flat), so the flat-surface loss is minimal and it stays fully reachable. (2+3) Two environment-hermeticity gaps in functional tests, both the same root cause: `resolveSecret`'s ambient `project-env` tier reads a `.env` at `process.cwd()`, and this repo's own gitignored `.env` carries live `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, defeating each test's "only key X present" premise. `tests/functional/model-resolver-no-defaults-chain.functional.test.mjs` now runs the schema-infer subtest from an isolated tmpdir cwd so no ambient `.env` resolves the OpenRouter path (the throw path fires as designed, no network call); `tests/functional/secret-resolver-real-op.functional.test.mjs` spawns its resolver child from the empty sandbox with `HOME` repointed there and the op-env-catalog pointer dropped, so the injected `op://` reference materializes through the stub `op` (op read count 1/2 as asserted) instead of being pre-empted by the repo's real key. All five residuals now pass as real fixes, not environment-gated skips.
- `construct-j745`: the session-start "Provider sources wired" hint is now built by iterating the manifest-declared source-target descriptors (`lib/config/source-target-registry.mjs` `listSourceTargetDescriptors()`) instead of four hardcoded `provider === 'github'/'jira'/'linear'/'slack'` filters in `lib/hooks/session-start.mjs`. Previously a fifth manifest-registered `provider_fetch` source, or a renamed manifest selector field, would be silently dropped from the hint — the orchestrator would then report "no context" for a source that was actually configured. The rewrite gates on the descriptor's `embed` block (the network `provider_fetch` targets declare it; a content-only `directory` target does not, so it stays out of a hint that says "use `provider_fetch`") and derives each label from `aliases.displayPrefix` with a title-cased provider fallback. New regression test in `tests/session-start-hook.test.mjs` configures all four providers via legacy env and asserts each is named.
- `construct-1l11`: `tests/functional/gate-worker-backend-visibility.functional.test.mjs` spawned the CLI with `cwd: REPO`, so config resolution walked up from the repo root and read a developer's gitignored `construct.config.local.json` (e.g. `orchestration.workerBackend=provider`) — flipping the resolved backend and failing three "with no config" gate assertions on the dev machine (green on a clean checkout/CI). The fixture now spawns from an isolated tmpdir cwd (isolating HOME alone was insufficient — config resolves from cwd), so the gates assert true default behavior regardless of any local override present.
- Linked `docs/guides/cookbook/multi-project-context.md` (shipped under `construct-760c.8`) into the `docs/README.md` cookbook index — it was unreachable from the README link-closure, which the `03-docs` orphaned-doc audit finder flagged as new drift beyond `scripts/audit/baseline.json` (failing the `audit-ratchet` gate). Linking the doc, rather than baselining the id, keeps the mandatory-docs invariant intact.
- `construct-6yo6o` (deterministic evidence gate): governed research output is now checked for citations by construction, closing the fabrication path a Free-Models-Router session exposed (a confident "research" answer inventing "Construct 3.0"/"DPKP"/"A' Expressions" — zero real sources, every web fetch failed). The `cx-researcher` persona forbids fabrication, but only as an honor-system prompt a weak/free model ignores. New `lib/orchestration/research-evidence-gate.mjs` `gateResearchEvidence({output, role, request})` is kind-aware: it infers the expected evidence kind from the request (external → a verifiable URL/DOI/arXiv id, codebase → a `file:line`, UX → a transcript/recording ref) and flags a *substantial* research answer that carries none of its expected kind. An honestly short answer or one that self-declares insufficient-evidence is never penalized, and non-research roles are out of scope. Applied at both finalization points in `lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs` (provider and host backends) so it is model-tier-agnostic — it validates the OUTPUT, never the choice of model, so a free-model selection is honored with no paid escalation. The verdict rides the task as `evidenceGate {ok, kind, citationCount, reason?}` and is surfaced through both `shapeRun` (the MCP/API response) and `hostAdapterMetadata`; a failing gate marks the task degraded rather than shipping it as verified, complementing the existing `unverifiedCitations` check (which flags a cited URL absent from governed webEvidence). Tests: `tests/orchestration-research-evidence-gate.test.mjs` (kind inference, zero-source flagging, URL/DOI/arXiv and file:line passes, honest-refusal and non-research exemptions) plus two end-to-end runtime tests in `tests/orchestration-worker.test.mjs` (an unsourced research task is flagged, a sourced one passes). Also, the invalid-tool-call half: `orchestration_policy` now hands back an explicit machine-actionable `nextAction` (`{ tool: 'orchestration_run', arguments: { request }, instruction }`) for any non-immediate track, and `null` for the immediate track. A weak model given only a `suggestedWorkflowType: research-synthesis` label would infer a tool from it (calling a non-existent `research_synthesis`/`construct-mcp_research_synthesis`) or freelance raw host `WebFetch`/`glob` — in both cases bypassing the governed specialist chain and the `cx-researcher` anti-fabrication persona, which is how a Free-Models-Router session produced a fully fabricated "research" answer. The `nextAction` routes the caller into `orchestration_run` (where the persona and evidence discipline apply) and its `instruction` states plainly that the workflow type is a plan label, not a tool. Additive (existing policy fields unchanged; 41 policy-shape assertions still green) and tier-neutral — `worker_backend` is deliberately left unset so the project's configured backend (free/inline/host) is honored, never escalated to a paid provider. Regression test in `tests/orchestration-policy.test.mjs` (nextAction present + routes to orchestration_run + warns against tool-from-workflow-type for orchestrated work; null for immediate). Follow-up on the bead: a deterministic evidence gate on governed research output so no-verifiable-citation results degrade rather than ship (honor-system persona enforcement is insufficient for weak free models).
- `construct-olpf` (re-landed on staging): the worker's `classifyWorkerProvider` (`lib/orchestration/worker.mjs`) again misrouted OpenRouter-served Claude slugs to the direct Anthropic API. `isAnthropic(provider, model)` matches `/anthropic|claude/` on the provider/model strings, so `openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6` (provider `openrouter-anthropic`) was classified `anthropic` and dispatched to `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` instead of `openrouter.ai/v1/chat/completions` with `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` — contradicting the module's own documented rule that `openai/*` slugs stay on OpenRouter unless the provider is explicitly `openai`. The original fix (`7bafd4aa`) had been verified on a worktree branch but never merged to `staging`, so mainline still carried the bug and its regression test was absent. Re-applied: `classifyWorkerProvider` now honors an explicit `openrouter/` model prefix (or a `provider` naming openrouter) before the `isAnthropic` substring heuristic. Regression test added to `tests/orchestration-worker.test.mjs` (an `openrouter/`-prefixed claude slug hits `openrouter.ai`, never `anthropic.com`, even with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in env). Surfaced by re-running the `construct-f8w6.4` CLI research smoke in an isolated `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` state root under real 1Password auth: the run first failed on Anthropic-direct billing (`credit balance is too low`), then — after the fix — completed with the researcher task `done` via `executor=provider:openrouter`, 6863 chars of real output, `webCapability=provider-native`, `webSearchRequests=25`, `webEvidence=25`, and a Sources section citing 8 primary `nodejs.org` URLs (`degraded=false`, `executionMode=construct-orchestrated`).
- `construct-nl9f` (ENOTEMPTY teardown-race sweep, round 5): extended the `rmTmpDir` teardown-helper adoption beyond the original `tests/functional` + `tests/acceptance` sweep (commit `a1cac2fe`) and the `knowledge-trends` miss (`9deb1910`) to the remaining in-`tests/`-root and subdir suites that spawn a real subprocess and then `rmSync` an `os.tmpdir()` sandbox — the exact shape that intermittently throws `ENOTEMPTY: directory not empty` under full-suite CI-load concurrency (a spawned child's file handles release after the recursive walk has already passed their directory, so `rmSync`'s `maxRetries` can't recover). Swept ~69 teardown sites across 37 files (`tests/hooks/*`, `tests/audit/f0x-*`, `tests/providers/*`, `tests/graph/*`, `tests/mcp/*`, `tests/packs/*`, `tests/security/*`, `tests/consumer/*`, and ~a dozen `tests/*.test.mjs`) to `tests/helpers/cleanup.mjs#rmTmpDir` (retry budget 10×250ms twice, then swallow-with-warning; throws on any non-`os.tmpdir()` path). Deliberately NOT swept: `tests/mcp-manager.test.mjs` (sandboxes rooted at a hardcoded `/tmp` literal, not `os.tmpdir()`), `tests/distribution-bootstrap.test.mjs` and `tests/sync-contract.test.mjs` (sandboxes from `writableTmpRoot()`, which prefers a repo-local `.tmp/tests` dir) — `rmTmpDir` would correctly refuse those non-tmpdir paths, so their raw `rmSync` teardowns are left intact; and per-test `beforeEach` `rmSync(repoDir)` resets (reset-then-`mkdirSync`, not sandbox teardown) were left as-is. Verified: all 37 files green (201 tests, 0 fail), comment-lint clean, eslint at baseline parity (4 pre-existing warnings, none introduced). No product code touched — test-teardown hardening only. Acceptance (a clean full-suite CI-load stress run showing zero ENOTEMPTY) still requires a real CI run; the bead stays open pending that evidence, since the race does not reproduce locally in isolation.
- `construct-mtgs` (state-root HOME-isolation, round 4): three in-process test files leaked project-state directories into the real `~/.construct/projects/` on the machine running the suite. Each called durable observation code — `captureSessionArtifacts` (`tests/artifact-capture.test.mjs`), `runBootstrap` (`tests/bootstrap.test.mjs`), and `invokeWorkflow`'s allow-durable-write case (`tests/embedded-contract-workflow-invoke.test.mjs`) — against a `mkdtemp` project root without isolating `HOME`, so `addObservation → vectorClientFor → resolveStateDir` (ADR-0066) resolved against the developer's real home. Fixed by pinning `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` to a throwaway home for the whole file (the `tests/orchestration/persona-degraded.test.mjs` idiom) and restoring it in an `after()` hook. Found via temporary stack-trace instrumentation in `lib/state-root.mjs` (reverted before commit) cross-referenced against the net-new project keys a full `npm test` run created — all 12 leaked keys traced to exactly these 3 files.
- `construct-edkj`: `construct sync` (and therefore `construct init`) left a stray empty `.cx/` directory in every consumer project, defeating the ADR-0074 single-`.construct/` consolidation. `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` hardcoded its sync lock and staging dir under `stateBase/.cx/` — two literals the phase-1b migration missed — which were created then cleaned each run, leaving the empty dir behind. They now resolve under `CONFIG_DIR_NAME` (`.construct/`). Regression coverage in `tests/sync-contract.test.mjs` (asserts no stray `.cx/` after sync); the repo-copy filter in `tests/mcp-manager.test.mjs` updated to exclude `.construct/` where the transient lock now lives.
- `construct-zsng`: VS Code flagged `construct.config.json`'s comments as invalid JSON. `construct-d1r7.4` made the config JSONC (with `//` comments), but the VS Code adapter sync never told the editor to treat it as JSONC, so every freshly-init'd consumer saw "Comments are not permitted in JSON" errors. `pinVscodeChatSettings` (`scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`) now writes a `files.associations` entry mapping `construct.config.json` and `construct.config.local.json` to `jsonc`, deep-merged so a user's own associations are preserved while other managed keys (e.g. `chat.agentFilesLocations`) keep their whole-key-when-unset behavior. Regression tests in `tests/vscode-mcp-toolkit-path.test.mjs`.

### Added

- `construct-d1r7.16`: release-gate coverage for the refit invariants, closing the d1r7 epic. `tests/functional/release-gate.functional.test.mjs` (run under `test:functional`, CI-enforced) now asserts three cross-cutting guarantees on HEAD: **no implicit active model defaults** — a clean install resolves every model tier to `null`/`not configured`, never a baked-in provider default (d1r7.5); **optional MCP silence** — catalog-only and installed-but-disabled servers raise no diagnostics, only an enabled server with an unresolved secret is actionable (d1r7.1–.3); and **certified document I/O** — when every export engine is present the certified matrix must pass (a format skipped for a missing tool is a hard failure), degrading to the graceful local matrix on a lean leg (d1r7.11). Cross-surface docs (document-io, config, custom-specialists/Org Studio, artifact completion states) were reconciled with the landed behavior across d1r7.9–.14.
- `construct-d1r7.12`: artifact completion ledger enforces exported-document *validity*, not just export. A standard document workflow no longer advances to completion on `exported` alone: after a successful export `runArtifactWorkflow` runs `validateExportedDocument` (new in `lib/export-validate.mjs` — composes structural integrity, content roundtrip where the format has a faithful text extractor, and local reference resolution) and records **`file-valid`** evidence per format; a missing validator tool degrades (records the miss, does not advance) while a real integrity/content/reference gap holds the format at `exported`. A per-format completion gate (`evaluateFormatCompletion`) checks each produced format against the required states the local workflow can produce (up to `file-valid`), reports states above that as host-owed *pending* rather than failures, and on a gap sets status `completion-incomplete` with a message naming the exact missing state and format. The `standard` gate default (`specialists/artifact-manifest.json` workflowDefaults + `lib/artifact-manifest.mjs`) now requires `["exported", "file-valid"]`, and the `export-validation` / `content-roundtrip` / `reference-integrity` gate categories flip from pending to **available** (`lib/artifact-gate-levels.mjs`) now that their checks are implemented. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/artifact-completion-file-valid.functional.test.mjs`; existing gate/quality-contract tests updated for the new default.
- `construct-d1r7.11`: certified document I/O matrix and engine profile (`lib/certification/document-io-matrix.mjs`). `construct certify document-io [--json] [--certified]` (npm: `npm run certify:document-io`) exports a realistic RichDocument fixture — table, figure with caption/alt over a real raster asset, code, callout, diagram — to every declared output format and validates each with the format's structural validator. Two modes over one matrix: **local** honours the existing graceful-degradation contract (a format whose engine is absent skips cleanly), while **certified** is the release contract — every declared format MUST have its engine (Pandoc, Typst, LibreOffice headless, pptxgenjs) present and MUST produce a file its validator accepts, so a format skipped for a missing tool is a *hard failure*, never a pass. Each result names the exact format, engine, and validation failure. Intake coverage is asserted by the existing fixture catalog. Contract tests in `tests/certification/document-io-matrix.test.mjs` (local-skips-vs-certified-fails simulated by stripping PATH).
- `construct-d1r7.10`: rich-media asset manifest and preservation pipeline (`lib/document-assets.mjs`). The manifest is *derived from* the RichDocument IR (ADR-0071), not a parallel structure: `buildAssetManifest(doc, { baseDir })` walks figure/media/diagram blocks (including those nested in lists and callouts) and records, per asset, its role (photo / screenshot / diagram), local-vs-remote ref, resolved absolute path, sha256 content hash, byte size, MIME type, dimensions, caption, alt text, source ref, and embed/link policy. Generated on import — `lib/document-ingest.mjs` builds it from the ingested content's RichDocument and writes a `<name>.assets.json` sidecar beside the markdown — and consumed on export: `exportRichDocument` now validates the manifest before any engine runs (`validateAssetManifest` fails closed on a broken local media ref, returning `brokenAssets` and writing no output) and rewrites local refs to absolute paths (`resolveDocAssets`) so a Pandoc/LibreOffice pass spawned in a temp directory still finds and embeds them — local raster assets inline as data URIs in self-contained HTML while captions and alt text survive to whatever the target format preserves. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/document-assets.functional.test.mjs` (raster photo, screenshot, diagram, remote ref, missing-asset failure, import-side generation).
- `construct-d1r7.9`: RichDocument (ADR-0071) export adapters — `lib/rich-document-export.mjs` serializes the IR to its canonical HTML and hands that HTML to the landed export engines rather than re-deriving a markdown pivot. `exportRichDocument({ doc, format, outputPath })` produces the full format set — self-contained `html`, the new `htmlfrag` copy/paste target (rich elements like merged cells, figure/caption, and callouts survive a clipboard paste), `pdf`, `docx`, `doc`, `odt`, the new `odp` (OpenDocument Presentation via LibreOffice from a pptxgenjs deck), `pptx`, `deck`, `rtf`, `epub`, `tex`, `txt`, and `md`/`mdx` from a new RichDocument→markdown writer. The landed `exportMarkdown` gained a backward-compatible `inputFormat: 'html'` path (ADR-0071 candidate site #4) so the HTML serialization drives Pandoc/Typst directly; `lib/libreoffice-export.mjs` generalized `convertDocxToDoc` into `convertViaLibreOffice` for arbitrary headless down-conversions. Per-format validation in `lib/export-validate.mjs` now covers Office/OpenDocument/EPUB zip structure and slide counts (`validateArchive`) and HTML DOM shape (`validateHtml`) alongside the existing PDF page-count and local-reference checks; a missing engine returns the same actionable diagnostic `detect()` reports and never a spurious certified pass. Functional coverage in `tests/functional/rich-document-export.functional.test.mjs`.
- Shadow-mode test-impact analysis infrastructure for PR CI. Introduces `--files-from=<path>` flag in `scripts/run-tests.mjs` that intersects a JSON-list of test files with the discovery set (applying the filter after `--exclude`, before `--shard`). Adds `scripts/shadow-lib.mjs` and `scripts/graph-impact-shadow.mjs` that compute the impacted test set via the living graph for each PR, run the full suite, and record which failures (if any) landed outside the impacted set as `.construct/shadow-impact.json` — never failing the build. Fail-open behavior: if the graph is stale, missing, or the diff touches graph-blind files (`.github/workflows/**`, `package-lock.json`, `scripts/ci/**`), the script reports `cannot_compute` rather than silently returning a wrong set. Non-blocking step wired into PR test job (runs on ubuntu-latest, shard 1 only).

### Changed

- Construct sync now treats optional MCP integrations as explicit opt-ins: generated host configs materialize only the Construct MCP server by default, leaving catalog/auth-only integrations such as GitHub or Context7 absent and silent until the user installs them. OpenCode sync no longer seeds an active root model, no longer backfills Construct model tiers with provider defaults, and strips legacy helper model pins so session title/summary/compaction follow user-owned OpenCode routing. Document ingest support now declares OpenDocument Presentation (`.odp`) and raster image inputs as high-fidelity/docling-backed formats with typed missing-engine diagnostics.

- `construct-d1r7.4`: `construct.config.json` (and its `.local` overlay) is now **JSONC**, authored like `tsconfig.json` — `//` and `/* */` comments and a trailing comma are tolerated via a shared `lib/jsonc.mjs` parser, wired through the central `loadProjectConfig` loader and the direct readers (`cost-ledger`, `scopes/loader`). `construct init` scaffolds the config with commented piped-option stubs (`deployment.mode`: `solo|team|enterprise`; `deployment.mcpBroker`: `auto|on|off`; a pointer that model tiers `fast|standard|reasoning` are set via `construct models set`/`CX_MODEL_*`). Commented stubs are stripped on load so none becomes a live value, and an invalid uncommented value still fails schema validation clearly. Documented in `docs/guides/reference/config.md`.

- `construct-d1r7.3`: `construct doctor` now distinguishes MCP state classes when diagnosing servers. A new pure classifier `classifyMcpState`/`diagnoseMcpStates` in `lib/mcp-manager.mjs` keeps catalog-only (not configured on any surface) and installed-but-disabled servers silent — they are optional integrations the user has not turned on — and raises an actionable diagnostic only for a server that is enabled on a managed surface but whose required secret is unresolved (`MCP <id> is enabled but missing <ENV>`). The `mcp-protocol` doctor watcher consumes it alongside its existing live-handshake probe (which already covers enabled-but-unhealthy servers). Functional coverage in `tests/functional/mcp-doctor-diagnostics.functional.test.mjs` exercises all four state classes.

### Docs

- `construct-1mjj` (ADR-0074, phase 4): swept ~638 user-facing `.cx` mentions to `.construct` across ~224 files — code comments, JSDoc/file headers, and log/error/help/template strings in `lib/**`, `bin/construct`, `scripts/**`, `docs/guides/**`, `docs/operations/**`, `docs/README.md`, and `templates/docs/**` — plus launcher prose repointed into `.construct/launcher/`. Home-scope `~/.cx`/`~/.construct`, dual-marker detection prose, legacy/migration wording, `cx-*` identifiers, ADRs under `docs/decisions/**`, and executable path/regex/comparison logic were deliberately left untouched. Also relaxed `lib/research-lint.mjs`'s source-cue heuristic to recognize both `.cx/` and `.construct/` document paths. `construct lint:comments` clean; CLI/doctor/status output tests green.

- `construct-d1r7.17`: corrected `docs/guides/concepts/opencode-config-ownership.md` to match the landed code. The OpenCode helper agents (`agent.title`/`summary`/`compaction`) are no longer "normalized onto a managed auxiliary model" — `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` now strips any model pin from them so OpenCode falls back to its own session-model/`small_model` routing (the doctrine, ownership table, and preservation sections all updated). Also documented `tui.json` `diff_style` (`auto` = side-by-side diff on wide terminals, `stacked` = single pane) as a user-owned setting Construct never writes.

### Added

- `construct-1mjj` (ADR-0074, phase 3): `legacy-layout-migration` reconciler (`lib/reconcile/legacy-layout-migration.mjs`) folds a pre-consolidation two-directory footprint into the single `.construct/` layout — it detects a legacy `.cx/` config directory and/or launcher files (`run.mjs`, `version`, `bootstrap.*`, `cache/`) sitting at `.construct/` top level, then `apply()` folds `.cx/` into `.construct/` (never clobbering newer `.construct/` state) and relocates the launcher into `.construct/launcher/`, leaving hook-command regeneration to `construct sync`. Registered in the ADR-0027 reconcile framework as `safety: 'ask'` so it never runs from the silent auto-sync path — `construct doctor` surfaces it and only `construct sync --reconcile=legacy-layout-migration` applies it (and only from a session not served by the launcher being moved). Idempotent; covered by `tests/functional/legacy-layout-migration.functional.test.mjs` (clean-layout detection, legacy detection, fold+relocate+idempotency, no-clobber). Still deferred: the physical relocation on the self-hosting Construct repo itself and the `construct sync` hook-command repoint, both of which must run outside a live session, plus the comprehensive prose/comment sweep.

### Changed

- `construct-1mjj` (ADR-0074, single project-directory consolidation): a host project now carries **one** Construct directory — `.construct/` — instead of two (`.construct/` launcher + `.cx/` config/state). The launcher is demoted to `.construct/launcher/`; config-layer and runtime markers (`context.md`/`context.json`, `workflow.json`, `org/`, intake, oracle, observations, knowledge, graph, demos, publish) sit at `.construct/` top-level. Delivered as centralize-then-flip so the rename is one line: all ~800 hardcoded directory references were first routed through a new `lib/config-dir.mjs` resolver (`CONFIG_DIR_NAME`, `LAUNCHER_REL_PATH`, `PROJECT_MARKERS`, `configPath`/`launcherPath`/`projectConfigDir`/`launcherDir`/`ignoredDirPatterns`) across four pure-refactor commits (phases 0–1c, `2b1f3fba`→`03b0117a`, unit suite unchanged at 4663/0), then the two resolver constants were flipped (`.cx`→`.construct`, `.construct`→`.construct/launcher`) in this commit. `PROJECT_MARKERS` still accepts a legacy `.cx/` marker during the transition window. Home-scope roots are untouched: `~/.construct/` (credentials, doctor state, ADR-0066 machine heavy-state under `~/.construct/projects/<key>/`) and the `~/.cx/` user telemetry fallback were never routed through the project resolver. The flip surfaced — and this commit fixes — several hardcoded `.cx` literals that path-construction conversion never reaches: validator regexes (`lib/demo-project.mjs` + `schemas/project-demo.schema.json` demo-script pattern), artifact-classification and no-fabrication/tool-invisibility regexes (`lib/artifact-type-from-path.mjs`, `lib/comment-lint.mjs`, `lib/research-lint.mjs`), the `docs-verify` stale-context matcher, the intake demo-source detector, display labels in `lib/service-manager.mjs`/`lib/init-update-guide.mjs`/`bin/construct` (doctor output), and a `walkDir` over-prune in `comment-lint`/`research-lint` that pruned `.construct` wholesale and would have silently stopped linting artifacts under `.construct/{knowledge,research,handoffs}` — all now derive from `CONFIG_DIR_NAME` so they cannot re-stale. Root `.gitignore` switches `.construct/` to `.construct/**` with `!.construct/org/` negations so custom specialists stay committable (mirrors the prior `.cx/org/` carve-out). `construct uninstall` redesigned for the single-dir layout: `.construct/launcher/` is auto-risk (removed on `--yes`), the rest of `.construct/` is ask-risk (removed with `--all`). ~155 test files migrated to the new layout. `construct doctor` 0 failed; full unit suite green; full functional suite green modulo pre-existing environmental cases (real `op` CLI, ambient provider keys, local `vhs` recorder). **Deferred to follow-up:** live-repo physical relocation of any existing `.cx/`→`.construct/` and `construct sync` hook-command regeneration (consumer migration + `construct doctor` legacy-layout detection), and the comprehensive prose/comment sweep of remaining user-facing `.cx` mentions.

### Fixed

- `construct-qn6e`: `construct init` no longer auto-starts the detached doctor/oracle daemons in automated contexts (`CI=true` or `NODE_ENV=test`). Previously every non-interactive init without `--no-start` spawned unref'd daemons that outlived the process and kept writing daemon state under `HOME`, leaking into test/CI runners. The start decision is now a pure exported resolver `resolveShouldStartServices({ args, interactive, env })` in `lib/init-unified.mjs`: `--no-start` always wins, `--auto-start` always forces a start (even in CI/test), and only the automated-context default flips to no-start — reusing the repo's standard CI/`NODE_ENV=test` wrong-context signal rather than a new skip env var. New functional test `tests/functional/init-no-daemons-in-test-context.functional.test.mjs` spawns real `construct init` under `NODE_ENV=test` in a sterile sandbox and asserts no `doctor.json`/oracle heartbeat is written, plus unit coverage of the resolver's flag precedence.

## [1.5.1] - 2026-07-07

### Fixed

- `construct-05u6` (GH #352): three consumer-install defects reproduced by packing the 1.5.0 tarball and installing it into a fresh `npm init` + `git init` project. (1) `lib/providers/directory/index.mjs` statically imported `minimatch`, which is not a declared dependency — it resolved in-repo only through devDependency transitives (eslint, glob, test-exclude), so every consumer install crashed with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on loading the directory provider. Per ADR-0001 (zero npm core) the provider now uses the existing in-tree `globToRegExp` from `lib/rules-delivery.mjs` with matchBase semantics (a slash-less pattern matches basenames), covering the documented pattern subset (`**/` spans directories, `*` stays within a segment); no new dependency.
