(2) `lib/init-unified.mjs` no longer imports `detectDockerCompose` from `lib/setup.mjs` — the function was removed in the Docker→LanceDB refactor (4956ca16) and `startServices()` stopped accepting `detectDockerComposeFn`, so every fresh `construct init` ended with "Services could not be started: detectDockerCompose is not a function"; services now start during init. (3) `construct graph build` crashed with `ENOENT: scandir .../node_modules/@geraldmaron/construct/tests` — the tarball ships `lib/` but not `tests/`, and `lib/test-corpus-inventory.mjs`'s `listTestFiles` walked the resolved root's `tests/` unguarded (its siblings `extractEmbedTestEdges`/`extractSecurityTestEdges` already guarded); the guard now lives in `listTestFiles` itself, so `extractCapabilityTestEdges` degrades to zero corpus edges in a consumer install and `graph build` completes (verified: 1149 nodes, 3043 edges in a clean consumer project). New gate: `tests/consumer/declared-imports.test.mjs` asserts every bare static import in `bin/` and `lib/` is declared in `dependencies`/`optionalDependencies`, so a dev-transitive can never again mask a missing dependency. Verified end to end: repacked tarball installed into a clean project — directory provider imports cleanly, `construct init` starts services, `construct doctor` 0 failed.

## [1.5.0] - 2026-07-07

### Added

- `construct-rf26.19` follow-up: re-verified the Bun-compiled binary end to end now that `construct-qvou`'s data-root fix is merged — `construct doctor` and a real demo flow (`sandbox create/list/delete`) both produce genuine, correct stdout on a compiled darwin-arm64 binary. Added a demo-flow smoke step to `.github/workflows/bun-binary-smoke.yml` alongside the existing doctor smoke, asserting on each subcommand's actual stdout, not just exit code. Added `bin/construct-shim.mjs`: the ADR-0064 npm-downloader-shim design (platform detection, cached/checksummed download, argv/exit-code-preserving exec, Node-CLI fallback) — not yet wired as `package.json`'s `bin` entry, since 5 existing install/acceptance tests assume synchronous, network-independent `node bin/construct` output; that cutover is a deliberate follow-up. Covered by `tests/functional/construct-shim.functional.test.mjs` (15 tests: cache hit/miss, checksum mismatch, network failure, override/fallback). Documented the full Bun-binary distribution track in `docs/operations/maintenance/release-and-deploy.md`. Still unverified in this environment: a real GitHub Actions run, `install.sh` against a real GitHub Release/clean VM, a real `npm install -g`, and Linux binary execution (compile-only).

### Changed

- `construct-rf26.11`: consolidated the 29-specialist roster to 12 (`cx-orchestrator` + 11 workers: `cx-architect`, `cx-data-analyst`, `cx-debugger`, `cx-designer`, `cx-engineer`, `cx-operations`, `cx-product-manager`, `cx-qa`, `cx-researcher`, `cx-reviewer`, `cx-security`). 15 specialists folded into a surviving anchor as skill-bundle modes (e.g. `cx-devil-advocate`→`cx-reviewer`'s plan-challenge mode, `cx-legal-compliance`→`cx-security`'s compliance-review mode, `cx-business-strategist`→`cx-product-manager`'s strategic-framing mode); `cx-oracle` and `cx-rd-lead` retired outright, absorbed into `cx-orchestrator`'s fleet-health synthesis (new skill `skills/operating/fleet-health-routing.md`) and `cx-architect`'s pre-architecture framing gate respectively. Contracts (43→35), teams, and groups under `specialists/org/` updated to match; `lib/orchestration/flow-selection.mjs` and `lib/orchestration/gates.mjs` (post-`rf26.10`-split) remap retired specialist ids to their anchor at every dispatch site. `lib/roles/flavor-bindings.mjs`, `lib/prompt-composer.js`, `lib/certification/skill-inventory.mjs`, `lib/registry/consolidation.mjs` gained fallback lookup since `cx-engineer`/`cx-operations`/`cx-reviewer`/`cx-researcher` now each carry multiple flavor bindings under their absorbed roles' names. Full mapping, contract-graph rationale, and the resolution of 7 flagged-ambiguous cases (business-strategist home, data-analyst/debugger standalone status, rd-lead/oracle retirement targets, docs-keeper placement, researcher model tier) recorded in `docs/decisions/adr/appendix-0065-roster-mapping.md`. Verified: `construct sync` clean (12 agents + 30 commands + 153 skills); 299/299 specialist/registry/contract/orchestration tests + 13/13 certification tests pass; `construct doctor` 0 failed; dead-code audit clean.
- `construct-rf26.10` (split monoliths along flow boundaries): pure refactor, no behavior change. `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs` (1104 lines, mixed classification/gates/chain-resolution) is now an 87-line re-export layer over four new modules — `lib/orchestration/policy-constants.mjs` (enums), `classification.mjs` (intent/work-category/risk-flag/flavor classifiers), `gates.mjs` (research/framing/executive-approval gates + team routing), `flow-selection.mjs` (specialist selection + `routeRequest`/`routeRequestVerified`/`buildConstructToOrchestratorPacket`) — kept as a single re-export surface rather than updating the ~25 existing import sites. `lib/mcp/server.mjs` (2027 lines) drops to 553: its ~1300-line hardcoded tool-schema catalog moved to `lib/mcp/tool-definitions.mjs` (split further into `tool-definitions-{project,skills,memory,workflow}.mjs` by capability, 80 tool defs preserved exactly), and its CallTool request envelope (identity, rate limiting, the destructive gate, tracing, timeout, audit logging) moved to `lib/mcp/dispatch-envelope.mjs`. Five tests that statically parsed the old monoliths' source text were updated to read the new module locations. Verified: export surface byte-identical on both files; full unit suite unchanged (4646/4624/7, same pre-existing failures); full functional suite 895/890/2 (both pre-existing); dead-code audit clean (0 unreferenced).

### Added

- `construct-rf26.9` follow-up: deleted the chain-injection remnants the prior pass on this bead deliberately left blocking. `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs`'s `buildDispatchPlan`/`buildDispatchSummary` and the `route.dispatchPlan`/`route.dispatchSummary` string fields are gone — no compat flag. Sequencing now lives entirely in the flow engine (`lib/orchestration/delegation-flow.mjs`); `personas/construct.md` was already dispatching through `orchestration_delegation_next` instead of reading these fields. `specialists/org/contracts/user-to-construct.json`'s `output.mustContain` drops `"dispatchPlan"`. `lib/workflow-state.mjs`'s `buildTaskPacketFromIntent` no longer stores `dispatchPlan` on a task's source metadata. `lib/artifact-loop-core.mjs` needed no change — its `dispatchSummary` field is unrelated ad hoc text for the artifact-authoring loop, confirmed via call-graph grep. Tests: `tests/orchestration-policy.test.mjs` rewritten against flow selection (`buildDelegationFlow`/`dispatchReasons`); full suite unchanged at the pre-existing failure baseline.
- Shared machine-wide docling venv (`construct-rf26.16`, ADR-0068): the docling venv, formerly resolved per-project via `resolveStateDir(cwd, 'runtime', 'docling')` (`~/.construct/projects/<key>/runtime/docling/`, one venv per project — the intermediate state left by the prior state-root migration), now resolves through a new `resolveSharedRuntimeDir` (`lib/state-root.mjs`) directly under `~/.construct/runtime/docling/` (or `$CX_TOOLKIT_DIR/runtime/docling/`) — not project-keyed at all, so every project on one machine shares exactly one provisioned venv, per ADR-0066's stated terminal shape for this bead. `lib/runtime/uv-bootstrap.mjs`'s `defaultRuntimeDir()` and `lib/ingest-tooling.mjs`'s `doclingVenvPath()` both dropped their now-meaningless `cwd` parameter. A version-pin mismatch against `.install-marker.json` (the lockfile-equivalent) now explicitly clears the stale venv directory before re-provisioning, a controlled re-provision rather than trusting `uv venv` to reconcile an existing directory left by a different pin. New ADR-0068 formalizes the ingestion sidecar process contract this venv change is part of: one JSON-RPC surface (already true of `docling-sidecar.py`), lazy provisioning only on first extraction (confirmed `lib/init-unified.mjs` provisions nothing), a parent-PID watch added to `docling-sidecar.py` (a background thread exits the sidecar if `os.getppid()` changes, a second line of defense alongside the existing Node-side `process.on('exit')` kill, for the `SIGKILL` case neither exit handler runs for), the existing `construct doctor` docling health entry (`describeDoclingRuntime`, already an optional/non-fatal finding), and no business logic in the sidecar (unchanged — routing/fallback/degradation stay Node-side). Left `lib/runtime/whisper-bootstrap.mjs` on its existing per-project scheme; the bead named docling only. Also left `lib/ingest/sidecar-providers.mjs`/`lib/extensions/manifests/docling.manifest.json`'s separate, currently-unwired-into-`construct doctor` install-probe path untouched — its `defaultRuntimeDir` field already predates and is now further out of sync with this change, but rewiring that dead code path is a distinct, larger change outside this bead's scope (documented as a known gap in ADR-0068's Consequences). Updated the two docs mentions of docling's provisioned path (`docs/guides/reference/document-io.md`, `docs/guides/concepts/knowledge-layout.md`). Tests: `tests/functional/docling-shared-venv.functional.test.mjs` (new — two isolated git-project fixtures with distinct `deriveProjectKey`s both resolve `doclingVenvPath()` to the identical path, and a second project reuses a first project's provisioned venv rather than re-provisioning), `tests/functional/ingest-tooling.functional.test.mjs` (docling-venv fixture updated to `doclingVenvPath()`'s new no-argument, machine-shared shape, each docling-touching test given its own isolated `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` since the venv is no longer scoped by fixture root), `tests/functional/mcp-ingest-resilience.functional.test.mjs` (sandbox stub venv path updated to the shared, non-project-keyed layout).
- `construct-rf26.17`: the machine-scoped LanceDB vector index and the ONNX embedding model now provision lazily instead of eagerly, and the machine-scoped observations store gets a real TTL/size eviction policy. `construct init` no longer calls `syncFileStateToSql` automatically — vector-index seeding from a project's existing docs is now opt-in via `--seed-index` (`lib/init-unified.mjs`), so a fresh init touches neither the ONNX model cache dir nor LanceDB. `construct install`'s embedding-model warmup and the LanceDB store-dir pre-creation are similarly gated behind a new `--with-embeddings` flag (`lib/setup.mjs`), mirroring the existing `--with-docling` pattern — both provision on first real use (`construct ingest`, first observation store/search) when the flag is omitted. `construct doctor`'s "Vector backend (LanceDB) reachable" check no longer connects to (and thereby creates) the index on every run in every project; it now checks for the index directory first and reports "not yet provisioned (lazy)" when absent. For the observations store: `lib/storage/vector-client.mjs`'s `VectorClient.pruneObservations({ maxAgeDays, maxRows })` evicts rows from `observations_v1` past an age cutoff and/or beyond a row-count cap without ever creating the table as a side effect of checking it; `lib/storage/admin.mjs`'s `purgeExpiredData()` (previously a stub always returning `{ purged: 0 }`, already called by `lib/mcp/tools/storage.mjs`'s `storageSync` on every `construct ingest`) now resolves the caps from `construct.config.json`'s new `resources.disk.observationsMaxDays` (default 90) / `observationsMaxRows` (default 5000) — env var (`CONSTRUCT_OBSERVATIONS_MAX_DAYS`/`CONSTRUCT_OBSERVATIONS_MAX_ROWS`) over config over default, matching `resolveSetting`'s existing precedence rule — and is a no-op when the vector index was never provisioned. `construct doctor` and `construct prune` both surface the result (current size, row count, oldest retained entry, entries evicted this run). Tests: `tests/observation-retention.test.mjs` (eviction by age, eviction by row-count cap, no-op-when-unprovisioned, end-to-end `purgeExpiredData`), `tests/functional/init-lazy-embedding.functional.test.mjs` (fresh init populates neither the embedding cache dir nor any project's `lancedb/` dir; `--seed-index` opts back into both, confirming the assertions aren't vacuous).
- Comment-lint now bans naming another software project (`CrewAI`, `LangGraph`, `AutoGen`, `LangChain`, `MetaGPT`, `Aider`, `Devin`) inside a code comment (`lib/comment-lint.mjs`'s `checkExternalProjectNames`/`BANNED_EXTERNAL_PROJECTS`) — a prior-art comparison is unverifiable narration unless it carries a citation, and only `docs/decisions/**`/`docs/notes/**` carry one, so those two paths are exempt (markdown/MDX prose is out of scope entirely — the check's comment-detection regex false-positives on markdown's own `**bold**`/`* bullet` syntax, matching `checkBanned`'s existing precedent). Swept `lib/`, `skills/`, `personas/`, `specialists/prompts/`, `rules/`, `templates/` plus a repo-wide `lint:comments` run and fixed the two real hits found: `lib/storage/shared-memory.mjs`'s header dropped a "per the LangGraph thread-vs-store split" aside in favor of describing the boundary directly, and `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` reworded two "aider architect/editor split" comments to "cheap-editor/capable-architect split" describing the same local/cloud model split without naming the external tool. Tests: `tests/comment-lint.test.mjs`.
- Deterministic flow engine core (ADR-0067, `lib/flows/`): a zero-dependency typed-state engine — flow definitions (`defineFlow`/`loadFlow`) validate their state schema, step graph, and fan-out rules at load time rather than at run time; `runStep`/`advanceRun`/`runFlow` execute steps and route between them via pure, synchronous routers with and/or join combinators (`andJoin`/`anyJoin`). A step's `workerBackend` field records which execution backend (`inline`/`provider`/`host`) it would delegate to without the engine calling it directly — this module sequences, the existing orchestration runtime still executes. Fan-out is rejected at load time unless every branch is `readOnly` and a `synthesis` step is named, per ADR-0067's evidence-based restriction. A step's per-run usage against its declared budget produces a typed `budget-exhausted` result rather than a silent truncation. The `Run` shape (state + frontier + join progress + history) is deliberately plain and clonable so checkpoint/resume (tracked separately) can persist it without an engine rewrite. Tests: `tests/flows-schema.test.mjs`, `tests/flows-state.test.mjs`, `tests/flows-define.test.mjs`, `tests/flows-engine.test.mjs`, `tests/flows-demo.test.mjs` (28 tests).
- `construct-rf26.7` (flow checkpoint/resume, ADR-0067 + ADR-0066): `lib/flows/checkpoint.mjs` persists a run after every completed step to the project's machine-scoped state root (`runtime/flows/runs/<runId>.json`), atomic write via temp-file-then-rename — the same pattern `lib/orchestration/run-store.mjs` already uses for orchestration runs. `resumeRun`/`startRun`/`tickCheckpointed`/`runCheckpointed` reconstruct a `Run`'s Set/Map fields (`completed`, `joinProgress`, `usage`) from the persisted plain-array shape; a checkpoint records the flow's `id` and throws `FlowCheckpointError` on resume against a mismatched flow rather than silently reinterpreting history against the wrong step graph. Idempotent re-entry is a property of the `Run` shape itself (a completed step is never back in the frontier a checkpoint holds) plus an authoring convention documented in `docs/guides/concepts/flow-authoring.md`: a step's `run()` must be idempotent under at-least-once execution, since the one open window (a crash between a step executing and its checkpoint write landing) isn't closed by the engine. New CLI: `construct flow resume <run-id> --flow=<path> [--state=<json>]` (starts or resumes and drives to completion) and `construct flow status <run-id>` (reads a checkpoint without driving it). Tests: `tests/flows-checkpoint.test.mjs` (7 tests), `tests/functional/flow-checkpoint-resume.functional.test.mjs` (kills a run mid-flow via an in-process tick, resumes it to completion through the real CLI binary in an isolated tmpdir, and confirms the already-checkpointed step's side effect was not re-applied).
- `construct-rf26.8` (effort budgets and parallelism policy, ADR-0067/ADR-0065): verified already satisfied by the flow engine core landed for `construct-rf26.7`'s prerequisite work, with no further code change needed — `defineFlow` already rejects a `fanOut: true` step that isn't also `readOnly: true` (`tests/flows-define.test.mjs`'s "defineFlow rejects a mutating step declaring fanOut"), budget exhaustion already surfaces as a typed `STEP_STATUS.BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` result rather than a silent truncation (`tests/flows-engine.test.mjs`'s "budget exhaustion produces a typed budget-exhausted result"), and `docs/guides/concepts/flow-authoring.md` already documents both. Confirmed each acceptance criterion against the actual test/doc content rather than assuming.
- `construct-rf26.9` (port orchestration-policy contract chains onto flows, ADR-0067, HIGH BLAST RADIUS): `lib/orchestration/delegation-flow.mjs` converts a resolved `routeRequest()` route into a flow definition (`buildDelegationFlow`) — one step per specialist in `route.specialists`' order (mirroring the same per-task delegation shape `lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs`'s `buildTasks()` already computes, not a new invented shape) — and drives it one checkpointed step at a time (`advanceDelegation`, built on `construct-rf26.7`'s `tickCheckpointed`). A caller (the new self-registered MCP tool `orchestration_delegation_next`, or the CLI/library directly) receives only the current specialist's delegation — `role`/`reason`/`handoffContract` — never the rest of the chain, and resumes correctly across separate calls/process restarts by reusing the same `run_id`. `personas/construct.md` now instructs dispatch through `orchestration_delegation_next` one step at a time instead of self-sequencing off a remembered dispatch plan (`construct sync` re-run; no other platform file changed). Classification itself (`classifyIntent`, `detectRiskFlags`, `selectSpecialists`, the full routing logic in `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs`) is untouched and still selects the chain deterministically. Tests: `tests/orchestration-delegation-flow.test.mjs` (6 tests: specialist ordering parity with `routeRequest` for a focused fix route and an orchestrated feature-build route, one-step-at-a-time surfacing, immediate-track/no-specialists handling, and checkpoint/resume across a simulated crash). Before/after parity: the full pre-existing `tests/orchestration-policy*.test.mjs` suite (94 tests across 4 files) passes unmodified — nothing in the existing classification/dispatch surface changed or regressed. **Scope note, reported rather than forced**: `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs`'s `dispatchPlan`/`dispatchSummary` string fields (and `buildDispatchPlan`/`buildDispatchSummary`) were deliberately NOT deleted. `dispatchPlan` is a `mustContain` field in `specialists/org/contracts/user-to-construct.json` (off-limits per this work's constraints) and both fields are consumed by `lib/workflow-state.mjs` and `lib/artifact-loop-core.mjs` plus their tests (`tests/concierge-routing.test.mjs`, `tests/prompt-composer.test.mjs`) outside this port's stated scope — deleting them outright would require editing a protected contract schema and cascade through files this work was told not to touch. The flow-based delegation path above was built additively alongside the existing fields rather than forcing a lossy deletion; full removal of `dispatchPlan`/`dispatchSummary` is left for a follow-up that has license to edit `specialists/org/**`.
- Host-subscription execution seat (ADR-0063): a third orchestration worker backend, `host`, materializes each specialist's prompt without spending any provider API credits — the calling MCP host executes it in its own model session (the subscription it is already running under) instead. `orchestration_run` now defaults to `host` for MCP-originated runs when no explicit `worker_backend`/`construct.config.json` setting is present; the CLI's own default (`inline`) is unchanged, and `construct orchestrate run --worker-backend=host` (no attached MCP host) fails loud instead of producing a run stranded with nobody to execute it. A materialized run stands at the new, real, non-terminal `awaiting-host` status until every task's result is submitted via the new self-registered MCP tool `orchestration_task_result`, which drives the run to the same honest `completed`/`completed-with-failures` terminal taxonomy every other backend uses; every host-reported field is tagged `provenanceSource: 'host-reported'`, never presented as a construct-verified provider execution. When the connected MCP client declares the `sampling` capability, construct-mcp instead drives the same loop itself via `sampling/createMessage` (`orchestration.hostExecution: auto|pickup|sampling`), completing the run in a single call. The `orchestration-runs` doctor watcher flags an abandoned `awaiting-host` run (default 30 minutes with no submitted result) as an advisory finding. Tests: `tests/orchestration-worker.test.mjs`, `tests/orchestration-runtime.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/host-execution-pickup.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/host-execution-sampling.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/honest-terminal-states.functional.test.mjs`.

- `construct-rf26.21` (docs refit — architecture, strategy, ADR index): grounded the refit's documentation in the 12 of 22 `construct-rf26.*` beads actually closed (git log + `bd show`), not just `plan.md`'s plan. `docs/guides/concepts/architecture.mdx` gains a `## Flow engine` section (previously undocumented despite `lib/flows/` landing in `construct-rf26.6`–`.9`): `defineFlow`/`runFlow`, the read-only+synthesis fan-out restriction (ADR-0065), checkpoint/resume (`lib/flows/checkpoint.mjs`, ADR-0066), and the additive port of contract-chain dispatch onto flows via `orchestration_delegation_next` (`construct-rf26.9`, explicitly noted as not-yet-complete — `dispatchPlan`/`dispatchSummary` remain live). The "Contract chain" gate description and "Agent contracts" bullet now note the `executable`/`advisory` postcondition classification and 39/111 coverage (`construct-rf26.12`). The specialist-roster section now points at the ADR-0065 roster-mapping appendix (`construct-rf26.11`, a proposal pending sign-off — the built-in roster is still all 29). `STRATEGY.md` gets a `## 2026-07 architecture refit` section (D1–D4, phase status, 12/22 beads closed), a new "Bet 6: Orchestrator-worker over a fixed role-crew org," amendments to Bet 1 and Bet 2 tying them to ADR-0067/ADR-0066, and "Current state" bullets for the flow engine, machine-scoped state root, shared docling venv, and custom specialist/team authoring (landed) alongside honest "partial" notes for the roster consolidation, the additive delegation-flow port, and the Bun-binary distribution gap. `docs/decisions/index.md`'s stale "Latest: ADR-0048" pointer (ten ADRs behind) now points at ADR-0068 and lists the refit's four ADRs (0064–0067) with their supersession targets.

### Dependencies

- Applied the dependabot minor-patch group (supersedes #343): `@lancedb/lancedb` 0.31.0, `eslint` 10.6.0, `globals` 17.7.0, `next`/`@next/mdx` 16.2.10, `mermaid` 11.16.0, `tailwindcss`/`@tailwindcss/postcss` 4.3.2, `autoprefixer` 10.5.2, `postcss` 8.5.16. LanceDB 0.31's two breaking changes (`IndexStatistics.loss` removal, repeated-`.where()` AND semantics) verified unused in this codebase. Also `@types/node` 26.1.0 in `apps/docs` (supersedes #346); the docs app type-checks clean against the new types.
- Bumped pinned GitHub Action SHAs (supersedes #338–#342): `actions/checkout` v7.0.0, `actions/setup-node` v6.4.0, `actions/upload-artifact` v7.0.1, `actions/setup-go` v6.5.0, `dorny/paths-filter` v4.0.2 — node24-runtime majors; every invocation input this repo passes survives unchanged, verified per action against its changelog.
- Deferred, each with a documented migration path: `js-yaml` 5.x (#347 — v5 removes the ESM default export that all 10 of this repo's import sites use, and throws on empty/comment-only input where v4 returned `undefined`, silently changing empty-frontmatter semantics behind try/catch; needs a coordinated namespace-import + empty-input-guard migration) and `@ai-sdk/anthropic` 4.x / `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible` 3.x (#344/#345 — provider-spec-v4 packages that pair with `ai@7`; this repo pins `ai@^6`, so all three `ai`/`@ai-sdk/*` majors must move together).

### Fixed

- Systemic `ENOTEMPTY` cleanup-race flakiness in CI (`construct-nl9f`): all 383 tmpdir-teardown `rmSync` call sites across `tests/functional/` and `tests/acceptance/` (182 files) now use the new `tests/helpers/cleanup.mjs`'s `rmTmpDir`, which retries generously (including one full re-walk, since `rmSync`'s own `maxRetries` only retries the single failed rmdir) and never fails an already-green test on teardown of an OS tmpdir — while refusing loudly to remove any path outside `os.tmpdir()`, so a mis-swept repo/HOME path can't vanish silently; real host state remains guarded by the sterile-host-env fingerprint. Also removed 8 acceptance cleanup-subtest assertions that re-failed the identical race as an assertion. Root-caused the deeper hazard separately: non-interactive `construct init` auto-starts detached doctor/oracle daemons that keep writing into a sandbox HOME after init exits — tracked as `construct-qn6e` (23 test files spawn init without `--no-start`; `CONSTRUCT_DOCTOR=off`/`CONSTRUCT_ORACLE=off` are the cheap mitigation). Verified: the 5 confirmed-flaky files 5×-stress-looped with zero failures; 27-file broad sample green; eslint at exact baseline parity.
- CI PR matrix trimmed to ubuntu × Node 20/22 (min + max supported); the macOS test legs now run on main push/schedule/dispatch only — macOS runners are an order of magnitude more expensive, were the flakiest legs, and their per-PR signal is largely redundant with `bun-binary-smoke`'s darwin-arm64 build+doctor+demo job that already runs on every PR. A macOS-only regression still gates the release path (main push runs the full matrix; `release.yml` builds darwin SEA binaries).
- Fixed 4 pre-existing CI gaps shared by `staging` and every integration branch off it: the `test` job never built the living graph or seeded the embedded vector index before running tests that read them for real (`tests/graph/*`, `tests/security/owasp-coverage`, the lancedb acceptance leg) — added `construct graph build` and a direct `syncFileStateToSql` call (the function `construct init --seed-index` uses, called narrowly instead of full `init` to avoid rewriting tracked files in the shared CI job); apt's older Ubuntu pandoc emitted a typst call (`blockquote`) our custom PDF template didn't define — added a compat shim forwarding to the `quote(block: true)` show-rule already handling newer pandoc's output, version-agnostic rather than pinning CI's pandoc; `lib/render-visual-check.mjs`'s `checkRenderedArtifact()` was missing a `failures` array on 4 of 5 typed-failure branches, crashing any caller reading `.failures` on those paths; `lib/orchestration/readiness.mjs` silently dropped its server-self-report caveat whenever model resolution was also unresolved (an unconditional later overwrite of `detail`, only reachable on a fully credential-less host) and incorrectly gated `webMode=governed` on LLM provider resolution when `resolveWebCapability` already checks `WEB_SEARCH_URL` independently; and 4 test helpers (`tests/functional/mcp-dynamic-tool-registration.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/providers/breaker-status.test.mjs`, `tests/doctor/oracle-liveness-watcher.test.mjs`, `tests/audit/f05-runtime-ownership/non-owner-refusal.test.mjs`) deleted their temp fixtures / restored mocked globals before their async callbacks had finished using them — the last one restored the real `process.kill` before `stopServices()`'s internal awaits resolved, undermining the one guarantee that fixture exists to prove. Verified end-to-end on a truly clean checkout (`git clone`, not a working-tree copy — the latter silently carries over gitignored `.cx/` state and produces false passes).
- `construct-mtgs` (wave 2): pinned `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` across the remaining 40 catalogued CLI-spawning test files (acceptance suite, providers, packs, ~20 `tests/functional/*` files) — a different leak class than wave 1's 20 in-process files: each of these spawns the real `construct` binary as a subprocess, so a module-level `process.env` pin doesn't reach the child; fixed by threading a per-file `mkdtemp`'d sandbox as both `HOME` and `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` into every `spawnSync`/`execFileSync`/`spawn` call's env. Confirmed 3 concrete live leaks pre-fix: `postinstall-mutation-manifest.test.mjs` (postinstall shells to `sync-specialists.mjs`), `acp-server.functional.test.mjs`'s in-process `runAcpServer` call, `certification-runner.functional.test.mjs`. Verified via `tests/helpers/sterile-host-env.mjs`'s real-host fingerprint guard, individually and in joint runs. `release-gate.functional.test.mjs`'s one remaining failure is a confirmed pre-existing worktree-only `core.hooksPath` artifact, reproduces identically on an unmodified checkout. This closes out the full 33–40-file catalogue left by wave 1; a true whole-suite `npm test` confirmation of the bead's end-to-end acceptance criterion (zero drift in the real `~/.construct/projects/`) remains open, since this session's concurrent sibling worktree-agents make that signal unreliable as a single-session check — bead stays in_progress.
- `construct-rf26.14`: the 4 curated scope profiles and the profile-lifecycle doc/CLI referenced specialists retired by `construct-rf26.11` (`cx-ux-researcher`, `cx-evaluator`, `cx-trace-reviewer`, `cx-docs-keeper`). Repointed to their roster anchors (`cx-researcher`, `cx-reviewer`, `cx-operations`); trimmed the lifecycle's "invent up to 80 roles/12 departments" ceremony (Stage 3) to "select skill emphasis over the fixed 12-role roster"; populated the 4 profiles' previously-always-empty `defaultSkills` (the profile-level skill-entitlement baseline in `lib/skills/router.mjs` was inert without them). Named custom scopes now reuse the `construct-rf26.13` config-layer precedence (builtin → user `~/.construct/org/scopes/` → project `.cx/org/scopes/`) instead of a scope-specific mechanism — `construct scope set <id>` can switch to a named custom profile the same way it switches to a curated one; the pre-existing single-file `.cx/scope.json` anonymous escape hatch is unchanged. Fixed a broken doc-path reference in `bin/construct`'s `scope create` CLI text. Tests: 4 new precedence tests in `tests/profiles/loader.test.mjs`; `tests/functional/profile-lifecycle.functional.test.mjs` updated to the new anchor ids. Verified: `lint:scopes` clean (4 scopes, 53 overlays); `construct doctor` 0 failed.
- `construct-y4iv`: `lib/roles/gateway.mjs`'s `createBdIncident()` called the real `runBd` (`lib/beads-client.mjs`) unconditionally, so any test reaching the escalation path (severity-immediate events with no kill switch) filed a genuine duplicate issue into the shared bd/dolt database regardless of `CONSTRUCT_ROLES_ROOT` test isolation — that env var only isolated the dedup-fingerprint files, not the actual bd write. Confirmed as the root cause of 9 real noise issues auto-filed during this session's heavy concurrent-worktree test activity (5x identical `config.protection.violation` fingerprint, 4x identical `service.down` fingerprint — both closed as duplicates, referencing this fix). Threaded an optional `{ runBd }` override through `tryEscalate`/`recordAndMaybeInvoke`/`processBacklog`, defaulting to the real client so every existing caller — including `lib/oracle/execute.mjs`, which legitimately sets `CONSTRUCT_ROLES_ROOT` in production — is unaffected; ruled out gating on `CONSTRUCT_ROLES_ROOT` itself for exactly that reason. Matches the existing DI convention used elsewhere (`lib/beads/auto-close.mjs`'s `{ runner }`, `lib/providers/copilot-auth.mjs`'s `{ fetchImpl }`). New regression test in `tests/roles/gateway.test.mjs` drives the full severity-immediate escalation path and asserts the injected fake is called exactly once while the real bd client never runs. Verified: `bd list --status open` count held steady across the fix's own test runs (no new leaked issues).
- Fixed the Bun-compiled binary track's `bin/construct` producing zero output on every command (`construct-qvou`). Two empirically-verified causes: Bun's `--compile` bundler silently skips an extensionless entry's imports entirely (`bin/construct` has no extension) — `scripts/build-binary.mjs` now compiles from a temporary `.mjs`-suffixed copy placed next to the real entry, removed after the build. Separately, every bundled module's `import.meta.url`/`process.argv[1]` collapses to the same virtual `/$bunfs/root` path, which broke both install-root resolution (`bin/construct` plus 4 `lib/*.mjs` modules with their own local root constants) and the standard "was I run directly" script guard used by ~19 `lib/*.mjs` files — each ran its own top-level CLI logic as a side effect of merely being imported, and `lib/headhunt.mjs`'s bare invocation crashed the whole binary before real command dispatch ran. `lib/roots.mjs` gains `resolveInstallRoot()` (falls back to `process.execPath`'s real directory under `/$bunfs`) and `isMainModule()` (always false under Bun-compile); all affected files route through these instead of duplicating the logic. `construct version`/`--help`/`doctor` now produce real output on a compiled macOS arm64 binary; `.github/workflows/bun-binary-smoke.yml`'s `continue-on-error` removed. Cross-compiled Linux x64/arm64 and macOS x64 targets remain unexecuted (no non-arm64-macOS host available). Tests: `tests/roots.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/bun-compiled-binary.functional.test.mjs` (gated on Bun being on PATH).
- test-hygiene: pinned `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` (module-level, throwaway `mkdtemp` sandbox) across 20 orchestration/embed/intake test files that resolved the machine-scoped state root (ADR-0066) without HOME isolation, which leaked real project-key directories into the developer machine's `~/.construct/projects/` on every run (`construct-mtgs`). Root cause in every case: `lib/paths.mjs`'s `homeDir()`/`constructDir()` read `process.env.CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` directly, never any `env` options object a test threaded through its own constructor args. Confirmed the leak concretely before fixing `tests/orchestration/execution-state.test.mjs` (6 new project-key directories appeared under the real `~/.construct/projects/` from one unfixed run). 160/160 tests pass after the fix. 33 additional CLI-spawning test files with the same gap are catalogued on the bead for follow-up; it stays in_progress.
- `construct-1xlz`: `bin/construct`'s `orchestrate run` subcommand built `request.workerBackend` from `--worker-backend` but called `runOrchestration(request, {})`/`planRun(request, {})` with an empty opts object — `executeRun` resolves the backend from `opts.workerBackend` first, so an explicit `--worker-backend` override was silently coupled to the incidental `run.workerBackend` persisted-fallback rather than reaching the engine directly. Now builds `runOpts` from the CLI flag and threads it into both calls. New test in `tests/functional/orchestration-mode-a.functional.test.mjs` spawns the real binary with `--worker-backend provider` and asserts every task's `executor` shows provider execution was actually attempted (`provider:error`), not just that the run's label says "provider".
- `construct-rf26.18`: closed a split-brain gap ADR-0066's writer migration left behind — `lib/orchestration/run-store.mjs` already writes orchestration runs to the machine-scoped state root, but three readers still duplicated the old project-relative `.cx/runtime/orchestration/runs/` path independently of it: `lib/status.mjs` (`countPersonaDegradedRuns`, `summarizeRecentRunExecutionStates`, `summarizeGraphWorkflows`, backing `construct status`), `lib/graph/runtime-evidence.mjs` (backing `construct status`'s workflow classification and the `graph explain`/`graph build`/`graph gap` CLI's execution-staleness dimension), and the LanceDB health check in both `lib/status.mjs`'s `probeStorageHealth` and `lib/storage/admin.mjs`'s `getStorageStatus` (checked `.cx/lancedb` directly while `resetStorage`/`vector-client.mjs` already resolved the real store through the state root). Each now resolves through `lib/state-root.mjs` (via `lib/orchestration/run-store.mjs`'s exported `runtimeDir()` for runs, `resolveStateDir` for lancedb), so `construct status` reports real data instead of silently always reporting zero/absent runs and a permanently-degraded vector store. Also verified `construct init` (unchanged — it never scaffolded heavy state), doctor's legacy-heavy-state watcher, and `construct sync` (`scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`, `lib/reconcile/*`) against the new layout, and rewrote the disposition table (`docs/guides/concepts/project-scopes.md`, `lib/host-disposition.mjs`'s header) to describe the accurate three-way split: config-layer (committed text) / machine-scoped heavy state (`~/.construct/projects/<key>/`) / beads (project-local exception). Tests: `tests/status.test.mjs`, `tests/orchestration/persona-degraded.test.mjs`, `tests/graph/evidence.test.mjs`, `tests/status-storage-probe.test.mjs`, `tests/storage-health-probe.test.mjs`, `tests/storage-admin-status-probe.test.mjs` (fixtures updated to write through the real state-root resolvers rather than the stale path), plus a new `construct status` case in `tests/functional/state-root-footprint.functional.test.mjs` pinning the regression end-to-end via the real CLI.
- ADR-0066 (machine-scoped state root) left 14 test files resolving trace/run-record/docling-venv state through the old project-relative `.cx/...` path, or reading it back in-process without pinning `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` around the call the way `lib/paths.mjs`'s `homeDir()`/`constructDir()` require — since those two functions read `process.env.CX_HOME_OVERRIDE`/`CX_TOOLKIT_DIR` directly rather than any `{ env }` option threaded through a function's own parameters, a subprocess-isolated sandbox's HOME is invisible to the parent test process unless the same override is pinned there too. Symptoms ranged from false assertion failures (stale `.cx/traces`/`.cx/runtime/orchestration/runs` expectations, a docling-venv fixture provisioned at the pre-migration path) to live leaks writing real trace/run/lancedb state into the developer machine's `~/.construct/projects/`. Fixed each file with whichever pattern fit its shape: a module-level `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` pin (restored via `test.after`) for a file sharing one implicit home throughout, or a per-sandbox pin/restore helper for a file creating multiple distinct sandboxes; `tests/functional/project-root-isolation.functional.test.mjs`'s expectation was itself stale and now asserts the real state-root shape via `resolveStateDir` instead of the old literal path. Separately, `tests/functional/claude-orchestration-prompt.functional.test.mjs` (plus the same anti-pattern in `tests/functional/opencode-local-mcp-trim.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/opencode-mcp-trim-scoping.functional.test.mjs`, and `tests/functional/orchestrator-dispatch-grant.functional.test.mjs`) spawned `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` with `CX_TOOLKIT_DIR` pointed at the real repo root to locate its hook/specialist source files — but `CX_TOOLKIT_DIR` also feeds `constructDir()`, so it redirected the entire machine-scoped state root into the repo checkout too; `sync-specialists.mjs` derives its own source root from `import.meta.dirname` and never actually needed the override, so it was removed rather than papered over (confirmed one stray `projects/<key>/lancedb/` directory this collision had already written into the repo root). `tests/mcp-manager.test.mjs` keeps its own `CX_TOOLKIT_DIR` (pointed at a disposable tmp repo copy, not the real checkout, so the collision doesn't apply) with a comment explaining why. Tests: `tests/functional/ingest-tooling.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/opencode-fewshot-prompt.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/mcp-broker-enforcement.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/project-root-isolation.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/regression-run-02158a157d53.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/run-events-semantics.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/surface-smoke-matrix.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/mcp/denial-audit.test.mjs`, `tests/mcp/host-role-clientinfo.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/claude-orchestration-prompt.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/opencode-local-mcp-trim.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/opencode-mcp-trim-scoping.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/orchestrator-dispatch-grant.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/mcp-manager.test.mjs` (all pass individually; no leakage into `~/.construct/projects/` from any of the fixes).
- `construct-0wmj`: real-vault manual QA confirmed `construct dev`'s single-parent `op run` re-exec (`maybeReExecUnderOpRun`) produces exactly one biometric prompt with the vault locked, as designed. Resolved the bead's open narrowing question: doctor, oracle, and cm consume no provider API key (confirmed via repo-wide grep — the only reference is a doctor *watcher* checking presence for parity, not consuming one), so the per-service `op run` fallback wrap (`wrapWithOpRun`, used only when a service restarts standalone outside the `construct dev` parent) is now narrowed to opencode and the copilot bridge only — a standalone doctor/oracle/cm restart no longer triggers a needless biometric prompt for services that never needed the resolved keys. Tests: `tests/functional/service-op-run-wiring.functional.test.mjs` (31/31 pass across the full op-run suite).

### Added

- LMCP-N (epic close, `construct-9oi4.14`): closed the two remaining OWASP GenAI gaps the epic's own tracking note left open (`graph owasp`: LLM03/LLM09 at 0 tests). LLM03 (Supply Chain): tagged the existing `tests/packs/validate.test.mjs` with `@owasp LLM03` — it already validates third-party pack manifests (id/version/`compatVersion`, `embedBindings` against a known-provider allowlist per ADR-0055) before they're trusted, a genuine supply-chain-integrity check, not a new test. LLM09 (Misinformation): added `tests/security/misinformation-evidence-grounding.test.mjs`, unit-testing `extractCitedUrls`/`findUnverifiedCitations` (`lib/orchestration/provider-outcome.mjs`, `construct-5wkl` AC#5) directly — the mechanism that flags a model citation absent from governed `webEvidence` as `evidenceStatus:'unverified-citations'` rather than presenting it as verified. `graph owasp` now shows all 10 categories covered (was 8/10).

### Fixed

- `construct-737t` (LMCP-B11 follow-up): the ADR-0060 filter block was enforced on the daemon poll path (`lib/embed/daemon.mjs`'s `enforceSectionFilters`) but never on the on-demand `provider_fetch` path — `lib/embed/demand-fetch.mjs`'s `demandFetch`/`demandFetchAll`/`demandFetchTeam` called `provider.read()` directly with no `validateFilterConfig`/`matchesFilter` call at all, so a source whose `embed.yaml` `repos`/`projects` list is broader than its `filter.scope` would drop out-of-scope items on the poll path but not the on-demand path. Added `applyEmbedFilter()` to `demand-fetch.mjs`: it loads `embed.yaml`'s `sources[]` (the only place an ADR-0060 filter block lives, per ADR-0060 §2 — `construct.config.json`'s `sources.targets` and team `filters` carry no `scope`/`predicates`/`nativeQuery` field), finds the governing source for the resolved provider+ref using the same plural-array/singular-scalar fallback the providers themselves use (`repos`/`repo`, `projects`/`project`, etc.), and applies `validateFilterConfig`/`matchesFilter` before `addObservation` in all three demand-fetch entry points — failing closed (all items dropped, `filter_config_error`/error entry recorded) on an invalid filter, same as the poll path. A broad/unscoped fetch (no single resolved ref) is left unfiltered rather than guessed at, and `type: 'error'` sentinel items bypass the filter entirely so a real provider error can never be judged (and hidden) by an unrelated `filter.scope`. Tests: `tests/functional/provider-filter-enforcement.functional.test.mjs` (two new `construct-737t` tests — a source with `repos: [org/a, org/b]` and `filter.scope.repos: [org/a]` now drops the `org/b` item via `demandFetchTeam`, matching `enforceSectionFilters`, confirmed regression-catching by reverting the fix and observing the test fail; and an error sentinel still surfaces in `result.errors` alongside a governing filter), `tests/embed-demand-fetch.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/team-sources.functional.test.mjs` (39/39 pass, zero regressions).
- `construct-hb9k`: `validateAllowlist()` (`lib/providers/contract.mjs` — `repoAllowlist`/`repoAllowGlob`/`projectAllowlist`/`channelAllowlist`) had zero real callers (`construct-hyg9`'s finding). Wired it into the one module the bead named that is actually exercised as a provider: `lib/providers/github/index.mjs`'s `read()` now validates `config.repo` and `search()` validates every `repo:` qualifier in the query, throwing a typed `OUT_OF_SCOPE` error before any network call; a search query with no `repo:` qualifier is rejected outright when an allowlist is configured, since an unscoped query could otherwise span repos outside it. Separately confirmed the bead's other flagged concern does not hold: `construct-9oi4.2.11` (LMCP-B11) is correctly closed — `lib/embed/daemon.mjs`'s `enforceSectionFilters` (added in `856c5b66`, predating this investigation) already drops out-of-filter items before observation distillation and records a `{provider, instance, filterHash, matched, dropped}` audit line per poll via the newer ADR-0060 filter block (`validateFilterConfig`/`matchesFilter`/`filterHash`), which superseded the legacy allowlist schema for the real poll path; `construct-hyg9`'s claim that this enforcement was absent conflated the two mechanisms. `tests/providers/scope-validators.test.mjs`'s `t.skip` replaced with 6 real assertions (blocked/allowed/unconfigured for both `read()` and `search()`, including a fetch-spy proving the network call never fires on a block). Tests: `tests/providers/scope-validators.test.mjs` (19/19 pass), `tests/embed-snapshot.test.mjs` + `tests/functional/provider-filter-enforcement.functional.test.mjs` (16/16 pass, confirming LMCP-B11's real seat), full suite 5475/5480 pass (5 pre-existing/unrelated env failures: missing `js-yaml` dep, missing `pandoc`/`libreoffice`, stale `graph build` state).
- `construct-q45y`: `tests/registry/surface-map.test.mjs` (added 2026-06-26, dashboard-tier retirement) was flagged by LMCP-L7 wave-4 triage (`construct-9oi4.12.7`) as a redundancy candidate against the newer, superset `tests/registry/surface-map-manifest.test.mjs` (LMCP-B7) but kept pending maintainer review since the two files aren't byte-identical. Independently re-verified assertion-by-assertion: two of the older file's three test blocks were fully subsumed, but its `surfaceForCommand('review'|'telemetry'|'evals'|'improvement')` and `surfaceForCommand('registry:validate')` calls were not — the manifest test only asserted the static `COMMAND_SURFACE` dict was byte-identical to the prior hardcoded map, never calling `surfaceForCommand()` for those specific names, so a regression isolated to `surfaceForCommand()` itself (not the dict) would have gone undetected. Merged those two missing runtime-call assertions into `surface-map-manifest.test.mjs`, then deleted the now-fully-subsumed `surface-map.test.mjs`. Tests: `tests/registry/surface-map-manifest.test.mjs`, `tests/test-corpus-inventory.test.mjs` (13/13 pass); corpus inventory regenerated via `scripts/generate-test-corpus-inventory.mjs`.
- `construct-ggdn`: `tests/security/destructive-gate.test.mjs` called `issueApprovalToken('storage_reset')` with no env override, so `doctorRoot()` resolved to the real machine's XDG state dir and mutated `~/.local/state/construct/destructive-approvals.json` on every run, with no cleanup — every sibling file in `tests/security/` isolates via `mkdtempSync`. Now sets `CONSTRUCT_DOCTOR_ROOT` to a `tempDir()` fixture in `test.before()`, the same pattern `tests/doctor/audit.test.mjs`/`tests/doctor/cost-ledger.test.mjs` already use, since `issueApprovalToken`/`consumeApprovalToken` resolve `doctorRoot()` from `process.env` at call time. Tests: `tests/security/destructive-gate.test.mjs` (7/7 pass); confirmed the real `destructive-approvals.json` mtime is unchanged before/after the run.
- `construct-hyg9`: `tests/providers/scope-validators.test.mjs`'s "throws OUT_OF_SCOPE" test had an always-pass else-branch (`assert.ok(true, 'github provider does not enforce allowlist internally...')`) — confirmed via grep that `validateAllowlist` (`lib/providers/contract.mjs`) has zero real callers anywhere in `lib/`, only the test itself and a documentation-only scaffold template. Replaced the fake pass with an honest `t.skip(...)` citing the real gap. Filed `construct-hb9k` to wire real enforcement, and flagged that `construct-9oi4.2.11` (LMCP-B11, "enforce provider filters at poll/read time") is marked closed in the tracker despite its described enforcement not being present in the current tree. Tests: `tests/providers/scope-validators.test.mjs` (13 pass, 1 honest skip, was 14/14 fake-pass before).
- `construct-ik8g`: `bin/construct`'s "Agent contract schema intact" doctor check had a ternary (`cond ? false : false`) that always evaluated false, so `missingTerminals` was always empty and the check always passed regardless of actual contract state. Wired in the real predicate (a contract is broken if it lacks `id`/`producer`/`consumer`, or declares neither `postconditions` nor `output`) that a prior triage session had already spec'd in `tests/doctor/contract-validation.test.mjs` but never wired into the doctor check itself. Verified with a mutation test: stripped `postconditions`/`output` from a real contract fixture, confirmed the check flips from ✓ to ✗, restored the fixture, confirmed ✓ again. Tests: `tests/doctor/contract-validation.test.mjs` (7/7 pass).
- `construct-zpa1`: three path-traversal/symlink-escape SECURITY tests in `tests/providers/directory-provider.test.mjs` used `catch (err) { assert.ok(err.message || true, ...) }` — always truthy, so the catch swallowed any error including a genuine `AssertionError` from the try block's own check, masking a real regression. Fixed to re-throw `AssertionError` and assert the caught error specifically names a path-escape/symlink rejection (grounded in `lib/providers/directory/index.mjs`'s actual `validatePathSecurity()` reason strings). Also fixed a lower-severity non-security tautological catch. Mutation-tested: with a realistic symlink-following regression injected into the provider, the old tautological test still reported 26/26 pass (fully masked); the fixed test correctly failed 2/26. Tests: `tests/providers/directory-provider.test.mjs` (26/26 pass).
- `construct-7g5q`: `tests/pre-compact-context-merge.test.mjs` re-pasted copies of `lib/hooks/pre-compact.mjs`'s `AUTO_SECTION_NAMES`/`parseSections`/`extractManualSections`/`mergeExistingJson` instead of importing the real exports, so a future edit to the hook could silently drift from the test undetected. The blocker (documented in a prior `bd note` on this bead): the hook had unconditional top-level code (a synchronous stdin read followed by `process.exit(0)`) that ran on any `import`, not just direct execution — confirmed to exit silently under `node -e` and hang under `node --test`. Wrapped that body in the same entrypoint guard `lib/hooks/rule-verifier.mjs:205` already uses (`if (import.meta.url === \`file://${process.argv[1]}\`) { ... }`), verified in isolation first (candidate copy imports cleanly with all four exports under both `node -e` and `node --test`, and produces byte-identical `context.md`/`context.json` output — aside from timestamp/cwd-derived fields — when invoked as the real hook entrypoint). The test file now imports the real helpers directly, using thin path-based adapters (`extractManualSectionsFromString`, `mergeExistingJsonFromObject`) that write scratch files, since the real `extractManualSections`/`mergeExistingJson` take file paths where the test fixtures are strings/objects. Tests: `tests/pre-compact-context-merge.test.mjs` (11/11 pass), `tests/hooks/*.test.mjs` (112/112 pass, no collateral damage).
- `construct-8nvo` (LMCP-B follow-up): `lib/config/source-targets.mjs` and `lib/embed/demand-fetch.mjs` hardcoded a `switch(provider)`/`if (provider === 'x')` chain for exactly github/jira/linear/slack across selector validation, legacy-env parsing, signature computation, embed-source shaping, alias resolution, and on-demand read-call construction — the same central-dispatch gap LMCP-B2 already closed for `lib/providers/registry.mjs`'s `BUILT_INS`, but left open here. Each of the four built-in manifests (`github`, `atlassian-jira`, `linear`, `slack`) now declares a `sourceTarget` block (new optional manifest field, `lib/extensions/manifest-schema.mjs`) describing its selector field/pattern/normalization, legacy env var(s), signature template, alias strategy, embed-source shape, and demand-fetch read-call shape; a new `lib/config/source-target-registry.mjs` loads these manifest-derived descriptors (mirroring `BUILT_INS`'s pattern), and both files now iterate the registry instead of naming providers directly. Adding a fifth source-target-eligible provider requires only a manifest `sourceTarget` block, not edits to either file. Behavior verified byte-identical for the four existing providers via the existing test suite plus targeted differential checks against the original per-provider formulas (github's `undefined`-vs-`""` signature quirk, jira's recency-guarded broad-JQL fallback, slack's colon-syntax legacy env parsing, the jira/linear-only catch-all alias). Tests: `tests/source-targets.test.mjs`, `tests/embed-demand-fetch.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/team-sources.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/extensions/manifest-strict.test.mjs`.
- `construct-qulg`: `tests/functional/ingest-tooling.functional.test.mjs` ran `detectNodeNativeDeps`/`detectIngestPipeline` against the live checked-out repo (`REPO = path.resolve(...'../..')`) instead of an isolated fixture, and only asserted return-value shape/type. Now builds `fs.mkdtempSync` fixture trees with controlled `node_modules/{unpdf,mammoth}` markers and a fake docling `.venv` binary, and asserts on the specific `present`/`message` values each fixture combination should produce.
- `construct-hfek`: `tests/pg-queue-reliability.test.mjs` and `tests/pg-queue.test.mjs` asserted the claim/retry/idempotency contract by regex-matching `lib/queue/pg-queue.mjs`'s source text instead of exercising it — a refactor that kept the grepped strings but broke the semantics would have passed undetected. Both files now instantiate the real `PostgresIntakeQueue` against a new in-memory fake `sql` tagged-template client (`tests/helpers/fake-pg-queue-sql.mjs`, following the existing `fakeSql` convention from `tests/worker-runtime.test.mjs`/`tests/enterprise/tenant-scoping.test.mjs`) that implements `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`-shaped claim semantics: two concurrent `claim()` calls on one item resolve to exactly one winner, lease expiry only makes an item reclaimable within `max_attempts`, `fail()` dead-letters at the attempt ceiling, and `markProcessed()`'s execution-key idempotency short-circuit is exercised for real (confirmed to catch a real regression by manually breaking the idempotency guard in `pg-queue.mjs` and re-running — the new test failed where the old regex test would not have). Tests: `tests/pg-queue.test.mjs`, `tests/pg-queue-reliability.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-H6 follow-up (`construct-9oi4.8`, ADR-0057): `construct status` and `construct doctor`'s enterprise capability reporting rendered every not-implemented capability with the bare registry status string, so `tenant-isolation`/`isolated-workers` (ADR-0057 FAIL-CLOSED — architecturally non-trivial, must hard-error) were visually and behaviorally indistinguishable from `rbac`/`signed-mcp-allowlists` (ADR-0057 LATER — no near-term path, no runtime effect), contradicting the ADR's explicit contract ("must emit exactly four categories... must never show a not-implemented capability as active" for `status`; "must not be a warning; it must be a hard error" surfaced "at startup (during construct doctor)" for fail-closed capabilities). Added `categorizeEnterpriseCapability()` (`lib/mode-capabilities.mjs`, re-exported from `lib/status.mjs`) mapping each capability to `active`/`fail-closed`/`later`/`absent`: `formatStatusReport`'s enterprise table now renders the category instead of the raw status, and `construct doctor` in enterprise mode now hard-fails (non-optional, exit 1) on unimplemented fail-closed capabilities while later-wave capabilities report informationally with no failure. Tests: `tests/status-enterprise.test.mjs`, `tests/acceptance/modes/enterprise.acceptance.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/enterprise-doctor-fail-closed.functional.test.mjs`.
- `construct-tyyy`: `construct uninstall`'s memory-MCP-removal and settings-un-merge categories, and `construct install --cleanup-legacy-global`, now also detect/strip Construct-managed MCP entries from `~/.claude.json`'s top-level `mcpServers` (construct-ranh's machine-scope write path) and `<project>/.mcp.json` (construct-ranh's project-scope write path), alongside the existing legacy `~/.claude/settings.json` path, which remains needed for pre-fix installs. Tests: `tests/uninstall.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/uninstall-coverage.functional.test.mjs`, `tests/functional/install-legacy-global-cleanup.functional.test.mjs`.
- `construct-fbxv` (AP5 epic close): all 9 numbered child bugs (`construct-fbxv.1`–`.9`) were already closed with passing tests, but `bin/construct orchestrate run`/`status` text-mode output (both in-process and `--remote`) still printed the raw persisted `run.status`/`run.execution.degraded` instead of routing through `shapeRun` (`lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`) — the same honest terminal-state taxonomy the MCP `orchestration_status` tool already applied. New runs persist an honest status directly (`lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs` `executeRun`), so this only mis-reported legacy pre-taxonomy run records and remote-daemon payloads, but it was the literal CLI-bypass `construct-fbxv.4`'s evidence named. Fixed by shaping CLI text output the same way for `orchestrate run`, `orchestrate status <id>`, `orchestrate status` (listing), and the `--remote` status equivalents.
- `construct-73n5`: resolved the LMCP-L7 wave-3 ambiguity over `tests/functional/self-hosting-cert.functional.test.mjs` — confirmed via grep that no code in `lib/`, `bin/`, or `scripts/` reads or regenerates `docs/notes/research/construct-self-audit` content, so the file's header now says plainly that it is an intentional anti-deletion tripwire for those one-time audit docs, not a code-behavior test, and that it must not be removed without maintainer sign-off. No test logic changed.
- `construct-spoz`: resolved the LMCP-L7 wave-4 ambiguity over `tests/workflows/template-resolution.test.mjs` and `tests/workflows/template-shape.test.mjs` — neither ever imports a `lib/*.mjs` module; both regex-parse `templates/workflows/*.yml` directly (reference-resolution against `templates/docs/` in one, required-field/id-slug/version/path-count shape checks in the other). Same judgment as `construct-73n5`: both headers now say plainly that a failure signals static template drift, not a `lib/workflows/instantiate.mjs` runtime regression, and that removal requires confirming their coverage moves elsewhere first. No test logic changed. Tests: `tests/workflows/template-resolution.test.mjs` + `tests/workflows/template-shape.test.mjs` (41/41 pass), full `tests/workflows/*.test.mjs` (93/93 pass, 0 fail).

### Added (2026-07-04 LMCP batch — ~50 beads across tracks B, D, E, G, H, K, L, M, N and AP0/2/4/5/6/8/9)

- LMCP-B4: model provider families (anthropic, openai, ollama, github-copilot, local, and six OpenRouter families) now register as `kind:"model"` extension manifests, bridged additively through the unified provider registry — `construct provider list` shows them alongside data-source providers with zero change to the model catalog's existing visibility/cache/poll behavior. Tests: `tests/models/catalog-provider-kind.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-B2 (`construct-9oi4.2.2`, closed 2026-07-03 in commit `fd62ba5f`, CHANGELOG entry missing until now): `lib/providers/registry.mjs`'s `BUILT_INS` is no longer a hardcoded array — it discovers `kind:"data-source"` manifests via the same `resolveManifestDirs()`/`loadManifestsFromDir()` path `resolveProviders()`/`describeProviders()` use, and is marked `@deprecated` in favor of those. Five built-in data-source manifests (github, jira, confluence, slack, salesforce) landed alongside it, plus strict-mode validation in `lib/extensions/validate.mjs` (unknown-field rejection, `secretEnvKeys` prefix allowlist, escalation-kind checks, builtin-prompt shadowing prevention). Tests: `tests/extensions/manifest-providers.test.mjs`, `tests/extensions/manifest-strict.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-B5 (`construct-9oi4.2.5`, closed): a `*.tool.mjs` self-registration convention (`lib/mcp/tool-registry.mjs`) lets a new MCP tool declare its def/handler/safety classification under `lib/mcp/tools/` and register with `lib/mcp/server.mjs` without any edit to that file — `scanToolModules()` throws a clear, attributable error if a discovered def has no inline `safety` block or names a tool with no matching handler, the same fail-loud contract `withSafetyEnvelope` already applies to the hand-maintained catalog. The existing 75+ tools remain unmigrated in `HARDCODED_TOOL_DEFS` (now `[...HARDCODED_TOOL_DEFS, ...scanned]` exported as `ALL_TOOL_DEFS`) — a full rewrite is out of scope. `tests/mcp-tools-doc-parity.test.mjs` now imports the real merged catalog instead of statically eval-parsing `server.mjs` source. Tests: `tests/functional/mcp-dynamic-tool-registration.functional.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-B7: `FORMAT_ENGINES` (document export) and `COMMAND_SURFACE`/`SURFACE_TIERS` move to default manifest JSON under `lib/registry/manifests/` with `.cx/registry/*.json` project-override support and a `validateSurfaceMap()` strict check; default behavior is byte-identical to the prior hardcoded dicts. Tests: `tests/artifacts/format-engines-manifest.test.mjs`, `tests/registry/surface-map-manifest.test.mjs`.
- Documented the `bd list` infinite-recursion hang (`construct-qqv9`): the default tree renderer in the external `bd` binary (`~/.local/bin/bd`, sourced from [steveyegge/beads](https://github.com/steveyegge/beads)) walks bidirectional `relates-to` edges as tree children with no visited-set, recursing forever — confirmed by direct reproduction (bare `bd list` produced tens of gigabytes of output within seconds). Not a fix — `bd` is an externally compiled binary outside this repo's remit — but `docs/operations/troubleshooting.md` now has a `## bd list hangs or never terminates` section prescribing `bd list --flat` / `--json` / `bd ready` as safe defaults and noting Construct's own internal automation already avoids the bare form.
- LMCP-B9: per-provider circuit-breaker state is now visible in `construct status` and a new doctor watcher (`lib/doctor/watchers/provider-breaker.mjs`) escalates once per OPEN episode instead of failing silently. Tests: `tests/providers/breaker-status.test.mjs`, `tests/doctor/provider-breaker-watcher.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-N5: durable per-actor/run consumption budgets (tokens, tool calls) wired into `Broker.invoke` — an exhausted run halts before executing with a durable `budget_denied` record that survives restart, and `construct status` shows consumption vs. budget per run. External-writes/queue-submission budget kinds are modeled but not yet wired to a real call site. Tests: `tests/security/consumption-limits.test.mjs`.
- `construct-a81q`: oracle producer-liveness now has a real doctor watcher (`lib/doctor/watchers/oracle-liveness.mjs`, registered in `WATCHERS`) applying the same idle-safe gate as session-prelude surfacing — a stale tick only escalates when pending oracle work also exists. Tests: `tests/doctor/oracle-liveness-watcher.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-K3: a new engine-health probe (`lib/doctor/engine-health.mjs`) gives `construct doctor` visibility into the document-export toolchain (pandoc, typst, LibreOffice, pptxgenjs), reusing the existing `detect()`/install-hint logic so doctor and export-preflight always agree. Tests: `tests/doctor-engine-health.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-K4: HuggingFace transformers disposition decided **keep** (it backs the active default `local` embedding adapter, not a dead path) — added `lib/doctor/embedding-health.mjs` doctor visibility for the embedding model, implementing the healthCheck already declared in `deps/intent.json`. Tests: `tests/doctor-embedding-health.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-L10: a new guard enumerates the full MCP `TOOL_SAFETY` catalog, asserts every tool declares an `outputSchema`, and validates 22 captured real tool results against their declared schemas with the same AJV validator the MCP SDK uses at runtime. Tests: `tests/mcp-tool-output-schema-guard.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-L8: Playwright adopted as a devDependency with a small, self-skipping test that renders a published HTML artifact in headless Chromium and checks font embedding, console errors, and link resolution; wired as a non-blocking CI job. Tests: `tests/browser/publish-html-artifact.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-L6: real solo-, team-, and enterprise-mode acceptance suites assert every `mode-capabilities` entry marked `implemented` has a passing, non-mocked check — team mode proven against a real local Postgres (two workers racing `claim()`, no double-claim); the enterprise leg proves the fail-closed contracts instead (H1 `resolveTenantContext` throws `TenantResolutionError` with no tenant id; H5 `enforceMandatoryAudit` denies enterprise actions on a genuinely broken audit sink, forced via a real ENOTDIR fs error rather than a mock; H6 `buildStatus`/`formatStatusReport` render the real capability table and `unsupported` verdict), plus a tenant-scoped `WorkerRegistry` check against real Postgres (two tenants, same `workerId`, each `list()` sees only its own row). New `enterprise-mode-acceptance` CI job (Postgres service container, non-blocking like `team-mode-acceptance`) exercises the DB-dependent leg; CI-required promotion for both jobs still deferred pending a few green real-CI runs. Tests: `tests/acceptance/modes/solo.acceptance.test.mjs`, `tests/acceptance/modes/team.acceptance.test.mjs`, `tests/acceptance/modes/enterprise.acceptance.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-L7 (partial, wave 4): full read-through triage of the last 17 subdirectories still at wave-0 sampling depth — `tests/certification`, `graph`, `asset-quality`, `hooks`, `providers`, `workflows`, `mcp`, `writes`, `security`, `registry`, `packs`, `roles`, `doctor`, `orchestration`, `extractors`, `extensions`, `capabilities` (141 files total, all classified against the A8 rubric). Combined with waves 2-3 (top-level `tests/*.test.mjs`: 343/343, `tests/functional/`: 186/186) and waves 0-2 (`tests/audit/**`: 100%), every test file in the repo's `tests/` tree has now had at least one full A8 read-through, satisfying the wave-0 triage-table acceptance criterion in full. Zero files met the confirmed-duplicate deletion bar this wave (one candidate, `tests/registry/surface-map.test.mjs`, was considered then kept after confirming via `diff` it is not byte-identical to its apparent superset `surface-map-manifest.test.mjs` — same conservative bar as wave 3, which also made zero deletions). 6 non-deletion quality issues (weak/mislabeled assertions, tautological catch blocks and always-pass branches, a false-parity docstring claim, non-isolated state), 1 redundancy-candidate, and 1 SUSPECT-STUB investigation were filed as follow-up beads (`construct-egwo`, `construct-hyg9`, `construct-zpa1`, `construct-j1yr`, `construct-hrz9`, `construct-ggdn`, `construct-q45y`, `construct-spoz`) rather than fixed inline, plus one incidental production-code bug found along the way (`construct-ik8g`: `bin/construct`'s "Agent contract schema intact" doctor check has a ternary that always evaluates false, so it never actually fails). See `bd note construct-9oi4.12.7` for the full wave-4 breakdown.
- LMCP-D3: the workflow-manifest loader now tracks provenance (`_source`/`_shadowedBy`) and `graph explain` surfaces which tier's manifest won a precedence conflict, closing the gap where project/pack workflow contributions were invisible in triage and graph output. Tests: `tests/functional/workflow-contribution.functional.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-E3 (partial): a durable pack enable/disable lifecycle (`.cx/packs.json`) with `construct pack list|enable|disable|info`, refusing enable on an incompatible `compatVersion`. No existing consumer yet gates on it (follow-up). Tests: `tests/packs/lifecycle.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-G10: a Postgres-backed team trace store and an explicit shared-memory boundary (opt-in `visibility:'shared-project'` + required provenance) so a trace one worker emits is readable by another store instance on the same substrate, while a private observation can never reach the shared tier. `construct status` now surfaces this in team/enterprise mode (`status.sharedState`: recent team traces + shared memory count, keyed on the same `projectKey` the write path uses) — the previously test-only `lib/storage/shared-memory.mjs` now has a real production call site. Tests: `tests/functional/shared-state.functional.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-H4 (partial): closed a structural cross-tenant leak in the Postgres run store (no `tenant_id` column despite runs already carrying `tenantId`) — all three team-mode Postgres stores now scope queries by tenant and fail closed (`TenantResolutionError`) for enterprise mode with no resolvable tenant. Telemetry emitters not yet updated. Tests: `tests/enterprise/tenant-scoping.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-M5: a new release evidence gate verifies every `mode-capabilities.mjs` "implemented" capability has both its backing file in the packed npm artifact and a passing acceptance test, wired as a blocking step in `release:preflight`/`release:check`. Tests: `tests/scripts/release-evidence-gate.test.mjs`.
- LMCP-N8: closed the security-coverage gap for all 9 previously-flagged executable workflows (`graph missing-tests --security`: 9 → 0) via a new output/prompt-leakage test plus honest `@secures` tagging of existing consumption-budget coverage, closing OWASP LLM05/LLM07/LLM10 gaps (LLM03/LLM09 left honestly undocumented — no genuine link found). Tests: `tests/security/output-and-prompt-handling.test.mjs`.
- `construct-5wkl`: hardened the provider worker path (`lib/orchestration/provider-outcome.mjs`, new) with typed error codes for rate-limit/server-error/timeout/auth/content-filtered/empty-content/reasoning-only/malformed-response outcomes, bounded exponential-backoff retry (transient codes only), redacted per-task provider telemetry (`task.providerMeta`), an OpenRouter reasoning-budget headroom fix (directly fixing a reasoning-only-output bug), and evidence grounding (`task.evidenceStatus`/`unverifiedCitations`) for web-capable specialists. Governed/native web-search tool-loops still use the original single-attempt path (deferred). Added `docs/guides/reference/provider-worker-reliability.md`.
- `construct-neq9.8`: a durable per-surface orchestration smoke matrix (CLI, Claude Code MCP, VS Code MCP, hooks session-start) plus a runbook, asserting non-degraded execution and envelope/disk parity as the standing regression gate for a prior silent-degraded-completion incident. Tests: `tests/functional/surface-smoke-matrix.functional.test.mjs`.
- `construct-neq9.7`: a regression pin for incident run-02158a157d53, driving the exact keys-present/tiers-absent machine state through the real spawned MCP server and asserting on the durable run file (not just the tool envelope) — hard invariant (never `degraded:true` + `tasks:[]` + `status:'completed'`), honest binary (refuse loudly or execute for real), and readiness/run parity. No production code changed: `construct-neq9.2`/`construct-neq9.3` already landed the credential-family-fallback and readiness-parity fixes this test depends on. Tests: `tests/functional/regression-run-02158a157d53.functional.test.mjs`.
- `construct-r8wr.4`: a new `construct oracle triage` bulk-triage command collapses the pending-approval queue to one representative row per content-dedup key with an occurrence count, archiving superseded rows to `pending-archive.jsonl` (history preserved, nothing deleted). Run once against the live queue: 299 rows → 6 unique survivors.
- `construct-r8wr.7`: the intake daemon's tick now sweeps `.cx/intake/pending/` every run — a packet whose source file is missing, or whose age exceeds the 14-day TTL, moves to `.cx/intake/dead-letter/` with a reason, and ingest is idempotent per source path. Dead-lettered 15 packets this session that were orphaned by an earlier `docs/intake/` removal.
- `construct-u3qe`: documented the mandatory full-gate-before-commit workflow for parallel worktree-agent collection (`docs/operations/parallel-worktree-collection.md`) — collision-revert check plus a full `npm test` run must be green before any collection commit.

### Fixed (2026-07-04 LMCP batch)

- AP2.2/.3/.8/.9 (`construct-1yhp`): `construct doctor` now surfaces the resolved worker backend and web-capability mode as advisory lines (both previously invisible, so a fully green doctor run gave no warning that orchestration would run prepare-only or that a web-capable specialist would degrade); MCP `orchestration_readiness` no longer mislabels a self-reported tool catalog as `host-session`, remote auth now derives from a configured `CONSTRUCT_ORCHESTRATION_URL` instead of requiring an opt-in flag, collected env facts are now actually read by the verdict chain, and an unreported client contract version no longer self-defaults to always-pass; doctor's PATH checks fall back to `sh` when `zsh` isn't installed and a dead always-true clause was removed from the model-tier check.
- AP5.6/.7/.9 (`construct-fbxv`): added a doctor watcher that reads the run store each tick and flags a recent error/degraded run (closing the gap where a comment claimed this already happened); the `lifecycle.completed` trace event and SSE `completed` run event now carry an explicit `executionState`/`degraded` boolean so a prepared-vs-executed or degraded run is distinguishable from the event stream alone without re-reading the run store; removed a stale `lib/server/**` entry from this file's own safe-edit list (the directory no longer exists).
- AP5.8 (`construct-fbxv.8`): the workflow done-gate now rejects non-reverifiable verification evidence (a bare "did stuff") for implement/validate/operate-phase tasks, requiring a runnable command, file path, URL, test reference, or a `cx-reviewer`/`cx-qa` attestation — previously any non-empty string satisfied the gate and only the implement phase was covered. `alignmentFindings` demotes the same condition to a HIGH finding for state loaded from disk, bypassing the live gate.
- AP4.7/.9/.10 (`construct-192h`): `construct integrations status` no longer performs live `op read`s or executes shell rc files just to report Jira/Confluence/GitHub configuration presence — it now detects by shape only (env presence, dotenv/config-file/rc-file text match), removing a third parallel rc-scrape-and-shell-source implementation; clarified that the audit trail's `op://` persistence and `mcp-platform-config.mjs`'s prohibition on writing `op://` refs govern different sinks (Construct's own audit JSONL vs. host-readable MCP config files), resolving an apparent policy contradiction; unified dotenv-line parsing (`lib/env-config.mjs`'s new `parseEnvContent`, shared by `parseEnvFile` and the secret resolver's file-tier reader) so an unpaired quote (`KEY="abc`) no longer resolves to different values (`abc` vs `"abc`) depending on which reader read it. Tests: `tests/integrations/status-no-materialization.test.mjs`, `tests/providers/dotenv-parity.test.mjs`.
- AP8.4/.6/.9 (`construct-6y6w`): unified MCP server-entry refresh across every host surface (Claude global/project, VS Code, Cursor, OpenCode) through one shared comparator instead of four divergent ad hoc rules — closing a gap where the construct-mcp telemetry env passthrough or a version pin could land on one host and never another; pinned context7 and sequential-thinking to exact versions in the MCP catalog; `construct://status` now detects code/version skew on long-running MCP servers by re-reading `package.json` on each read; generated VS Code `mcp.json` entries now pin `cwd` to `${workspaceFolder}` and the MCP server logs its resolved cwd at startup. `.7` (clientInfo-derived hostRole) and `.8` (project `.env` tier on MCP) remain open — both need files outside this pass's scope.
- AP6.2/.3/.4/.5 (`construct-neq9`): spawned test envs are now read-hermetic via an allowlist-built `sterileSpawnEnv()` (mkdtemp-pinned HOME/XDG), closing a path where ambient `CX_MODEL_*`/provider keys/`WEB_SEARCH_URL` and a real `op` read could leak into "sterile" functional suites — also fixed a live secret leak in `secret-resolver.functional.test.mjs` where an unpinned `cwd` read this machine's real `.env`. Added regression-guard tests confirming `resolveEmbeddedModel`'s credential-family-fallback and the readiness/executability gate already correctly handle the keys-but-no-tiers machine state, and that the production provider-call timeout default (already raised to 120s) is exercised against real-shaped mock latency.
- `construct-r7bp`: fixed a wrong OpenRouter model slug (`qwen2.5-coder-32b-instruct` was missing OpenRouter's hyphenated id) and added verified `:free`-tier fallback diversity so a rate-limited model has a real alternate to roll to; `gemma-3-27b-it:free`'s retirement was already handled by a prior commit.
- `construct-trxz.12`: reverted an incorrect VS Code `mcp.json` value→`${env:VAR}`-reference flip after confirming VS Code does not support that expansion in stdio env blocks (live-validated against the real binary); OpenCode's expansion was confirmed genuinely safe. Discovered and filed separately (`construct-ranh`, unverified pending independent confirmation) that `~/.claude/settings.json`'s `mcpServers` key may not be read by the installed Claude Code CLI at all.
- LMCP-O2/O3/O5 (`construct-9oi4.15`): added a config-key-consumer-parity test confirming `DATABASE_URL`, `CONSTRUCT_TENANT_ID`, and the postgres-queue-to-git alias — all flagged as dead reads — were already wired by prior work in this same batch; found four genuinely dead config keys during the sweep, tracked as follow-up `construct-9oi4.15.9` rather than fixed silently. Added a comment-lint check enforcing that every "staged/experimental" or "not yet implemented" doc claim cites a bead id, extended to `.mdx` files (previously unscanned), and corrected a stale `providers/overview.md` claim that write/watch were unimplemented when the LMCP-J governed-write adapters already ship them. Added regression coverage for `GitIntakeQueue.read()`'s claimed/`<worker>`/ traversal, which was already fixed by a prior bead but had no dedicated test.
