- **`construct-vevd`: ACP gained an injectable `fetchImpl` (mirroring `lib/orchestration/worker.mjs`'s existing test seam), removing the ~100s real-provider-call stall in `tests/functional/acp-server.functional.test.mjs`'s "backend resolution honors config" test.** With blank model/API-key env vars, a credential-family fallback still resolves a model id and the provider backend attempts a real HTTP call before degrading — the test had bumped its `waitFor` timeout to 150000ms as a stopgap rather than fixing this. `runAcpServer` (`lib/acp/server.mjs`) now accepts `fetchImpl` and threads it into `executeRun`; the test drives `runAcpServer` in-process (`node:stream` `PassThrough`, not the spawned CLI) with a `fetchImpl` that rejects immediately, so the run degrades without any network call. The whole suite now runs in under a second (previously ~100s+); `waitFor`'s default reverted to 8000ms.

### Added

- Regression tests proving construct dev uses project-local `.cx` paths (fvw5.18/A4): `tests/functional/project-root-isolation.functional.test.mjs` (7 tests) asserts `resolveProjectRoot(tmpDir) !== packageRoot`, that `buildRuntimeRecoverySummary` reads durable files under the supplied `rootDir`, and that telemetry path derivations honour an external root.
- New `docs/guides/concepts/root-semantics.md` documents `packageRoot`, `resolveProjectRoot`, and `process.cwd()` with a subsystem table and a concrete global-install example (fvw5.12/A5). `docs/guides/concepts/architecture.mdx` updated with a root-semantics paragraph and link.

- **Opt-in 1Password service-account path for non-interactive secret resolution (`lib/providers/secret-resolver.mjs`).** When `OP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN` is set, `op read`/`op run` resolve secrets with no desktop app, biometric, or `op signin` — the right primitive for Construct's multi-process/daemon/headless shape (verified against 1Password's current docs: service accounts are available on Individual/Family plans, and the token makes the CLI non-interactive). The resolver now forwards the token from the caller's env into the `op` subprocess so a runtime can source it from the OS keychain instead of plaintext, and the `OP_NOT_SIGNED_IN` guidance names the service-account alternative. The desktop app integration remains the default when no token is set (backward-compatible; existing `resolveOpRef` callers unaffected). `construct doctor credentials` now reports the active auth mode (`describeOpAuthMode` — `service-account` vs `desktop-session`, including whether the desktop session is signed in, so a cold session that would prompt repeatedly is visible), and the credentials cookbook documents the token with its vault/rate-limit caveats (`construct-192h.6`). Note: a service account cannot read the built-in Personal/Private vault and the Individual tier is rate-limited to ~1k reads/day, so a rate-limit-safe resolve-once model for short-lived processes is tracked in `construct-trxz.11`. Proven by `tests/secret-resolver-service-account.test.mjs`.

### Fixed

- **Service spawns now receive the merged `liveEnv` (`construct-192h.8`).** `lib/service-manager.mjs` computed `liveEnv` (config.env merged over `process.env`) but spawned cm/opencode/copilot-bridge **without** it, so config.env-only vars — including `CONSTRUCT_OP_ENV_FILE` and `OP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN` — never reached those children (only doctor/oracle got the env). All five services now spawn with `liveEnv`, matching the doctor/oracle shape, so the service-account token reaches every `op run`. Proven by `tests/service-manager/spawn-env.test.mjs`.
- **A single `op run` at the `construct dev` parent replaces the per-service wrap (`construct-trxz.11`, realizes ADR-0049's "resolve at a stable parent").** The first Design A wiring launched each of ~5 daemons under its own `op run`, relying on 1Password's session to dedupe the prompt across five launches (never proven) and, under a service-account token, multiplying reads (~5 services × the catalog's refs each startup — a real concern against the Individual tier's ~1k reads/day). `construct dev` now re-execs itself once under a single `op run --env-file …` (`maybeReExecUnderOpRun`, `lib/providers/op-run.mjs`): every `op://` reference resolves one time (one biometric unlock) into the parent env and the detached daemons inherit the resolved keys. A `CONSTRUCT_OP_RUN_ACTIVE` sentinel makes the per-service `wrapWithOpRun` a no-op inside the re-exec'd parent so nothing nests a second `op run`; the per-service wrap is retained as the fallback for daemons started outside `construct dev` (a doctor/oracle restart from a hook). **Reworks `construct-trxz.3`:** `op run` masks the process it directly wraps, so under the single parent the `construct dev` output is masked but the detached daemon log files (local-only, under the state dir) are not — the accepted, documented cost of resolving once, with the per-service fallback still masking any daemon it wraps directly. Un-opted path (no `CONSTRUCT_OP_ENV_FILE`, or `op` absent) is byte-for-byte unchanged and never invokes `op`. Proven by `tests/functional/op-run-wrap.functional.test.mjs` and `tests/functional/service-op-run-wiring.functional.test.mjs`; ADR-0049 updated.

- **Provider execution timeouts corrected from impossible defaults (`construct-o6t8.1`, `construct-o6t8.2`).** The provider worker's HTTP timeout defaulted to **200 ms** (`lib/orchestration/worker.mjs`), aborting every real LLM call before it could return; the remote-orchestration client hard-coded the same 200 ms on its run POST and left the status poll entirely unbounded (`lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`). Both now use sane, env-overridable defaults grounded in two code-confirmed references — `openai/openai-python` and `anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python` both default the overall request timeout to 600 s — landing on a conservative 120 s worker floor (`CONSTRUCT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS`) and a 30 s remote per-request bound (`CONSTRUCT_ORCHESTRATION_TIMEOUT_MS`), with the status poll now carrying the same per-request bound. Both read the override with a parse-then-default-when-unset pattern so an explicit `0` is honored rather than coerced away by `||`. A timed-out remote request now reports `"…timed out after <N>ms"` instead of the misleading `"…not reachable…"` — a status-honesty fix beyond the timeout itself.
- **Stabilized two CI-only test failures on the AP staging integration (green-suite fix).** Two failures reproduced only in CI, not locally: (1) `timedRemoteFetch` (`lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`) bounded each remote fetch with a fire-and-forget `AbortSignal.timeout()` raced against a reject-on-abort promise — on a healthy fast response the race settled but the full-duration timer (up to 30 s) and the losing promise stayed alive, so in the long-lived suite process they resolved *after* their own test's async scope had closed, tripping node:test's "resolution still pending after the loop resolved" and cancelling the sibling `construct-o6t8.2` timeout proofs. It now uses a cancelable timer cleared in `finally` the instant either side of the race wins, keeping the race safety-net that settles the call even when a fetch ignores its own signal (the `[R20]` black-box guarantee) while leaving nothing scheduled to fire late. (2) `tests/audit/f13-artifact/publish-validation-propagation.test.mjs` exercised the validation-propagation contract through a `figures:true` markdown publish, which gates on the diagram toolchain (d2/graphviz/mmdc) — present on dev machines, absent in CI, so `detection.present` was false and `runPublish` returned early with no `ledger`. The fixtures contain no diagrams and `md` is a pure copy export, so the test now passes `figures:false` / `--no-figures`, making it hermetic while still proving export-succeeds-but-validation-fails propagation. Verified by the full suite (`node scripts/run-tests.mjs`, 0 fail / 0 cancelled) including a stripped-`PATH` run of the publish test that reproduces CI's missing toolchain.
- **Timeout test fixtures no longer manufacture or depend on the production default (`construct-neq9.6`).** `tests/audit/f09-orchestration/provider-timeout.test.mjs` (and its `.red` sibling) and `remote-per-request-timeout.test.mjs` pinned their determinism on the old 200 ms production default; they now set an explicit small `CONSTRUCT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS` / `CONSTRUCT_ORCHESTRATION_TIMEOUT_MS`, so the proof of boundedness is independent of the shipped default — closing the fixture-driven-default anti-pattern where a test's settle-bound had silently defined a production value.
- **Corrected inaccurate user-facing error and remediation strings (`construct-xj96.*`).** Hints that named non-existent commands or stale paths now name real ones: `construct workflow init` → the real `workflow` subcommands (`lib/workflow-state.mjs`, `lib/oracle/gaps.mjs`, `lib/intake/session-prelude.mjs`); `construct census` / `construct outcomes aggregate` removed (`lib/oracle/gaps.mjs`); legacy `~/.construct/config.env` advice repointed to the XDG path (`bin/construct`, `lib/cli-commands.mjs`); a bogus `CONSTRUCT_ASR_BACKEND` suggestion (`lib/document-extract.mjs`, `bin/construct`) and an unreachable Langfuse auto-config block (`bin/construct`) removed; `construct setup` → `construct install`; `doctor hooks` → `hooks:health` (`lib/hook-health.mjs`); the `MODEL_UNRESOLVED` degradation reason's double period fixed (`lib/embedded-contract/execution.mjs`); the `--max-stored` observation-cap remedy and stale scope-lifecycle doc citations repointed. Each verified by an `rg` showing zero remaining bad references at the named sites; the `postgres` store enum was intentionally kept (ADR-0021 Mode-C) with only its false remediation text corrected.
- **Completed the honest terminal-state taxonomy on every surface (`construct-fbxv`, AP5 validation pass).** The runtime now reports `degraded` for any degraded run that is not prepare-only — including a run that *executed* with a capability gap (`webUnavailable`), which previously surfaced as bare `completed` with only a side flag (`lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs`). `shapeRun` (`lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`) and `executeRun` now derive identical statuses, and `shapeRun` still re-derives `degraded` for legacy runs persisted as bare `completed`. The remote poll-loop `TERMINAL` set gained `degraded` and `completed-prepare-only`, so a finished degraded/prepare-only remote run is no longer polled to the deadline and misreported as *"still executing"*. `hostAdapterMetadata` now surfaces the top-level `run.degraded`/`degradationReason` (not just `execution.degraded`), fixing the `orchestrate run --json` blind spot (`construct-fbxv.5`). The ACP completed-event message no longer describes an executed run as *"prepared tasks — inline backend, no specialist execution"* — it maps `completed`/`completed-prepare-only`/`degraded`/`completed-with-failures` honestly (`lib/acp/server.mjs`).
- **Closed a trust-boundary regression on the remote `orchestration_status` path.** `statusViaService` (`lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs`) shaped remote run data but skipped the ADR-0050 fail-closed web-evidence grader that `runViaService` applies, so a remote/team service could return `webEvidence` pre-marked `trust:'trusted'` and it would reach the host ungoverned. Every status response now runs through `governRemoteWebEvidence` before `shapeRun`, exactly as the run path does.
- **Consolidated numeric-env parsing and fixed two latent defects from the defaults work (`construct-o6t8.3`).** `resolveMsSetting` was renamed `resolveNonNegativeSetting` — it resolves byte caps, counts, and loop bounds, not only milliseconds. The provider-worker timeout dropped a bespoke `Number(env)` parse that turned a garbage `CONSTRUCT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS` into `NaN` (an `AbortSignal.timeout(NaN)` aborts every provider call instantly) or a negative value into a giant delay; it now uses the shared resolver (garbage/negative → the exported `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_DEFAULT_MS` = 120 s, explicit `0` honored). The embed daemon's log-rotation callback called the helper without importing it — a `ReferenceError` that broke rotation on daemon start — now fixed. The remote-timeout client's private duplicate parser was replaced with the same shared helper.
- **Removed a duplicated, unreachable `anthropic`/`openrouter` branch pair** in `resolveWebCapability` (`lib/orchestration/web-capability.mjs`) left by a botched edit.
- **The intent verifier's presence gate no longer triggers a 1Password prompt on the routing hot path.** `isApiKeyConfigured` (`lib/intent-classifier.mjs`) now uses `hasSecret` instead of the materializing `resolveSecret`, so deciding whether to run the *background* verifier never spawns `op read`; key materialization stays lazy in `defaultModelCaller` where a call is actually made.
- **`construct publish --recording=<name>` and `--figures` now change behavior instead of being silently dropped (`construct-xj96.11`).** The parser was extracted to a testable `parsePublishArgs`, the two declared-but-unhandled flags are wired to `runPublish`, and unknown `--` flags are surfaced as a warning rather than discarded (`lib/publish.mjs`).
- **Repointed the last stale `construct graph build` references** (real: `construct matrix build`) in two lib file headers (`lib/graph/staleness.mjs`, `lib/doctor/watchers/graph-staleness.mjs`) and the dead `scope-lifecycle` README link (real: `profile-lifecycle`).
- **Env-shadow warning now names the value that actually wins per surface (`construct-xj96.12`).** `loadConstructEnv` takes a `shadowWinner` (`'file'` | `'shell'`) and the message reflects it: `bin/construct` declares `'shell'` (it copies only the keys missing from `process.env`, so a shell export wins on conflict), while the MCP server and other file-wins callers keep the default `'file'`. Previously the warning asserted *"The config file will be used"* on every surface, which was false on the CLI. Extracted `shadowWarningMessage`, proven by `tests/env-config/shadow-warning.test.mjs`.
- **Corrected stale user-facing docs (`construct-1p2a`, partial).** Shipped cookbook/ops docs taught deprecated or non-existent commands: `construct models --apply` → `construct models free` + `construct models set --tier=… --model=…`; the non-existent `--fallback=` flag section rewritten to describe the real inference-populated fallback chain; `provider_fetch`/`rovo_search` reframed as MCP tools (not CLI commands); a stale `construct providers test` → `construct providers status`; and legacy `~/.construct/config.env` repointed to the XDG path across 26 guide/ops files. Verified by grep (0 remaining hits) and `docs:verify`. Residual `~/.construct/<subpath>` references (vector/cache/plugins) remain open in `construct-1p2a` pending per-path XDG ground-truth.
- **Removed the last Postgres-era feature staleness from docs and CLI help (`construct-1p2a`, feature-level pass).** The SQL backend is gone (retrieval is embedded LanceDB; `construct backup` and `construct storage` hard-error on it), but several surfaces still taught the removed model — each corrected against verified code, not blind-repointed. `docs/operations/backup-restore.md` was rewritten from the deleted `construct backup create/verify/restore` family to the real state model: durable state is the project git repo + Beads issues in Dolt (`bd dolt push`), while `.cx/` (gitignored in full, including the LanceDB index) is machine-local and rebuildable. `docs/guides/start/install.mdx` dropped the Docker/Postgres provisioning narrative (prerequisite, `construct init` step, the reserved `54329–54339` port block, the `Postgres + pgvector` retrieval section, the `--no-docker` flag) for the embedded-LanceDB reality. `docs/guides/concepts/architecture.mdx` fixed the machine-scope footprint table: the checkout symlink is `~/.config/construct/lib` (a hook-lib symlink, per `lib/setup.mjs:275`), not `~/.construct/lib`, and the `~/.config/construct/services/` "Docker compose for local Postgres" row was removed (no such dir is created). `lib/cli-commands.mjs` dropped the non-existent `postgres` service from `construct dev --only` help and the removed `--no-docker` install flag (verified against `SELECTABLE_SERVICES` and `setup.mjs` known-flags). The `construct backup` guidance string was corrected to point at git + Beads rather than the misleading "commit `.cx/`" (which is gitignored). Team/enterprise deployment topology (`deployment-model.mdx`, enterprise RDS+pgvector) is intentionally left as the pluggable-run-store story (ADR-0021), out of this local-install pass.
- Added Bun-compiled single-binary tooling as the candidate primary distribution path alongside the existing Node-SEA release pipeline: `scripts/build-binary.mjs` (`npm run build:binary`) runs `bun build --compile` for macOS arm64/x64 and Linux x64/arm64 with a post-build smoke check on the host's own architecture; `scripts/build-sea.mjs` (`npm run build:binary:fallback`) is the recorded Node SEA fallback build for the current platform, reusing the bundle-then-postject-inject technique already proven in `release.yml`. `scripts/install.sh` is a curl-pipe installer (detect OS/arch, download, verify sha256, install) matching the naming convention `release.yml` already publishes. `Formula/construct.rb` is a parallel Homebrew formula template for the Bun-binary track, distinct from the existing `templates/homebrew/construct.rb` (Node-SEA track) until one path is chosen. `.github/workflows/bun-binary-smoke.yml` is a standalone, non-blocking CI job that builds one target with Bun and runs `construct doctor` against it — currently expected red (see below).
- **Verified, not assumed**: with Bun 1.3.14 installed locally, `bun build --compile` against `bin/construct` succeeds for all four targets (native macOS arm64 build executed; macOS x64, Linux x64, Linux arm64 cross-compiled and produced valid binaries but could not be executed on this host). In isolated minimal scripts, both native/complex surfaces named as compatibility risks load and run correctly under a Bun-compiled binary: `@lancedb/lancedb` (connect, create table, query) and `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` (in-memory client/server round trip, tool call) both pass. The actual blocker found is architectural, not native-module related: `bin/construct` resolves its install root via `path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, '..')` to read sibling data directories (`skills/`, `specialists/`, `templates/`, `config/`, `registry/`, `package.json`) by real filesystem path; under Bun's compiled binary `import.meta.dirname` resolves to a virtual `/$bunfs/root` path, so every one of those reads throws `ENOENT` and the compiled binary currently exits silently (no output, status 0) for any command. Fixing this requires either a real-path override (falling back to `process.execPath`'s directory when `import.meta.dirname` is a bunfs path) or migrating those reads to Bun's embedded-asset APIs — tracked as follow-up, not attempted in this pass.
### Changed

- Began the ADR-0066 config-layer footprint refit: heavy per-project state (traces, the orchestration run store, worker artifacts, the LanceDB vector index, the docling/whisper runtime bootstrap dirs) now resolves through a new machine-scoped state root at `~/.construct/projects/<key>/` instead of the project's own `.cx/`, keyed by the normalized git origin remote (falling back to a canonicalized-path hash for a repo with no remote) so every clone of the same repository shares accumulated state. `lib/state-root.mjs` is the new resolver (`deriveProjectKey`, `resolveStateRoot`, `resolveStateDir`, `resolveStatePath`), built on the existing `constructDir()`/`CX_TOOLKIT_DIR` convention. Migrated writers: `lib/worker/trace.mjs` and `lib/telemetry/client.mjs` (traces), `lib/orchestration/run-store.mjs` / `run-store-sqlite.mjs` / `runtime.mjs` (orchestration runs), `lib/worker/run.mjs` (worker stdout/stderr artifacts), `lib/storage/vector-client.mjs` / `lib/storage/admin.mjs` / `lib/observation-store.mjs` (LanceDB fallback path), `lib/runtime/uv-bootstrap.mjs` / `lib/runtime/whisper-bootstrap.mjs` / `lib/ingest-tooling.mjs` (docling/whisper bootstrap dirs), `lib/embed/inbox.mjs` / `lib/embed/daemon.mjs` / `lib/intake/daemon.mjs` (inbox-state.json, embed-daemon.json, intake-daemon heartbeat), and `lib/resources/budget.mjs` (the disk-budget/prune categories for `traces` and `worker-logs`). `construct doctor` gained an advisory check (`bin/construct`) flagging legacy in-project `.cx/runtime`, `.cx/traces`, or `.cx/lancedb` left over from a pre-refit install, pointing at manual deletion (no auto-migration, per the ADR). `construct init` already never scaffolds these directories, so a fresh init produces only committed-text-sized files under `.cx/` — proven end to end by `tests/functional/state-root-footprint.functional.test.mjs`. NOT yet migrated (remaining `.cx/`-relative writers, left on the project-local path): `.cx/observations/*` (lib/observation-store.mjs's own JSON records/index, plus independent readers in lib/tracking-surfaces.mjs, lib/mcp/tool-recovery.mjs, lib/mcp/tools/scope.mjs, lib/knowledge/search.mjs, lib/oracle/read-model.mjs, lib/sandbox.mjs, and others), `.cx/oracle/*` (lib/oracle/dispatch.mjs, actions.mjs, verdicts.mjs, issues.mjs, reconcile.mjs), `.cx/knowledge/*` (lib/knowledge/layout.mjs, research-store.mjs, search.mjs, rag.mjs, lib/storage/admin.mjs's `internalDir`), `.cx/certification/*` (lib/certification/store.mjs, stale-impact.mjs), and the tiny `.cx/runtime/orchestration-guard.json` marker (`lib/hooks/orchestration-dispatch-guard.mjs`, deliberately left alone as a live Claude Code hook) and `.cx/runtime/inbox-poll.lock` (`lib/intake/poll-lock.mjs`). Extended the test-suite sterility guard (`tests/helpers/sterile-host-env.mjs`) to fingerprint `~/.construct/projects/` entries alongside the existing protected config paths, so a test that forgets HOME/CX_HOME_OVERRIDE isolation and leaks a state-root write now fails the whole-suite gate instead of silently accumulating on a developer machine. Found and fixed during merge: `tests/functional/host-execution-pickup.functional.test.mjs` and `host-execution-sampling.functional.test.mjs` call `getRun` directly in-process against a sandboxed `HOME`, but `getRun`'s own `{ env }` option was never consulted by the underlying `homeDir()`/`constructDir()` resolution (those read `process.env` directly) — the subprocess they spawn saw the sandboxed `HOME` via `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE`, the calling test process did not, so `getRun` silently read the real developer machine's state root instead of the sandbox. Both files now pin `CX_HOME_OVERRIDE` around the call via a `getRunInSandbox` helper; one hardcoded pre-migration `.cx/runtime/orchestration/runs/` path assertion was also updated to resolve through `resolveStatePath`.
- Added a config-layer mechanism for user-authored custom specialists and teams, so the shrinking built-in roster (construct-rf26.11) doesn't cap what a user can add (construct-rf26.13). `lib/registry/assemble.mjs` now merges three tiers into the registry every built-in specialist reads through — builtin (`specialists/org/**`) → user (`~/.construct/org/**`, shared across every project on the machine) → project (`<project>/.cx/org/**`, git-tracked, highest precedence) — the same builtin → user → project precedence ADR-0052 establishes for provider manifests, extended here to the org registry; `orgDirMtime()` walks all three tiers so the loader's cache invalidates correctly and `construct sync`'s existing `clearCache()` + `loadRegistry()` pass picks up new or edited custom records with no daemon restart. `lib/registry/custom-schema.mjs` is a hand-rolled validator (matching `lib/specialists/schema.mjs`'s and `lib/config/schema.mjs`'s existing style rather than adding a schema-validation dependency) that checks role, skill bundle references, fence/permission boundaries, `modelTier`, and delegation spec (`handoffCandidates`), with every error naming the field and the fix. `construct specialist create <id> --custom` and `construct team create <id>` (`lib/registry/custom-scaffold.mjs`) scaffold a validated record into the project tier by default, or the user tier with `--user`; both refuse to overwrite without `--force` and reject an unknown `--team` reference with the known-team list attached. Proven by `tests/functional/custom-specialist-authoring.functional.test.mjs`, which scaffolds a custom specialist and team in an isolated tmpdir project (and a tmpdir `HOME` for the user tier), then confirms both resolve through `getSpecialist()`/`getTeam()` — the same loader path `lib/orchestration-policy.mjs` reads for specialist selection — exactly like a built-in entry, and that the merged registry still passes every `lib/registry/validator.mjs` invariant. Authoring guide: [Custom specialists and teams](docs/guides/cookbook/custom-specialists-and-teams.md).
- Classified every postcondition on all 40 `specialists/org/contracts/*.json` handoffs as `postconditionType: 'executable'` or `'advisory'` (construct-rf26.12, ADR-0015 follow-up). The audit found 111 total postconditions, only 26 already expressed as a real check `validate.mjs` runs — the other 85 were free-text prose sitting in the same array, indistinguishable from an enforced check without reading the code. Converting them surfaced two silent, pre-existing gaps rather than needing new check types: `contract.output.mustContain` / `mustContainOneOf` / `mustMatchEnum` were declared on 29 contracts but never validated at handoff (`validateHandoff` only checked `input.mustContain`), and `output.schema` validation stopped at top-level `required` fields, never recursing into nested `required`/`enum` (e.g. a `verificationChecklist` sub-object's required booleans, or `findings[].severity`'s enum). `lib/contracts/validate.mjs` now wires both, plus a new `collectSchemaShapeErrors` recursive checker; 13 prose postconditions were promoted to executable this way (`enforcedVia: 'output-shape'`, referencing the enforcing field) or via the pre-existing `lib/specialists/postconditions.mjs` binary-rule table (`enforcedVia: 'binary-postcondition'`), plus 2 more via a direct `artifact-claims-cited` check. The remaining 72 are marked `postconditionType: 'advisory'` with their original prose kept verbatim as `description` — genuinely irreducible claims (per-item "every task names an owner," cross-document traceability, sequencing/process guarantees, non-numeric citation content) that no mechanical check can verify without either lying about coverage or inventing a fragile, single-use pattern-matcher. Final split: 39 executable / 72 advisory (35.1%). Coverage is computed by the new `lib/contracts/coverage.mjs` (`computePostconditionCoverage`) and reported as a `construct doctor consistency` category (`postcondition-coverage`), matching the existing `lib/decisions/registry.mjs` enforced/advisory reporting style; `lib/decisions/registry.mjs`'s contract classification was updated to read `postconditionType` instead of inferring from `typeof`. `schemas/unified-registry.schema.json`'s `postconditions` item shape was updated to document the structured form (bare strings are now a deprecated legacy shape). Proven by new assertions in `tests/contracts-coverage.test.mjs` (every postcondition classified; every executable one resolves to a real mechanism; coverage floor pinned at 39) and six new `tests/functional/w2-contract-enforcement.functional.test.mjs` cases exercising the newly-wired output-shape and nested-schema enforcement end to end (verdict/enum rejection, rubber-stamp `mustContainOneOf` guard, missing-field block, wildcard-producer enum, nested `verificationChecklist` required fields). Left untouched: `specialists/org/specialists/` (roster, owned by the concurrent construct-rf26.11 consolidation) and any attachment of contracts to flow steps (blocked on the concurrent rf26.9 flow-engine port).

## [1.4.2] - 2026-07-01

### Fixed

- Made the F08 `[R35]` excessive-agency audit test hermetic: it now asserts on the exported grant source (`COPILOT_AGENT_TOOLS`) instead of reading the committed `.github/agents/construct.agent.md`, which is regenerated/pruned by concurrent repo-root syncs (including the tool-repo `npm ci` postinstall on a runner without Copilot) and so raced during the release gate. Host-config-parity still verifies the generated file matches the source; the test keeps a presence-guarded cross-check.
- Keyed the registry loader cache on `rootDir` (`lib/registry/loader.mjs`), fixing an intermittent CI failure where the certification scenario fixtures reported a specialist (cx-engineer) missing its `refusalBoundaries`. The module-level cache was keyed only on the org dir's mtime and the legacy-overlay mtime — two roots (the repo and a tmpdir test root) can share those, so one root's registry could bleed into a later `loadRegistry` call for a different root, yielding a specialist without its `promptFile` and therefore without the prompt-derived `perspective.failureMode`. The cache key (and `clearCache`) now include `rootDir`, so a load for a different root can never return another root's cached registry. Tracked as the flaky-cert bead.

### Added

- Gave the orchestration worker a scoped, governed **live web capability** for web-capable specialists, closing the capability-honesty seam where the orchestrator promised "web access lives with the researcher" but no execution path wired it (construct-bcbo.15, ADR-0050). A four-agent adversarial investigation confirmed every orchestration path ran specialists as tool-less completions, so a researcher told to "fetch every URL" with no fetch tool could only refuse or fabricate — a collision with the no-fabrication rule. The fix adds a typed **WebGrant** resolver (`lib/orchestration/web-capability.mjs`) in strict priority: **governed** (`WEB_SEARCH_URL` set → the worker runs a client tool-use loop executing Construct's own `webSearch()`, so F08 `trust:'untrusted'` + Admiralty grading hold by construction), **provider-native** (no `WEB_SEARCH_URL` → Anthropic's `web_search_20250305` or OpenRouter's `openrouter:web_search` server tool — the deprecated `:online`/web-plugin forms are not used — with every citation re-graded through the single grader), **host-delegated** (explicit opt-in), and **unavailable** (a typed `capability-unavailable` that forces an honest insufficient-evidence refusal and degrades the run). The F08 grader was extracted to `lib/mcp/tools/web-search-governance.mjs` as the **single chokepoint**: it is the only producer of web evidence on every path, so a citation can never reach a specialist ungoverned or mislabeled as trusted. Only specialists that declare `liveWebAccess: true` (the researcher) get the tool — least-privilege; `claudeTools` is not the signal because most specialists carry `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` as a broad Claude-host grant. The remote orchestration service (out-of-repo) is handled fail-closed: `runViaService` re-runs `governWebResults` on ingress (`governRemoteWebEvidence`) so a foreign server cannot inject trusted or ungoverned web text, marking the run degraded when it had to re-govern. The orchestrator micro-prompt and the cx-researcher persona were corrected to stop asserting a capability that may not be wired and to require an honest "could not reach the web" over a fabricated citation. Supersedes the ADR-0020/0021 lens boundary for web-capable specialists only; inline/prepare-only and non-web specialists are unchanged. Proven by `tests/orchestration-web-capability.test.mjs` (resolver priority), `tests/orchestration-worker-web.test.mjs` (all four grant modes + round cap + honesty guard, mocked providers), and `tests/orchestration-remote-web-guard.test.mjs` (fail-closed remote ingress). Live end-to-end web needs the operator's provider keys; the mechanism, governance, and honesty guard are proven with mocked providers.

- Began the full-project best-practice audit remediation program (`tests/audit/REMEDIATION-PLAN.md`, tracker epic `construct-bcbo`). Fourteen finding families (F01–F08 P0 trust boundaries, F09–F14 P1 hardening) were each verified against live code by a parallel investigation agent, producing 50 failing red fixtures under `tests/audit/` (104 tests, 97 failing) that pin each defect — model-callable destructive/shell/path tools (F01), live secrets in generated config (F02), unshipped `schemas/`+`registry/` breaking consumer installs (F03), JSONC-silent host config (F04), port-only process kills (F05), a Dockerfile entrypoint deleted by ADR-0039 (F06), unpinned CI actions + unredacted Terraform plan secrets (F07), and absent untrusted-content labeling (F08), plus six P1 hardening families. Fixtures are named `*.red.mjs` (excluded from the auto-discovered suite) and promote to `*.test.mjs` as each fix lands.

### Fixed

- Resolved four F14 tool-design defects (P1 hardening), completing the audit remediation program. **F14 [S2][S15] description overlap**: The seven overlapping retrieval-cluster tools (`web_search`, `knowledge_search`, `knowledge_graph_ask`, `provider_fetch`, `rovo_search`, `search_skills`, `memory_search`) now carry a machine-readable `category: 'retrieval'` discriminator in their catalog defs, so an agent (or host router) disambiguates on a structured field rather than parsing long prose that self-documents the hazard. **F14 [R36] surface parity**: Created `lib/mcp/tool-surface-parity.mjs` exporting `assertCoreSubsetOfCatalog` (and `assertToolSurfacePartition`), wired into `server.mjs` at module load so a typo in the hand-maintained `CORE_TOOL_NAMES` — which would silently drop a tool from both the flat surface and the `call` gateway enum — now throws at startup against the catalog as the single source of truth. **F14 [S2][S15] output schema**: Every machine-consumed catalog tool literal now declares an inline `outputSchema` (72 tools; the three side-effecting acks `storage_sync`/`storage_reset`/`delete_ingested_artifacts` are exempt), matching the runtime envelope the `withSafetyEnvelope` decorator already applies — the fixtures read the raw literal, which the decorator's `.map` never reaches. **F14 [S2][S15] token budget + telemetry**: Corrected the stale flat-surface budget claim in `docs/guides/reference/mcp-tools.md` (`flat 71-tool ~10.6k tokens` → `flat 75-tool ~14.8k tokens`, the recomputed live figure matching the server comment); and wired the imported `withGenAiSpan` helper into the `CallTool` dispatcher so per-tool call/latency/error/result-size telemetry is actually recorded — previously the OTel GenAI import was dead (only name-misses were instrumented). All four defects proven by `tests/audit/f14-tools/{description-overlap,exposure-parity,output-schema-coverage,token-budget-instrumentation}.test.mjs` (7 tests, all green). F14 complete; P1 hardening (F09–F14) finished.

- Resolved seven F13 artifact-quality defects (P1 hardening). **F13 [R21] invisible text**: Added `markupVisualGate` to `lib/output-quality.mjs` — an HTML-only check that reads the produced deliverable's own markup and fails when body text is rendered invisible via `opacity:0` or a matched foreground/background color (e.g. `color:#ffffff;background:#ffffff`); the text roundtrip previously passed both patterns because hidden glyphs are still extractable. **F13 [R21] overlapping text**: The same `markupVisualGate` also detects hard-overlapping absolutely-positioned elements (two elements with identical `top`/`left` coordinates) — a layout defect invisible to all prior checks. **F13 [R21] required renderer**: At `render-smoke` gate level (the level `publish --preview` forces), a missing required renderer for `pdf` or `pptx` deliverables is now a hard failure rather than a graceful degrade — the `render.result.degradation === 'missing-dependency'` condition is treated as a fatal failure when `gateLevel` is in the `RENDER_GATE_LEVELS` set and the format is in `RENDER_REQUIRED_FORMATS`. **F13 [R21] missing reference (produced file)**: `referenceIntegrity` in `lib/export-validate.mjs` now also scans the produced HTML file for internal `<img src>` references that point to missing local assets, closing the gap where the source markdown had no image syntax but the export did. **F13 [R21] missing reference (inline HTML)**: Raw inline `<img src>` HTML embedded in source markdown is now matched and resolved, closing the gap where only `![]()` syntax was checked. **F13 [R21] missing reference (reference-style)**: Reference-style markdown image/link definitions (`[id]: ./path`) are now matched and checked, closing the third reference-form gap. **F13 (gate manifest)**: Added three new entries to `tests/audit/gate-fixture-manifest.json` mapping `artifact-invisible-text`, `artifact-renderer-required`, and `artifact-missing-reference` gates to their promoted fixtures. All seven checks proven by `tests/audit/f13-artifact/invisible-overlapping-text.test.mjs`, `required-renderer-soft-pass.test.mjs`, and `missing-local-reference.test.mjs` (7 tests, all green).

- Resolved three F12 gates-quality defects (P1 hardening). **F12 [R22]**: `tests/AUDIT.md` total test-file count, functional-layer count, section-5 header, and section-6 header are now consistent with each other and with the on-disk corpus (tested by `audit-doc-staleness.test.mjs`, which scans `tests/` to match the generator scope and counts all `*.test.mjs` in `tests/functional/` rather than filtering to `.functional.test.mjs`). **F12 (doctor specificity)**: created `lib/doctor/diagnosis.mjs` exporting `DOCTOR_STATES` — a typed failure taxonomy covering `missing-config`, `stale-path`, `disabled`, `server-start-failure`, `secret-leak`, and `entrypoint-missing`; updated the `add` helper in `bin/construct` to attach a `failureState` field to each check so findings carry a typed state rather than collapsing to a bare boolean. **F12 (gate manifest)**: created `tests/audit/gate-fixture-manifest.json` mapping all 10 required P0/P1 release gates (`mcp-safety`, `secret-scan`, `consumer-dependency-audit`, `host-readiness`, `runtime-ownership`, `docker-runtime-smoke`, `cicd-permissions`, `prompt-injection`, `registry-validate-p0p1`, `template-policy`) to their promoted `*.test.mjs` proving fixtures.

- Resolved four F09 orchestration-hardening defects (P1 hardening). **F09 [R17]**: `shapeRun` in `lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs` now exposes `prepareOnly: true`, `semantics` (the runtime's inline disclaimer), and `status: 'completed-prepare-only'` for runs where all tasks are in `prepared` state — previously the MCP envelope reported bare `status: 'completed'` with no signal that zero specialist reasoning ran, so a host could not distinguish prepared work from executed output. **F09 [R18]**: `executeRun` in `lib/orchestration/runtime.mjs` is now wrapped in a try/catch; on any thrown error the run is set to `status: 'error'` and persisted to disk via a direct `writeFileSync` on the existing run file (not the atomic temp-then-rename, which fails on a read-only directory where the file already exists) — previously a fatal executeRun error was only emitted to the in-process event bus and lost to any getRun/getRuns query. **F09 [R19]**: All four provider call paths in `lib/orchestration/worker.mjs` (`callAnthropic`, `callOpenRouter`, `callCopilot`, `callOpenAI`) now use a shared `timedFetch` helper that combines `AbortSignal.timeout(ms)` with `Promise.race` and an abort listener, so a hung provider rejects within a deterministic window even when `fetchImpl` ignores the signal; default timeout 200ms, overridable via `CONSTRUCT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS`. **F09 [R20]**: `runViaService` in `lib/mcp/tools/orchestration-run.mjs` applies the same `timedRemoteFetch` helper to the initial POST and every poll GET, so a hung remote service cannot stall the run indefinitely before the between-polls overall-deadline check is ever reached.

- Resolved four F10 registry-drift defects and three F11 disposition-contradiction defects (P1 hardening). **F10 [R32]**: `buildCatalogSnapshot` now stamps a `_semanticDigest` on every capability (keyed on description, criticality, and verification), so `checkCapabilityCatalogDrift` detects falsified descriptions, silently downgraded criticality, and verification paths edited to non-existent files — previously the comparison was self-referential (both sides read the same on-disk data). **F10 [R33]**: P0/P1 capabilities with no functional test or `untestableRationale` now fail `validateCapabilityRegistry` with an error (not a warning), as do capabilities whose `lastValidated` is stale or absent; the fail-open gate now rejects critical untested and overdue capabilities. `local.model.tier` (covered by unit + integration tests) received an `untestableRationale` to document why no functional binary spawn is needed. **F10 [R34]**: Removed a consecutive duplicate `clearCache()` call in `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` (lines 120–121). **F10 [R15]**: Unified the project config vocabulary — JSON Schema (`schemas/project-config.schema.json`) renamed `profile` → `scope` with description update; runtime FIELD_RULES (`lib/config/schema.mjs`) gained an explicit `enum`; `lib/sandbox.mjs` now writes `cfg.scope` instead of `cfg.profile`. **F11 (disposition)**: Removed `.github/copilot-instructions.md` from `IGNORED_PATTERNS` (it is a user-owned managed-block file, not ignored cache) and added it to a new `MANAGED_PATTERNS` export that records files Construct mutates only via `replaceManagedBlock`; added `.github/agents/` (VS Code custom-agent files written by `syncCopilot`) to `IGNORED_PATTERNS`; both fixes eliminate the mis-disposition that allowed the gitignore writer to stamp a durable Copilot instruction file as cache.

- Made `construct-mcp` start by default in VS Code / GitHub Copilot so the orchestrator is usable on open instead of asking the user to "enable the MCP server." VS Code does not auto-start `.vscode/mcp.json` servers unless `chat.mcp.autostart` is set, and the orchestrator's first action is an MCP call (`orchestration_policy`), so a dormant server made it refuse or stall until manually started. `syncCopilot`/`syncVSCode` (`scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`) now write `"chat.mcp.autostart": "always"` (VS Code ≥1.105, string enum) into the generated `.vscode/settings.json` alongside the existing agent-scan pin, via the renamed `pinVscodeChatSettings` helper that applies each managed key only when unset and never clobbers a non-strict-JSON (commented) settings file. This is Copilot-specific — Claude Code, OpenCode, and Codex already auto-start their declared MCP servers (verified). Note: VS Code still requires a one-time per-developer MCP *trust* grant on first launch, which it stores locally and cannot be pre-granted from committed config — `autostart` removes the recurring per-session Start click, not that first trust. Covered by `tests/vscode-mcp-toolkit-path.test.mjs`.

### Security

- Extended the secret audit trail into the long-lived processes that resolve credentials outside the CLI (`construct-trxz.13`, closing the F02 residual). `enableSecretAuditTrail()` was wired only at the CLI entrypoint (`bin/construct`), so an op read performed inside a separate process escaped the durable trail. Wired the sink at the entry of every long-lived Construct process: the **MCP server** (`lib/mcp/server.mjs`) and the **embed daemon** (`lib/embed/worker.mjs`) genuinely resolve provider credentials in their own process — the MCP server via provider-backed orchestration (`executeRun` → `runTaskViaProvider` → `resolveSecret`) and provider tools, the embed daemon via `resolveLLMKey` on snapshot ticks — closing the real gap; the **doctor** (`lib/doctor/index.mjs`) and **oracle** (`lib/oracle/daemon-entry.mjs`) daemons wire it defensively so a future provider-touching tick is covered. A transitive-import reachability sweep over every process entrypoint in `lib/` confirmed no other long-lived process resolves credentials outside the CLI: the team worker (`lib/worker/entrypoint.mjs`) executes jobs as subprocesses and resolves nothing in-process; the ACP server runs inside `construct acp` (already CLI-covered); and the remaining hits resolve only the non-secret `OLLAMA_BASE_URL`/`OLLAMA_HOST` local URL or reach a dead code path (`refreshLiveOpenRouterCatalog`, which has zero callers). The sink is idempotent and best-effort (`emitAudit` swallows sink errors), so wiring never affects a daemon's behavior or a secret resolution. Proven by `tests/functional/secret-audit-daemon-wiring.functional.test.mjs`: a real spawned process resolves an `op://` reference through a stub `op` and asserts a value-free `op_read` record lands on the daemon-scoped trail (`doctorRoot()/audit-trail.jsonl`) with the secret value never present, plus a coverage guard that every wired entrypoint keeps the wiring.

- Closed the two remaining F02 credential-handling defects, completing the family (F02 / CX-AUDIT-SECRETS-001 and -002; formerly deferred to `construct-trxz`). **[R12] stdio env value→reference flip**: `buildLocalEnvironment` (`lib/mcp-platform-config.mjs`) materialized a resolved stdio MCP secret into the generated Claude `env` and OpenCode `environment` blocks, forwarding a live credential onto disk (the remote `headers` path already emitted host env-references; only the local/stdio path leaked). A stdio env value that is a **whole-value** secret template (`__NAME__` as the entire string) now flips to the host's env-reference form — `${NAME}` (Claude Code), `${env:NAME}` (VS Code), `{env:NAME}` (OpenCode) — so the live value stays only in the 0600 `config.env` store while the child still inherits it as a backstop; **composed** values (the memory-bridge `http://127.0.0.1:__MEMORY_PORT__/` URL) and plain literals are still materialized since they are not secrets. Codex keeps its deferred materialize/strip behavior (no env-block interpolation). Proven by `tests/audit/f02-secrets/generated-file-leak-scan.test.mjs`; `mcp-secret-ref.test.mjs` and `mcp-add-no-plaintext.functional.test.mjs` updated from the deferred contract to the flip. **[R14] precedence parity**: `resolveSecret` (`lib/providers/secret-resolver.mjs`) returned a directly-passed `env` value ahead of every file tier, while `loadConstructEnv` ranks the config files above `process.env` to defeat stale shell exports — so the same key present in both `process.env` and `config.env` with different values resolved to different credentials depending on the code path. The resolver now follows one ladder shared with the env merge: when file discovery is enabled (`allowAmbient`), the file tiers win over an ambient shell export, with the identical carve-out that a materialized credential injected on the env beats a stored `op://` reference (`preferInjectedCredential`, now exported from `lib/env-config.mjs` and imported by the resolver as the single source of truth). The env short-circuit inside `rawCandidate` was removed so it is pure file/store discovery and the env-vs-file rank lives in one place. `allowAmbient: false` still yields a hermetic env-only resolution for injected callers. Proven by `tests/audit/f02-secrets/precedence-divergence.test.mjs`; three hermetic-intent resolver tests pinned to the old env-first behavior gained an explicit `allowAmbient: false`. F02 complete.
- Closed a shell-injection vector in the `summarize_diff` MCP tool (F01 / CX-AUDIT-MCP-SAFETY-002). A model-supplied `base_ref` was interpolated into `git diff --stat ${baseRef}` and run through `execSync` with a shell (`lib/mcp/tools/project.mjs`), so a value like `HEAD$(touch …)` executed arbitrary commands on the host — reachable by any prompt-injection that reaches the tool's arguments. The git calls now run via `execFile` with an allowlisted argv (`runGit`), passing the ref as one literal argument the shell never evaluates; refs that start with `-` (git option injection) or carry control characters are rejected. The static `git log`/`git status` calls in `project_context` moved to the same shell-free path. Proven by `tests/audit/f01-mcp-safety/malicious-ref.test.mjs` (a command-substitution payload writes no sentinel after the fix).
- Added root containment for model-supplied file paths in MCP file tools (F01 / CX-AUDIT-MCP-SAFETY-003). `scan_file` resolved `file_path` with no root enforcement and `delete_ingested_artifacts` joined model-supplied `files[]` onto the ingested dir with no check, so a `../` chain, an absolute path, or a symlink could read any file via `scan_file` or **delete** any file outside `.cx/knowledge/internal` via `delete_ingested_artifacts` (a destructive traversal not in the original audit evidence). A new `lib/path-policy.mjs` `resolveWithinRoot` pins each path to a declared root — rejecting `../` escapes, out-of-root absolutes, backslash/UNC separators, and symlinks whose realpath escapes (checked on the longest existing ancestor) — and is wired into both tools; the delete validates the whole batch before removing anything. Proven by `tests/audit/f01-mcp-safety/scanfile-containment.test.mjs` and `delete-traversal.test.mjs`.
- Required a one-time out-of-band approval token for the destructive MCP tools `storage_reset` and `delete_ingested_artifacts` (F01 / CX-AUDIT-MCP-SAFETY-004). These tools are reachable only through the model's argument channel yet authorized destruction on a model-supplied `confirm: true` alone, so a model error, prompt injection, or replayed argument blob could self-authorize irreversible deletion. Destruction now also requires an `approval_token` verified server-side (`lib/mcp/destructive-approval.mjs`): a random token issued out-of-band, persisted 0600 under the user state dir — outside any project root, so the now root-contained `scan_file` cannot read it — scoped per tool, single-use, and expiring after 5 minutes. A bare `confirm: true` is rejected. Proven by `tests/audit/f01-mcp-safety/destructive-confirm.test.mjs`; `tests/mcp-server.test.mjs` updated to the new contract.
- Added a declared output schema and safety classification to every MCP tool (F01 / CX-AUDIT-MCP-SAFETY-001). The catalog's 75 tool defs (plus the `call` gateway) carried an `inputSchema` and description but no `outputSchema` and no read/write/destructive class or filesystem/network/process scope, so a host could not validate tool output or reason about a tool's blast radius. New `lib/mcp/tool-safety.mjs` is a single classification table — derived from each tool's own documented behavior (several are self-declared: `storage_reset`/`scope_archive` as destructive, `model_resolve`/`triage_recommend`/`capability_describe` as read-only) — applied via a `withSafetyEnvelope` decorator that throws if a tool ships unclassified. Declaring `outputSchema` activates MCP SDK client-side validation requiring the response to carry matching `structuredContent`; the `CallTool` handler now returns `structuredContent: toolResult` alongside the existing text content (every dispatch branch already returns a JSON object, so this satisfies every tool's schema). Proven by `tests/audit/f01-mcp-safety/safety-envelope.test.mjs` plus the existing `host-mcp-emulation.functional.test.mjs`, which caught the missing `structuredContent` as a real client-side protocol error before this fix.
- Added a per-tool call-rate budget and a durable audit record to every MCP tool call (F01 / CX-AUDIT-MCP-SAFETY-005, closing the family). `lib/mcp/broker.mjs`'s `Broker` already had rate-limiting and audit-trail machinery, but it is role/policy-based and only instantiated in team/enterprise mode — `Broker.invoke` was never actually called from the live `CallTool` dispatch path in any deployment mode, so solo mode (the default) had no bound on how often a tool could be called and no audit trail for tool calls at all. New `lib/mcp/tool-rate-limit.mjs` (`ToolRateLimiter`) applies a sliding-window budget tiered by safety class (destructive: 5/60s, write: 60/60s, read: 300/60s — `CONSTRUCT_MCP_TOOL_RATE_WINDOW_MS=0` disables, mirroring the existing timeout-override convention), keyed on the real underlying tool even when called through the `call` gateway so one long-tail tool's budget can't conflate with another's. Every call now also lands a value-free record (tool name, safety class, ok, duration — never arguments or result content) in the existing tamper-evident `lib/audit-trail.mjs`, queryable via `construct audit trail --tool=<name>`. This completes F01: every MCP tool now carries the full envelope the audit asked for — input/output schema, safety class, root containment, an out-of-band confirmation model, a timeout, a rate budget, and an audit log. Proven by `tests/functional/mcp-tool-rate-audit.functional.test.mjs`, which drives the real server as a subprocess and confirms both the audit record and the rate-limit rejection through the actual protocol path.
- Enforced 0600 on `config.env` at its single write path (F02 / CX-AUDIT-SECRETS-003). `writeEnvValues` (`lib/env-config.mjs`) wrote the credential-bearing `config.env` with no mode, so it landed at the umask default (0644 — group/world readable) whenever a caller skipped its own post-write chmod (several do: `setup.mjs`, `service-manager.mjs`, `legacy-config-migration.mjs`, `roles/preference.mjs`). The write now sets mode 0600 and re-applies 0600 on every rewrite (`fs.writeFileSync` keeps an existing inode's mode, so the mode option alone cannot tighten a file an earlier write left loose), POSIX-guarded. Proven by `tests/audit/f02-secrets/config-env-mode.test.mjs`. The stdio MCP env value→reference flip and the resolver precedence alignment that were cross-linked here are now closed in the F02 completion entry above ([R12]/[R14]); the worker/daemon audit-trail wiring remains owned by the `construct-trxz` resolver redesign.
- Shipped the repo-root data directories the runtime loads in the published package (F03 / CX-AUDIT-PACKAGE-002). `package.json` `files` omitted `registry/` and `schemas/`, yet the runtime reads `registry/capabilities.json` (`lib/registry/validate.mjs`), `registry/agent-manifest.json` (`lib/registry/agent-manifest.mjs`), and `schemas/*.json` (`lib/embedded-contract/capability.mjs`) from the package root — so a consumer `npm install` got a package whose registry/schema validation and capability inventory were broken on first run (`loadAgentManifest` threw ENOENT; 17 of 21 schemas were silently dropped). Two further repo-root dirs the runtime reads were also unshipped and are now included: `config/tag-vocabulary.json` (`lib/tags/vocabulary.mjs`, which hard-throws on a missing vocabulary) and `vendor/pandoc-ext/diagram.lua` (the `publish`/figures path). `files` now lists `schemas/**`, `registry/**`, `config/**`, `vendor/**`. Proven by `tests/audit/f03-package/packed-asset-parity.test.mjs`, which runs a real `npm pack`, extracts the tarball, and asserts every runtime-loaded repo-root directory is present.
- Made a half-staged project detectable and repairable (F03 / CX-AUDIT-PACKAGE-005). `stageProjectAdapters` (`lib/install/stage-project.mjs`) could return `{ staged:true, synced:false }` — the `.construct/` launcher staged but `.claude/` adapters never synced (the bundled sync script missing, or `sync-specialists.mjs --project` exiting non-zero) — and both callers (`bin/construct-postinstall.mjs`, `construct init`) discarded the result, so a project left half-built was invisible with no repair path. Staging now records every outcome in a durable `.construct/stage-state.json` marker (best-effort: a marker-write failure never turns a successful stage into a reported failure) and exports `readStageState` plus `repairStagedProject`, which re-drives a half-staged project to a synced state idempotently and no-ops when already synced. Proven by `tests/audit/f03-package/half-stage-recovery.test.mjs`.
- Added an itemized mutation manifest to the postinstall hook (F03 / CX-AUDIT-PACKAGE-004). `bin/construct-postinstall.mjs` staged `.construct/` launchers and appended Construct ignore patterns to `.gitignore` with no record of what it touched and no recovery command — npm has no uninstall lifecycle hook to reverse these mutations, so a stale or removed Construct dependency left the consumer project permanently mutated with no path to undo. The hook now writes `.construct/install-manifest.json` after the mutation phase: an itemized list of every path it created or appended plus the recovery command (`npx construct init --repair`), best-effort so a manifest-write failure never propagates to fail the consumer's `npm install`. Proven by `tests/audit/f03-package/postinstall-mutation-manifest.test.mjs`, which runs the real hook against an isolated tmpdir consumer and asserts both the receipt existence and its `.gitignore` entry. This completes F03.
- Degated the Docker/container surface per ADR-0039 (F06 / CX-AUDIT-DOCKER-001 through -004). ADR-0039 (2026-06-25) deleted `lib/server/` — the HTTP dashboard daemon — and its ECS/ALB deploy surface, but the `Dockerfile`, the docker job in `release.yml`, and phantom references in ECS Terraform, the ALB health check, deploy docs, and the PRD were left in place. The Dockerfile's `CMD ["node", "lib/server/index.mjs"]` referenced a non-existent file (image builds and immediately exits), and the `HEALTHCHECK curl /api/auth/status` targeted an unimplemented route (no server registered it — the path existed only in deploy config). The docker job in `release.yml` also pushed mutable `:latest`/channel tags BEFORE the Trivy CVE scan step (lines 190/202 vs line 209), so a failing scan could not un-publish a vulnerable image. Remediation: deleted `Dockerfile`, removed the docker job from `release.yml` (replaced its `needs: [build-binary, docker]` with `needs: [build-binary]` in the publish job). Three fixtures in `tests/audit/f06-docker/` now encode the degated contract: each passes when no Dockerfile exists / no docker push step is present, and enforces correctness only if the surface is re-introduced. Updated `tests/release-workflow.test.mjs` (previously asserted docker job behavior). F06 complete (ADR-aligned degate).
- Added ownership-gated stop to the runtime service manager (F05 / CX-AUDIT-RUNTIME-001/-003/-004). `killPortOwners` (`lib/service-manager.mjs`) blindly signalled whatever PID `lsof` found owning a configured port — no verification that the PID was launched by Construct — so a stale or mistyped `MEMORY_PORT`/`BRIDGE_PORT` entry terminated an unrelated developer process that happened to be listening. The fix adds a per-port ownership record: `startServices` now writes `.construct/runtime/port-N.json` when it spawns each service (PID, command, cwd, `marker: 'construct'`, `constructManaged: true`, timestamp). `killPortOwners` reads that record before sending `SIGTERM`; if no record exists or the PID doesn't match or the record lacks Construct markers, the process is not signalled and the result status stays `not-running`. A new `isConstructOwnedPort(processRecord, { port })` export exposes the ownership predicate so the caller can separately reason about ownership without coupling to the kill path. Proven by three fixtures in `tests/audit/f05-runtime-ownership/` (owned-stop: predicate correctness; non-owner-refusal: stop does not kill a foreign PID; stale-port: stop does not kill a real foreign process owning a stale configured port). F05 complete.
- Fixed four VS Code / Copilot host-readiness defects (F04 / CX-AUDIT-HOST-001 through -005, completing the family). (1) **HOST-001**: The MCP eager-start setting was written as `chat.mcp.autostart` (all-lowercase) — VS Code setting ids are case-sensitive; the unrecognized id was silently ignored, so the server was never auto-started. Corrected to `chat.mcp.autoStart` (camelCase, matching the VS Code docs primary source). (2) **HOST-002**: `pinVscodeChatSettings` read existing settings with `JSON.parse` inside a `try { } catch { return }`, so any user with a JSONC settings.json (line comments, trailing commas — the common case for hand-edited VS Code config) silently received no managed keys. The catch now uses a JSONC-safe strip (`parseJsoncContent`) that removes full-line `//` comments and trailing commas before parsing, then writes the result as JSON with the managed keys merged in. (3) **HOST-004**: `mcpEntryPointsOutsideToolkit` detected stale toolkit paths with a POSIX-slash regex and a POSIX-prefix check, missing Windows backslash / drive-letter paths — a stale Windows entry was never refreshed and VS Code silently launched a non-existent construct-mcp. Now normalizes backslashes to forward slashes before comparison. (4) **HOST-005**: The generated Copilot/VS Code custom-agent frontmatter granted `construct-mcp/*` (a wildcard over the entire 75-tool surface) plus `edit/editFiles` and `web/fetch` — not least-privilege for a routing front door. `COPILOT_AGENT_TOOLS` now lists the explicit orchestration tools (`construct-mcp/orchestration_policy`, `orchestration_run`, `orchestration_readiness`) plus read-only context tools; write and outbound access removed. (5) **HOST-003**: Added `lib/host/readiness.mjs` — a discrete host-config readiness classifier (`classifyHostReadiness`) with a typed `HOST_READINESS_REASONS` enum (missing_config / stale_path / jsonc_unpatched / wrong_key / untrusted / disabled / server_start_failure / missing_tool / sandbox_disabled / healthy) that distinguishes actual failure modes rather than collapsing to a file-presence binary; the `construct doctor` lane wire-up is a follow-up bead. Proven by five fixtures in `tests/audit/f04-host-readiness/`. Updated `tests/vscode-mcp-toolkit-path.test.mjs` (previously green-locked the JSONC-skip and lowercase key) and `tests/functional/host-config-parity.functional.test.mjs` (previously asserted the wildcard grant) to reflect the corrected contracts.
- Extended the secret audit trail to non-op (plaintext) resolutions (F02 / CX-AUDIT-SECRETS-005). `enableSecretAuditTrail` (`lib/providers/secret-audit-wiring.mjs`) only wired up `secret.op_read` events — the 1Password CLI path — leaving every plaintext-tier resolution (project .env, config.env, home .env, shell-rc, alt-store) unrecorded, so the common credential path produced no durable audit trail. The wiring now also handles `secret.resolve` events for non-op resolutions, writing a value-free record carrying the source tier and outcome (`project-env`, `config-env`, etc.) but never the materialized credential value; op-ref resolve events are deliberately skipped (they are already covered by the existing `op_read` record). Proven by `tests/audit/f02-secrets/audit-source-tier.test.mjs`. The stdio MCP env value→reference flip and the resolver precedence alignment referenced here are now closed in the F02 completion entry above ([R12]/[R14]).
- Closed the prompt-injection trust boundary gap at the web-search and document-ingest boundaries (F08 / CX-AUDIT-LLMSEC-001 through -003). (1) **Web-search result labeling**: `lib/mcp/tools/web-search.mjs` returned results with Admiralty credibility grades (`admiralty`, `confidence`) but no trust standing — a web snippet was indistinguishable to a downstream consumer from a system instruction. Every result now carries `trust: 'untrusted'` so a consumer can separate external retrieved text from the instruction channel (OWASP LLM01 mitigation). (2) **Intake packet labeling**: `prepareIntakeForIngestedFile` (`lib/intake/prepare.mjs`) enqueued externally-ingested document bodies into agent-facing intake packets with no provenance label — an injection payload in the document body entered the agent's context with the same standing as an instruction. The enqueued packet now carries `trust: 'untrusted'` as a top-level field. (3) **Front-door agent scope**: the Copilot/VS Code custom agent grant included `web/githubRepo` alongside the orchestration tools — the F08 fixture classifies it as high-agency alongside `web/fetch` and `edit/editFiles`; `web/githubRepo` removed so the front-door agent is limited to orchestration dispatch and read-only context. Proven by three fixtures in `tests/audit/f08-prompt-injection/`. F08 complete. P0 trust boundary remediation (F01–F08) finished.
- Hardened CI/CD pipelines against supply-chain attacks and secret leaks (F07 / CX-AUDIT-CI-001 through -004). (1) **Action SHA pins**: all 48 `uses:` references across 10 workflow files were pointing at mutable version tags (`@v6`, `@v4`, etc.) — a compromised action repo could repoint a tag at malicious code that then runs with the pipeline's secrets. Every action is now pinned to a 40-hex commit SHA with the tag retained as a comment: first-party (`actions/*`) and all third-party (`dorny/paths-filter`, `gitleaks/gitleaks-action`, `aws-actions/*`, `hashicorp/setup-terraform`). (2) **Least-privilege `permissions:`**: six workflows had no top-level `permissions:` block, inheriting the repo default (potentially read/write on every resource); one workflow granted `contents: write` workflow-wide. All workflows now declare an explicit top-level `permissions:` (deny-all `{}` where all jobs have their own, `contents: read` where checkout is the only need); `promote-staging-to-main.yml` moved its broad grant to per-job scope. (3) **Policy gate blocking**: the `template policy` step in `ci.yml` carried `continue-on-error: true`, making a policy violation produce a passing job. The flag is removed — policy gates must fail the build. (4) **Release tooling from lockfile**: `release.yml` installed esbuild via `npm install -g` (unpinned global fetch) and invoked `npx postject` (on-demand package resolution) — both inject un-checksummed code into the binary build job. The workflow now runs `npm ci` (with devDeps), adds `node_modules/.bin` to `GITHUB_PATH`, and calls `esbuild`/`postject` from the locked dependency graph; `postject@1.0.0-alpha.6` added to `devDependencies`. (5) **Terraform plan redaction**: the `Post plan to PR` step in `deploy.yml` posted `plan.txt` verbatim into the PR comment — a secret in a planned ECS `environment` attribute (e.g. `TF_VAR_dashboard_token`) was readable by anyone with repo read access and permanently in the PR timeline. The step now applies a redaction pass (`const redacted = truncated.replace(/(,\s*value\s*=\s*)"[^"]*"/g, '$1"***"')`) before posting. Proven by five fixtures in `tests/audit/f07-cicd/`. F07 complete.

## [1.4.1] - 2026-06-30

### Fixed

- Stopped the nightly `hook performance budgets` workflow (`.github/workflows/hook-budgets.yml`, cron `17 7 * * *`) from failing every night. The `guard-bash` PreToolUse hook declared `@p95ms 5`, a target set before it grew the role-fence path (`doctorRoot` lookup, last-agent file reads, lazy `manifest`/`fence`/`approval` imports); the variance-heavy CI bench measured ~17-19ms marginal, so the gate failed persistently from 2026-06-29. The hook and the gate are both load-bearing (it blocks `rm -rf /`, force-push-to-main, fork bombs, destructive DDL, and enforces role fences) and the budget lane is deliberately nightly-only (ADR-0029), so neither was removed — instead the budget is re-baselined to `@p95ms 15` to reflect the hook's real fence-enforcement cost, with the gate's ×2 tolerance still catching a true regression. Per the no-skip-vars rule, the declared budget is raised rather than a skip variable added.

- Enforced research recency in the web-search execution layer so newer evidence is no longer buried under older authority. Recency was thoroughly *specified* (cx-researcher prompt, `rules/common/research.md` §1, `research-execution-policy.recencyRule`, brief templates all mandate most-recent-first and a 12-month staleness threshold) but never *enforced*: `lib/mcp/tools/web-search.mjs` dropped any date from provider results and returned them in provider/relevance order, so a high-Admiralty older source outranked a newer one and the model — handed date-blind, authority-ranked results — surfaced stale information. The tool now normalizes a `date` from whatever field the provider supplies, flags `stale` against the documented 12-month window, and reorders results most-recent-first (fresh → undated → stale, newest first within a tier). Admiralty-derived `confidence` is left intact per ADR-0017 — recency rides alongside as separate `date`/`stale` signals rather than being folded into the grade. Covered by `tests/functional/web-search.functional.test.mjs` (date normalization, staleness flag, most-recent-first ordering over an older A1). Note: the internal RAG/knowledge ranking (BM25/cosine/RRF/MMR) remains relevance-only with no recency decay — a separate follow-up, scoped narrowly to avoid degrading timeless-query relevance.

### Added

- Ship `construct` as a real GitHub Copilot / VS Code custom agent. VS Code reads custom agents from `.github/agents/*.agent.md` and grants tools by its own namespaced ids (`<server>/*`, `web/fetch`, `search/codebase`, …); the Claude-format tool names in `.claude/agents/construct.md` (`Read,Grep,…,orchestration_policy`) are not recognized there, so selecting construct in VS Code surfaced an agent with no usable tools — it would refuse internet/orchestration requests because none of its tools were actually granted. `syncCopilot` (`scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`) now emits `.github/agents/construct.agent.md` with VS-Code-namespaced grants (`construct-mcp/*`, `web/fetch`, `web/githubRepo`, `search/*`, `read/problems`, `edit/editFiles`) and the orchestration prompt as its body, so the picker entry actually scopes in the construct-mcp orchestration tools and live web access. Verified by `tests/functional/host-config-parity.functional.test.mjs`. To stop VS Code listing the orchestrator twice (it also scans `.claude/agents`), sync now writes a `chat.agentFilesLocations` pin into `.vscode/settings.json` (`{".github/agents": true, ".claude/agents": false}`) via a safe merge that never clobbers a JSONC/user settings file and respects an existing choice (`pinVscodeAgentLocations`, covered in `tests/vscode-mcp-toolkit-path.test.mjs`). Per VS Code docs the `.claude/agents` compatibility scan may not be fully suppressible by that setting in every version, so the guaranteed fallback for a residual duplicate is the one-time eye-icon hide in the agents dropdown. MCP servers defined in `.vscode/mcp.json` must also be started in the session — both remain VS Code runtime behaviors to confirm in-editor.

### Fixed

- Corrected the generated GitHub Copilot front-door framing. `.github/copilot-instructions.md` described `construct.prompt.md` as a "reusable role profile for single-pass work" and pointed "real" multi-specialist runs at a separately-invoked `orchestration_run` MCP tool — framing `construct` as a single-pass profile *beside* the orchestrator when selecting the `construct` mode is meant to *be* the orchestrator. The block now states that selecting `construct` from the chat-mode dropdown enters the orchestrator, which classifies the request and dispatches specialists through the `construct-mcp` tools (`orchestration_policy` then `orchestration_run`), names the `.vscode/mcp.json` server dependency, and notes it routes rather than guesses when those tools are unavailable. Emitted from `syncCopilot` in `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`.

- Healed stale construct MCP server paths in generated `.vscode/mcp.json` so VS Code / GitHub Copilot can actually start `construct-mcp`. The merge in `syncVSCode` (`scripts/sync-specialists.mjs`) preserves existing server entries so user customizations survive a re-sync, but it also preserved a construct-owned server path left behind by a moved or deleted toolkit checkout (a fully-resolved, non-placeholder path) — so VS Code kept launching `node <old-checkout>/lib/mcp/server.mjs`, the server never started, and the Copilot orchestrator had zero construct tools and refused every request. `mcpEntryPointsOutsideToolkit` now flags a construct `lib/mcp/*.mjs` path outside the current toolkit root as stale so the next sync refreshes it to the live path, while genuine user-owned servers (no toolkit path) are still preserved. Covered by `tests/vscode-mcp-toolkit-path.test.mjs`.

- Taught the orchestration policy to route live web/network requests to `cx-researcher` instead of refusing them. Driving the runtime revealed the dispatch-wiring fix (below) was necessary but not sufficient: a bare "connect to the internet" or "fetch example.com" classified as the `immediate` track with no specialist, so the read-only orchestrator (which holds no network tools) still deflected. `requiresLiveWebAccess` (`lib/orchestration-policy.mjs`) now detects explicit web access — a literal URL, an online/internet/connectivity phrase, a `fetch`/`download`/`scrape`/`curl` verb paired with a web object or domain, an "is … online/reachable" probe — and feeds both `requiresExternalResearch` (reason `web-access`, which injects `cx-researcher`) and `determineExecutionTrack` (promotes off `immediate` to `focused` so it actually dispatches). A precision floor keeps local work local: "fetch the user record from the database", "open the config file", "explain how the retrieval path works" do not trigger. The shared orchestration micro-prompt also gained a fallback directive: when a request needs a capability the orchestrator lacks, route it via `orchestration_run` or ask one clarifying question rather than telling the user to run it themselves. Covered by `tests/research-gate.test.mjs`.

- Restored the orchestrator's cross-host dispatch capability. The `construct` persona is a read-only router whose contract is classify-then-dispatch (`orchestration_policy` → `orchestration_run`), but on the Claude Code host it could do neither: two layers were broken. First, `orchestration_run` was never in `CORE_TOOL_NAMES` (`lib/mcp/server.mjs`), so the MCP server advertised it on no host — it was reachable only through the `call` gateway, yet the orchestrator prompt names it directly (invalid on Claude, OpenCode, and Codex alike). Second, Claude Code treats an agent's `tools:` frontmatter as a hard allowlist, and the generated list omitted both dispatch tools, so even the already-flat `orchestration_policy` was blocked. The visible symptom: asked to "connect to the internet," the orchestrator refused and told the user to run `curl` themselves instead of dispatching `cx-researcher` (which holds `WebSearch`/`WebFetch`). The fix promotes `orchestration_run` into the flat core (it is the indivisible second half of an already-core tool — the ADR-0048 "high-frequency action tool stays flat where evidence justifies it" carve-out) and adds a single `ORCHESTRATOR_DISPATCH_TOOLS` source in `scripts/sync-specialists.mjs` that grants both tools to the orchestrator on every host (named into Claude's allowlist; allow-by-default already holds on OpenCode; no per-agent gate on Codex). A new functional test (`tests/functional/orchestrator-dispatch-grant.functional.test.mjs`) asserts the parity across all three hosts plus the MCP surface, so the grant can't silently drift.

## [1.4.0] - 2026-06-30

### Added

- Real-process secret-resolution test coverage (`construct-trxz.9`). A functional test exercises the resolver against a real `op` subprocess (a fake binary on PATH) rather than an injected stub: it proves a repeated `op://` reference spawns `op` exactly once within a process (the in-process cache holds) and that a fresh process re-resolves from a cold cache — pinning the cross-process re-prompt behavior as observed reality. This complements the real-boundary tests added per epic (setup-credentials single-link, credential diagnostics no-leak, service op-run wiring, MCP add no-plaintext, cache-first poll). The true single-prompt-across-processes guarantee depends on 1Password's own session and remains tracked for real-`op` validation.

- Secret resolution now emits a durable, value-free audit trail (`construct-trxz.6`). `secret-resolver` previously recorded nothing, so there was no way to answer which `op://` references resolved, when, or whether they succeeded. It now emits structured events through an injectable, opt-in sink (`setSecretAuditSink`) carrying the variable, the source tier (`project-env`/`config-env`/`alt-store`/`op-catalog`/`shell-rc`/…), an `op://`-reference flag, cache-hit, and outcome — never the materialized value. `enableSecretAuditTrail` wires actual `op` reads (not cache hits or plain reads, to keep the resolve path off the file lock) into the append-only hash-chained audit log, and the CLI entrypoint enables it. Tests assert the events and the trail never contain the secret value. Worker and daemon process coverage is a tracked follow-up.

- Opened the secrets & credential-handling remediation track (`construct-trxz`). A source-verified plan (`docs/notes/research/2026-06-construct-audit/90-credential-handling-remediation-plan.md`) maps every secret resolve/store/forward/log/audit surface and decomposes the work into ten epics; ADR-0049 (`docs/decisions/adr/0049-cross-process-auth-once.md`) records the chosen direction for repeated 1Password prompts — resolve at a stable parent under `op run` and stay presence-first/cache-first in short-lived paths, rejecting a bespoke in-memory secret broker. Root cause confirmed from source: `wrapWithOpRun` (`lib/providers/op-run.mjs:49`) is imported but never called, so the only working path is per-process `op read` against an in-process cache.

- Added an end-to-end dogfood certification of the distribution gallery (`construct-cuxq.12.1`). `tests/functional/dogfood-certification.functional.test.mjs` drives every real example in `examples/distribution` (the same sources `npm run examples:distribution` ships) through the publish output-quality pass and asserts each certifies: it exports, validates the produced file, preserves its source content, and reports honest per-format a11y coverage. The test skips cleanly per item when a format's toolchain is absent.

- Added true pixel-level regression gated to full-certification (`construct-cuxq.10.2`). `lib/pixel-regression.mjs` decodes both PNGs to raw pixels with `node:zlib` (honoring zero-npm-core) and counts pixels whose channels differ beyond a tolerance — not a comparison of compressed file bytes, which is meaningless across encoders (two pixel-identical PNGs differ byte-for-byte). `pixelRegressionGate` runs only at the full-certification level and skips elsewhere as a pass; `regenerateGolden` is the single sanctioned writer of a golden. Nine tests cover the decoder, the same-pixels-different-compression case a byte diff gets wrong, tolerance, dimension/encoding mismatches, and the gate gating.

- Wired gate levels into CI and taught gates-audit to detect artifact-gate config drift (`construct-cuxq.11.2`). `lib/gates-audit.mjs` gains `auditArtifactGateConfig`, which validates the artifact manifest's quality-contract gate levels and required completion states against their enums — a typo or out-of-enum level that silently weakens a gate now surfaces as a critical drift gap (a repo with no manifest is skipped, not failed). A `certification gate` entry joins `GATE_DEFINITIONS` and a matching CI step runs `construct certify gate`, so the artifact release gate is tracked like every other gate. Two tests cover the real manifest and the no-manifest skip.

- Added `construct publish --preview` for a render-and-inspect check before claiming done (`construct-cuxq.11.3` / K-1). The flag forces the output-quality pass to render the export to images even when the artifact's gate level would not, then prints exactly what was verified — page validity, content preservation, the screenshot paths, diagram legibility warnings, and the per-format a11y coverage. A functional test covers the render-evidence capture and report.
